# Minimum feature set: classification of every catalog entry

> Generated from [`minimum-feature-set.json`](minimum-feature-set.json); do not edit by hand. Companion of [the minimum feature set](minimum-feature-set.md). Entry ids link to the [feature catalog](../mds-rust-map/features/README.md).

Tiers: **P0** first production release, **P1** design now and build next, **P2** optional, **OUT** never. Modes: **contract** (keep the external behaviour), **redesign** (keep the outcome, new mechanism), **subset**, **tolerate** (accept and ignore, or answer with a fixed value), **drop** (nothing to build).

## Platform: msgr2, cephx, MonClient, MgrClient, Objecter

### Platform: msgr2, cephx, MonClient, MgrClient, Objecter: P0 (31)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-108](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-108) | Process bootstrap (ceph-mds -i <id>) | subset | cephadm and Rook start `ceph-mds` from the Ceph image with fixed command lines (--id/-n, --fsid, --keyring, --mon-host, --setuser/--setgroup, --foreground, --default-* options, --public-addr) and expect ceph.conf plus the mon config DB to apply (F-ADM-113). | Accept that command line and those config sources, authenticate with cephx, wait for rotating keys, subscribe mdsmap and start beaconing in up:boot; `--hot-standby` and msgr1 protocol 36 are legacy.<br>Risk: Rook resolves `ceph-mds` through PATH and its liveness probe needs the `ceph` CLI, bash and jq in the same image. |
| [F-CLU-109](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-109) | Single public messenger with per-peer-type policies | contract | One msgr2 messenger whose address vector is what the mon records from beacons, what clients connect to and OSD fencing blocklists, and what the kernel checks in the server ident (address and nonce); client connections are lossless stateful-server. | Peer-MDS policies are free (Rust only). The msgr1 accept side serves CLIENT peers with protocol 32 (decision 3); protocol 36 between MDS daemons stays out.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-CLU-110](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-110) | MonClient session, keys and subscriptions | contract | The daemon needs a MonClient with cephx service keys for MON, OSD, MDS and MGR, subscriptions to monmap, config, mdsmap, mgrmap and osdmap, and the `profile mds` caps (beacons, blocklist commands, cluster log); Rook and cephadm set mds_join_fs and mds_cache_memory_limit only in the mon config DB. | Dropping MMonCommand from pre-Luminous monitors is legacy. |
| [F-CLU-115](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-115) | Rank message validation and routing | subset | The rank must accept exactly the client message types (session, reconnect, reclaim, request, caps, cap release, lease, metrics) from clients, MDSMap and OSDMap from mons, and MCommand (tell) from admin clients and mgr, dropping anything from an unexpected peer type (F-ADM-004). | MDS-to-MDS whitelist is free; MRemoveSnaps comes with P1 snapshots; MMonCommand from legacy mons is out. Never abort on an unexpected message (C++ asserts on a non-MMDSOp from an MDS). |
| [F-PROTO-001](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-001) | msgr1 (legacy protocol) server side for client connections | subset | Kernel clients mounted without ms_mode speak msgr1: ceph-csi under Rook's CRD default requireMsgr2=false, the old mon:/path device syntax and ms_mode=legacy. Supporting them is simpler than changing every deployment and test setup (decision 3). | Accept side only, for CLIENT peers, following the transport page 7.3 checklist: banner with the v1 address equal to the MDSMap entry (nonce included); connect with protocol 32; cephx authorizer with one challenge round; READY/SEQ with MSG_AUTH in the features; header, front, middle and data crcs; signed 21-byte footer; strict in_seq + 1; ACK; KEEPALIVE2_ACK; RESETSESSION for unknown sessions; never WAIT; front and middle at most 16 MiB, data at most 64 MiB. Outbound connections to mons, mgr, OSDs and peer MDS daemons stay msgr2. The beacon address vector lists v2 first and v1 second with the same nonce, so C++ clients keep dialling msgr2.<br>Risk: msgr1 has no encryption, only per-message signatures. The kernel treats WAIT, BADPROTOVER, FEATURES and unknown tags as connection errors and retries with backoff.<br>Question: Make requireMsgr2: true (the value in Rook's example cluster.yaml) a documented deployment requirement, or keep a msgr1 listener for ceph-csi kernel mounts?<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-002](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-002) | msgr2.1 crc mode for client connections | contract | Every current client (7.3 kernel with ms_mode=prefer-crc, libcephfs, ceph-fuse, Ganesha) reaches the MDS over msgr2.1 crc mode (transport 7.2, 7.4). | msgr2.1 framing only; the msgr2.0 (rev0) layout for Ceph 14.2.0-14.2.10 and 15.2.0-15.2.4 peers is out.<br>Risk: The kernel faults the connection on WAIT, a server KEEPALIVE2, SESSION_RESET(full=0), empty or aborted control frames, bad crcs (it ignores nocrc), a first v2 SERVER_IDENT address that differs from the MDSMap entry, or MSG_AUTH missing from the server features. |
| [F-PROTO-003](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-003) | msgr2.1 secure mode (AES-128-GCM) | contract | The MDS's own mon and mgr connections select secure mode by default (transport 3.1), and kernel ms_mode=secure or Rook network encryption select it towards the MDS, so AES-128-GCM msgr2.1 is needed anyway. | msgr2.1 64-bit nonce counters only; the msgr2.0 nonce format is out. |
| [F-PROTO-005](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-005) | Address vector publication and server identity checks | contract | Clients learn the MDS address only from the MDSMap and check SERVER_IDENT against it (kernel: first v2 address byte for byte incl. nonce; C++ clients: equality for remote-reset handling); the mon and OSD fencing use the same vector. | Publish the v2 entry first and a v1 (TYPE_LEGACY) entry second with the same nonce (decision 3). The msgr2 SERVER_IDENT and the msgr1 banner both present exactly the published address.<br>Risk: An MDSMap entry that has addresses but no TYPE_MSGR2 address makes the kernel shut the mount down; a restarted daemon must use a new nonce. See F-PROTO-001 for the Rook default.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-006](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-006) | Lossless stateful-server connection policy and replay of unacknowledged messages | contract | Client connections are lossless stateful-server: unacknowledged messages must be replayed with their original sequence numbers after SESSION_RECONNECT, which push_seq and cap-sequence correctness depend on. | msgr2 ack_seq/ACK frames and SESSION_RECONNECT(_OK); on kernel msgr1 connections also TAG_ACK and the SEQ exchange on reconnect (decision 3).<br>Risk: The kernel rejects any sequence gap; a C++ client that receives an already-seen sequence number drops it and, by code reading, stops reading the connection; the MDS never initiates TCP to a client.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-008](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-008) | MDS entity name and rank identity on the wire | contract | libcephfs takes the sending rank from the msgr2 SERVER_IDENT gid and drops messages whose (rank, connection) pair matches no session, so the messenger entity number must be the rank before the daemon is reachable as that rank (failover D9, rank changes D6). | The name number is sent once in the msgr2 SERVER_IDENT and in the src of every msgr1 message header (decision 3). Accept session, reconnect, reclaim, caps, cap-release and lease messages only from CLIENT peers.<br>Risk: A connection accepted while the messenger had another number keeps that number for its lifetime (transport 8 item 32).<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-009](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-009) | Keepalives and connection timeouts | subset | Clients send KEEPALIVE2 (the kernel at least every 5 s when it does not renew) and expect KEEPALIVE2_ACK echoing the timestamp. | Answer KEEPALIVE2 with KEEPALIVE2_ACK, as a msgr2 frame or as the msgr1 tag on kernel msgr1 connections (decision 3); never send KEEPALIVE2 to clients; a handshake timeout is recommended; the 900 s idle fault is optional.<br>Risk: A server-originated KEEPALIVE2 is a kernel protocol error.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-010](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-010) | Inbound flow control (messenger dispatch throttle) | redesign | No wire contract (clients only see TCP back-pressure), but an MDS serving thousands of sessions must bound buffered inbound bytes; the C++ messenger-wide 100 MiB budget couples every client and subvolume. | Risk: Stopping reads on a shared Ganesha session (F-PROTO-101) because one shard is slow stalls every other subvolume on that session (head-of-line blocking).<br>Question: Choose the bounding unit (connection, session, per-shard queue) so one slow or noisy shard cannot block other tenants that share a session. |
| [F-PROTO-011](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-011) | cephx authorizer verification for service mds | contract | Every client connection presents a cephx authorizer for service mds (auth_service_required default cephx); AES and AES256KRB5 keys both occur depending on cluster history. | msgr2 exchange (challenge in AUTH_REPLY_MORE, CephXAuthorizeReply with a 64-byte secret in secure mode, none in crc mode) and, on kernel msgr1 connections, CHALLENGE_AUTHORIZER/BADAUTHORIZER with exactly one challenge round (decision 3).<br>Risk: Ticket expiry is not checked, only that secret_id is among the three held rotating secrets; an empty authorizer must fail.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-012](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-012) | Rotating mds service secrets | contract | Without the current rotating mds secrets from the monitors no client authorizer can be verified (transport 7.5). | Risk: A MonMap auth_epoch increase wipes secrets and tickets; client authorizers fail until new secrets arrive. |
| [F-PROTO-013](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-013) | msgr1 message signatures | contract | The kernel requires MSG_AUTH on msgr1 unless mounted with nocephx_sign_messages, so every message on a kernel msgr1 connection carries a cephx signature in the 21-byte footer and incoming signatures are verified (decision 3). | Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-016](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-016) | Encoding primitives and versioned-struct rules | contract | Kernel and libcephfs decode with ENCODE_START rules: struct_compat must be 1 on InodeStat, DirStat, LeaseStat and quota_info_t, unknown trailing bytes are skipped by struct_len, and the raw heads are packed memcpy layouts. | Risk: The kernel parses MDSCapAuth/MDSCapMatch positionally without struct_len: never append fields to them (F-ADM-021). |
| [F-PROTO-018](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-018) | Decode failures and client-triggerable aborts | redesign | No peer depends on the C++ aborts, but one malformed message must not take down a rank that serves many tenants (robustness goal, multi-tenant isolation). | Risk: Decode fallibly: fault the connection or fail the request (-EINVAL/-EBADMSG) on a caps xattr_len/middle mismatch, trailing bytes, unknown lease actions, bad initial-xattr, fscrypt or flock blobs and owner -1; never abort. |
| [F-PROTO-019](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-019) | Golden encodings and encoding test data | redesign | No runtime contract, but clients fail or assert on any byte deviation, so the Rust codecs need golden-data conformance tests: ceph-dencoder round trips, ceph-object-corpus, the test_frames_v2 frame tables and the RFC 8009 crypto vectors. | Test-time only; nothing is registered with ceph-dencoder at run time. |
| [F-PROTO-103](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-103) | MonClient obligations | contract | The MDS needs the monitors for MonMap and config bootstrap, authentication (global id = FSMap gid), service tickets, MDSMap/OSDMap/MgrMap subscriptions, mon commands (blocklist) and the cluster log (transport 7.5). | msgr2 AUTH_MODE_MON only; msgr1 MAuth is out.<br>Risk: Re-authenticating without the previous ticket yields a new global id, which the FSMap treats as a different daemon. |
| [F-PROTO-104](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-104) | MgrClient obligations | contract | status, dashboard and prometheus (D13) read MDS perf counters and task status that the MDS sends in MMgrReport after MMgrOpen and MMgrConfigure. | Risk: Values are packed without keys in lexicographic order of the declared paths; any other order shifts every value on the mgr side. |
| [F-PROTO-105](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-105) | Objecter obligations (OSD client protocol) | contract | All metadata persistence goes to RADOS through an Objecter that must follow OSDMaps, place objects exactly like the C++ OSDMap/CRUSH code, resend correctly and honour epoch barriers and -EBLOCKLISTED. | MOSDOp v9 (SERVER_SQUID OSDs); older encodings are out.<br>Risk: librados is a separate identity (own address and nonce): OSD traffic that does not leave from the beaconed address is not fenced when the monitor blocklists a failed MDS.<br>Question: Rust OSD client: own implementation on the MDS messenger, or a librados variant that shares the MDS address vector (F-PROTO-106)? |
| [F-STORE-090](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-090) | Per-rank Objecter RADOS client | contract | The MDS is a RADOS client of the metadata and data pools, and cephadm re-applies `osd 'allow rw tag cephfs *=*'` (no x) on deploy, so no object classes or exec. | Plain RADOS ops only (omap, xattrs, compound ops, cmpxattr guards); follow OSDMaps; bound in-flight ops per shard (C++ never enforces its budget). |
| [F-STORE-091](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-091) | RADOS identity and request ids | contract | OSDs deduplicate ops by (entity, incarnation, tid), so a new instance must never be mistaken for the previous holder of the rank. | Risk: If the RADOS client reuses an entity name with tids from 0, set the incarnation (C++ uses the FSMap inc); OSD full exemptions also key on the mds entity type. |
| [F-STORE-092](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-092) | FULL_FORCE: MDS I/O ignores full flags | contract | Journal, checkpoint and purge writes must continue when pools are marked full so that deletes can free space. | PAUSERD/PAUSEWR and the OSD fail-safe ratio still stop writes; they surface as slow metadata IO.<br>Risk: Verify the chosen RADOS client sends as an mds entity with FULL_FORCE (librados normally sends as client.*). |
| [F-STORE-093](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-093) | Continuous OSDMap following | contract | Every OSDMap can change the metadata-pool full flag and the pool set, and can blocklist clients whose sessions every rank must then kill. |  |
| [F-STORE-101](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-101) | Pool requirements for CephFS pools | contract | The mon enforces the pool rules (replicated metadata pool, cephfs application tag, EC overwrite rules, no pool snapshots) and the MDS relies on them, using only MDSMap data_pools for layouts. | Nothing to build beyond validating layout pools and using the MDSMap metadata pool.<br>Question: Beacons must still advertise MDSMap incompat 1-12 (named after C++ formats) to get a rank; add a private incompat bit (`fs compat add_incompat`) to keep C++ daemons out (constraint 3)? |
| [F-ADM-001](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-001) | MDS self-authentication, keyring and rotating service secrets | contract | The MDS must authenticate as mds.<id> to mons, OSDs and mgr and verify client authorizers with the rotating mds secrets; cephadm puts the keyring in $mds_data, Rook passes --keyring. | Keyring default /var/lib/ceph/mds/ceph-<id>/keyring or --keyring; rotating-secret renewal; aes256k keys and rotating secrets (Rook v1.21 sets auth_preferred_cipher/auth_service_cipher aes256k on supporting releases).<br>Risk: Without aes256k support the MDS cannot authenticate on Rook clusters that prefer aes256k. |
| [F-ADM-002](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-002) | Peer authorizer verification and caps loading at connection accept | contract | Every peer connection carries a cephx ticket whose mds caps become the session's auth caps; a missing or unparseable mds cap must refuse the connection (msgr2 AUTH_BAD_METHOD, kclient returns -EACCES). | On kernel msgr1 connections a refused authorizer gets BADAUTHORIZER, and the cephx_require_signatures and cephx_service_require_version gates apply (decision 3).<br>Risk: Caps are a snapshot per connection; mgr/nfs relies on caps never changing on an established connection.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-ADM-004](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-004) | Peer-type message filtering and rank address check | redesign | Client-only messages must be accepted only from client peers and inter-rank messages only from authenticated MDS peers of the file system; this is part of the multi-tenant boundary, the C++ dispatch mechanism is internal. | Risk: C++ does not bind the HELLO peer type and source to the authenticated entity; copying that keeps a spoofing gap.<br>Question: Bind peer type and source to the authenticated cephx entity (stricter than C++, no current peer affected)? |
| [F-ADM-061](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-061) | Runtime config observer and per-subsystem fan-out | redesign | Runtime `ceph config set` and `tell config set` must reach the running MDS and apply runtime-updatable options; the C++ observer, tracked-key list and finisher are internal. | Risk: Do not reproduce the C++ ignored-runtime-change bugs (F-ADM-063). |
| [F-ADM-062](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-062) | Config delivery from the monitor config DB and reporting to the mgr | contract | Rook and cephadm write per-daemon options (mds_join_fs, mds_cache_memory_limit) to the mon config DB, delivered by MConfig; `ceph config show mds.<id>` is served from the config sent in MMgrOpen/MMgrReport. | Ignore unknown option names in MConfig; the pre-Pacific blacklist renames (mon side) are out. |

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-ADM-003](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-003) | Authentication method none (and Kerberos) means allow-all | subset | With auth_service_required=none every client is treated as `allow *`; cephadm and Rook always deploy cephx, so MVP clusters do not need it. | auth none => allow_all caps (including tell); Kerberos/gss not supported.<br>Risk: If auth-none clusters are in scope for the MVP, every client is refused without it. |

| [F-ADM-005](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-005) | Daemon key rotation commands (rotate-key, wipe-rotating-secrets) | contract | cephadm's rotate-key path has no caller at this commit and Rook rotates with mon `auth rotate` plus a pod restart, so no default flow sends rotate-key or wipe-rotating-secrets. | Risk: If cephadm activates daemon key rotation, a missing rotate-key forces its restart fallback. |
| [F-ADM-086](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-086) | In-memory log dump on extraordinary events | redesign | Debug aid that dumps the C++ in-memory log ring after evictions or missed beacons; a Rust tracing ring can offer the same, no peer depends on it. |  |

<a id="membership"></a>

## Cluster membership, rank lifecycle and failover

### Cluster membership, rank lifecycle and failover: P0 (65)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-001](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-001) | FSMap: monitor-owned map of all file systems | drop | Monitor-owned map; the C++ MDS never subscribes to `fsmap` and only shapes it through beacons, so there is nothing to implement; mgr, tools, cephfs-mirror and libcephfs keep reading it from the unmodified mon. | Risk: Its content (states, addrs, metadata, compat) is only as correct as the Rust beacons (F-CLU-021, F-CLU-025). |
| [F-CLU-002](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-002) | MDSMap: per-file-system map and its wire encoding | subset | Every Rust daemon must decode the MDSMap the mon sends to drive its own state and its view of peer ranks; clients get the map from the mon, never from the MDS. | Decode the current encoding only (MMDSMap v2, MDSMap struct_v 5 / ev 20, mds_info_t v10, unknown trailing fields skipped by length) and use in/up/failed/stopped/damaged, mds_info (gid, name, rank, inc, state, addrs, laggy_since), flags (REFUSE_CLIENT_SESSION, ALLOW_SNAPS, ALLOW_STANDBY_REPLAY), session_timeout/autoclose, max_xattr_size, required_client_features, pools, compat, fs_name, last_failure_osd_epoch; tolerate balancer and qdb fields. Per-connection re-encoding for old peers is legacy; forwarding maps to peer ranks is free (Rust-only MDS-to-MDS). |
| [F-CLU-003](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-003) | Epoch model: FSMap, MDSMap and standby epochs, null map | contract | Beacons must carry the right epoch (standby_epochs[gid] as a standby, the fs MDSMap epoch once attached) and maps must be applied in order, dropping non-newer ones; every beacon that reaches prepare_beacon bumps the MDSMap epoch pushed to all clients of the fs. | Risk: Changing health text or flapping states produce an MDSMap epoch per beacon to every mounted client (map churn). |
| [F-CLU-004](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-004) | Map subscriptions and the MDS view of mdsmap (null maps) | contract | The mon serves the MDS view of `mdsmap` only to an MDS-type session whose address vector equals the recorded mds_info addrs (the beacon source), and signals removal with a null map of epoch UINT32_MAX (beacon-and-monitor 3.17 items 1, 3, 9). | Risk: Applying the UINT32_MAX null-map epoch without restarting stalls every later beacon in PaxosService::dispatch; subscribing from another messenger identity yields no MDS view. |
| [F-CLU-005](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-005) | FSMapUser: file system name directory for clients | drop | Mon-to-client name directory used by kclient `mds_namespace=` and libcephfs `client_fs`; the MDS never uses it, so mounts by fs name keep working with nothing to implement. |  |
| [F-CLU-006](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-006) | Kernel client partial MDSMap decode | drop | Kernel-side decoding of the mon-encoded MDSMap; nothing to implement, but it fixes constraints: every mds_info needs a msgr2 address (F-CLU-109), entries with state <= 0 (creating, starting, standby-replay) are invisible, reconnect is sent at state >= 10 and new requests only at >= ACTIVE. | Kernels mounted without ms_mode select the v1 address, which the MDS publishes (decision 3).<br>Risk: Ranks must stay contiguous (array bounded by size(in)); the mon keeps them so, `mds rmfailed` breaks it.<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-CLU-007](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-007) | libcephfs map use and mount availability check | drop | libcephfs, ceph-fuse and Ganesha decode the mon's map themselves; the Rust MDS's obligations are indirect: keep beacons flowing (ceph-fuse refuses mounts while any up rank is laggy) and honour REFUSE_CLIENT_SESSION and session_autoclose semantics (sessions category). |  |
| [F-CLU-008](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-008) | FSMap filtering for file-system-restricted sessions | drop | Mon-side filtering of the FSMap for fs-restricted sessions (subscriptions, read-only commands); no MDS involvement. |  |
| [F-CLU-010](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-010) | Multiple file systems: enable_multiple and the default file system | drop | enable_multiple and the default fscid are mon state used by unqualified client subscriptions and role parsing; no MDS involvement. | Deprecated alias `fs set_default` is legacy. |
| [F-CLU-012](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-012) | max_file_size published in the MDSMap | tolerate | Clients enforce max_file_size from the mon's map (kclient, libcephfs); the C++ MDS only decodes it and performs no size check. | Decode and ignore; no MDS-side check. |
| [F-CLU-014](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-014) | CompatSet encoding and the MDS incompat feature ids | subset | Every beacon carries a CompatSet whose incompat mask the unmodified mon uses to freeze the daemon's compat and to decide fs eligibility; it is the only mon-enforced way to keep C++ and Rust daemons out of each other's file systems (constraint 3). | Encode CompatSet exactly (three u64 masks, bit 0 cleared on the wire, a name for every set bit, ids 1..63). Advertise one constant Rust set: incompat 7 (forced by the mon, F-CLU-016) plus a private Rust format bit N (13..63, ideally reserved upstream) and none of the C++ format bits 1-6, 8-12. C++ feature semantics (inline data, omap dirfrags, anchor table, ...) are out.<br>Risk: If the Rust set contains bit 1 (or all C++ default bits) a Rust standby is eligible for any upgradeable C++ fs (one rank, no standby-replay) and gets promoted into it, merging N; if the Rust fs compat lacks N, C++ standbys can take Rust ranks.<br>Question: Decide the compat strategy. Recommended: {7, N} without bit 1. Cost: `fs new` (mgr/volumes `fs volume create`, Rook CephFilesystem) copies default_compat (C++ bits), so no Rust daemon is eligible until the new fs compat is edited while no rank is up (`fs compat <fs> rm_incompat` 1-6, 8-12, add N), or default_compat is stripped once with the deprecated `mds compat rm_incompat` (pure-Rust clusters only). Alternatives: also advertise the C++ bits (zero-touch, unsafe in mixed clusters) or ask for a small mon/mgr change. |
| [F-CLU-015](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-015) | Standby eligibility by compat set | drop | Eligibility filters run in the unmodified mon (a standby must hold every incompat bit of the fs; extra bits only while the fs is upgradeable); with the compat of F-CLU-014 they keep C++ daemons out of Rust file systems (no N) and Rust daemons out of C++ ones (no bit 1). | Risk: Scoring and mds_join_fs (F-CLU-030, F-CLU-031) only rank candidates; only these filters prevent cross-assignment. |
| [F-CLU-016](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-016) | Forced INLINE incompat bit and compat bootstrap | contract | The mon inserts incompat 7 into every stored daemon compat at boot, so a Rust daemon whose beacons omit bit 7 is evicted (blocklisted if ranked) at its first beacon that needs a proposal. | Always send bit 7 with a name. The empty-compat bootstrap and pre-v10 decode defaults are old-daemons legacy. Advertising bit 7 does not mean inline data support (D12: inline data OUT).<br>Risk: The eviction can fire long after boot, e.g. when a promoted daemon first reports leaving up:replay. |
| [F-CLU-017](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-017) | Compat set must not change after boot | contract | The mon compares the compat masks of each state-changing beacon with the boot-time compat and fails the daemon on any difference (beacon-and-monitor 3.17 item 7). | Risk: A Rust upgrade that adds a format bit must restart the process (new gid), never change compat inside one identity. |
| [F-CLU-018](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-018) | Upgradeable file systems and compat merge on promotion | drop | Mon-side rule: a daemon with extra incompat bits may join only an upgradeable fs and then merges its bits permanently; with F-CLU-014 this is how N enters a fresh Rust fs and how later Rust format bits roll out through cephadm/Rook's max_mds=1, standby-replay-off upgrade choreography. | The C++ cross-release merge path is old-daemons legacy; for Rust it is the future format-upgrade path.<br>Question: Plan Rust on-disk format evolution as new private incompat bits merged this way (needs the fs upgradeable: one rank in, standby-replay off). |
| [F-CLU-019](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-019) | fs compat and mds compat commands | drop | Mon commands only; with the recommended compat strategy `fs compat <fs> rm_incompat/add_incompat` is the operator step that makes a new fs Rust-only while no rank is up. | Deprecated `mds compat show/rm_compat/rm_incompat` (default_compat) are legacy; at most a one-time default_compat strip in a pure-Rust cluster (F-CLU-014 question).<br>Risk: `fs compat add_*` with id >= 64 aborts the monitor; adding an incompat bit while a standby-replay daemon is attached trips FSMap::sanity. |
| [F-CLU-020](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-020) | mds_features in the boot beacon and get_up_features | tolerate | The boot beacon must carry a u64 mds_features; the mon stores and dumps it (`features` in fs dump) but nothing interprets it. | Send a constant mask (e.g. the msgr feature bits the Rust messenger supports). Choosing encodings by the AND over ranks (get_up_features) is C++ mixed-version legacy and out.<br>Question: Rust-to-Rust rolling upgrades with mixed versions across ranks need their own version gating (P1 design item). |
| [F-CLU-021](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-021) | MMDSBeacon message and the beacon sender thread | contract | The beacon is the MDS's only control channel to the unmodified mon (liveness, wanted state, last epoch, compat, health, affinity); it must be MMDSBeacon v8 with the full field order, sent about every 4 s from a context that metadata work cannot block. | Vestigial standby_for_* and standby_replay fields are sent as constants; v6/v7 decoding is mon-side legacy.<br>Risk: A health metric of type 0 (MDS_HEALTH_NULL) asserts in the mon's decoder and aborts the monitor. |
| [F-CLU-022](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-022) | Monitor beacon admission rules | contract | The Rust daemon must satisfy every admission rule (mon cap `mds x`, correct fsid, nonzero source port, strictly increasing seq, `version` equal to its effective epoch) or its state requests and health are silently ignored. | Risk: A `version` above the committed epoch stalls the beacon in PaxosService::dispatch; an old seq or a repeated up:boot is not counted as liveness and leads to removal. |
| [F-CLU-023](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-023) | Beacon acks and MDS-side lagginess (self-fencing deferral) | redesign | Partly inherent safety rule: a daemon with no beacon ack for mds_beacon_grace may already be replaced, so it must stop answering clients and peers (it could otherwise keep renewing caps and serving stale reads) until the mon answers; messages are delayed, not dropped. | Keep: defer all client/peer work while acks are missing, do not serve before the first ack, suppress session staleness right after. Process acks on a path independent of metadata work (the C++ false-laggy coupling to mds_lock disappears).<br>Risk: Blocklisting fences only writes; without self-fencing a partitioned old holder can still answer reads and renewals from cache. |
| [F-CLU-024](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-024) | Heartbeat-gated beacons (internal watchdog) | redesign | The mon fails over only daemons that stop beaconing, so a wedged-but-alive Rust daemon must stop beaconing; the liveness signal must reflect real progress of the metadata engine (workers, journal commits), not just the beacon thread. | Risk: Long recovery scans without progress signals cause false failovers that restart recovery (resolve-and-rejoin 3.20). |
| [F-CLU-025](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-025) | Boot registration as a standby with daemon metadata | contract | The up:boot beacon registers the daemon in the shared standby pool and its sys_info becomes `mds metadata`/`ceph versions`, which mgr (list_servers, prometheus), cephadm (upgrade version check, stray detection) and Rook (upgrade detection) parse; want_state stays up:boot until the map shows up:standby. | Risk: Rook compares each MDS `ceph_version` (`ceph version X.Y.Z`, build, commit id) with the image's `ceph --version`: a lower version or another commit id makes every reconcile rerun the MDS upgrade sequence (max_mds 1, pods scaled down), and an unparsable or missing string makes every reconcile fail.<br>Question: Which version string does the Rust MDS report? It must equal the bundled Ceph CLI's `ceph --version` exactly to satisfy Rook and cephadm. |
| [F-CLU-026](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-026) | Unique daemon names | contract | On boot the mon fails (and blocklists, if ranked) any existing daemon with the same name, so a restarted Rust daemon must reuse its configured name to fence its predecessor at once (Rook node loss, respawn) and refuse empty or digit-leading ids that mon commands parse as ranks or gids. |  |
| [F-CLU-027](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-027) | DNE beacon on clean shutdown | contract | cephadm and Rook stop MDS daemons with SIGTERM and their waits only watch the FSMap; the DNE beacon (sent, ack awaited about 1 s) makes the mon fail the rank immediately instead of after mds_beacon_grace (15-20 s outage per restart). | Risk: Each clean exit of a rank holder blocklists its addresses for a day (harmless: new nonce per process). |
| [F-CLU-029](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-029) | Monitor-side laggy detection and its protections | drop | Laggy marking and replacement are done by the unmodified mon from beacon timestamps; the Rust part is F-CLU-021/024 (beacons within the mon's mds_beacon_grace from every state, including long replay and stopping). | Risk: A laggy holder without an eligible standby is never replaced and ceph-fuse refuses new mounts meanwhile; the mon uses its own mds_beacon_grace, so read the option from the same config. |
| [F-CLU-030](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-030) | Standby selection scoring | drop | Standby scoring runs in the mon; the Rust daemon only supplies its join_fs (F-CLU-031) and addresses. | Risk: Ranking is not prevention: a lower-scored eligible standby is still used, so only compat (F-CLU-014, F-CLU-015) separates C++ and Rust daemons. |
| [F-CLU-031](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-031) | mds_join_fs file system affinity | contract | cephadm sets mds_join_fs=<service_id> and Rook sets mds_join_fs=<fs> for every MDS, and the mon uses the beacon `fs` field for scoring and affinity swaps; the daemon must read the option (mon config DB) on every beacon. |  |
| [F-CLU-032](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-032) | Per-file-system selection flags: refuse_standby_for_another_fs and host anti-affinity | drop | refuse_standby_for_another_fs and host anti-affinity (default on) are mon-side selection policies; nothing to implement in the MDS. | Question: Recommend setting refuse_standby_for_another_fs on every fs of a mixed C++/Rust cluster as defence in depth next to compat (cephadm and Rook always set mds_join_fs). |
| [F-CLU-033](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-033) | Affinity-driven swaps of healthy daemons | drop | The mon may fail over a healthy active or standby-replay daemon whose affinity is worse than an available standby's; the Rust MDS only handles an ordinary failover (F-CLU-035, F-CLU-036, F-CLU-080). | Risk: Causes failovers of healthy actives in default cephadm/Rook deployments, so cold takeover must be routine and fast. |
| [F-CLU-034](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-034) | Failed-rank and laggy-holder replacement | drop | Failed-rank promotion and laggy-holder replacement are mon policy; the Rust part is entering up:replay (F-CLU-035, F-CLU-037, F-CLU-066). | Risk: With defaults, detection to promotion takes 15-20 s plus a Paxos commit, and only an available standby enables replacement. |
| [F-CLU-035](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-035) | Promotion into a rank and the initial rank state | contract | The mon alone assigns ranks and the entry state and the daemon cannot refuse: the Rust MDS must implement up:creating, up:starting, up:replay (also from standby-replay), up:standby-replay (tolerated, F-CLU-039) and up:stopping (beacon-and-monitor 3.17 item 8). | Risk: The promotion epoch becomes the rank's inc, which must be adopted (F-CLU-038). |
| [F-CLU-036](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-036) | Fencing failed rank holders with OSD blocklists | contract | Fencing a failed rank holder is the mon's OSD blocklist of every address in the beaconed address vector (exact nonce); D9 makes fencing P0 and the Rust daemon must treat -EBLOCKLISTED as loss of identity (stop serving, restart with a new nonce). | Risk: Every RADOS operation must leave through the messenger whose addresses are beaconed; separate librados/Objecter instances with other nonces (tempting for per-core parallelism) are not fenced and break single-writer safety.<br>Question: Confirm the RADOS client design keeps one fenced identity (optionally adding per-shard ownership epochs checked OSD-side). |
| [F-CLU-037](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-037) | Wait for last_failure_osd_epoch before touching rank objects | contract | A daemon taking over a failed rank must not read or write any rank or shard object before its OSDMap epoch reaches last_failure_osd_epoch, so that the old holder is already fenced at the OSDs (F-STORE-042). | Risk: The unmodified mon can overwrite last_failure_osd_epoch with 0 (standby-replay removal, `fs fail` in gid order), and the C++ MDS does not wait in up:creating/up:starting (README observation 12).<br>Question: Defence in depth: wait for the latest OSDMap in every entry state and guard journal/shard writes with an ownership epoch checked on the OSD side? |
| [F-CLU-038](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-038) | Rank incarnation | contract | inc distinguishes successive holders of a rank: it must be the OSD client incarnation so OSD duplicate detection never confuses two holders' requests; libcephfs treats an inc change as a restart and peer ranks use it to detect restarted ranks. |  |
| [F-CLU-039](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-039) | Standby-replay assignment | tolerate | D9 defers standby-replay to P1, but Rook runs `fs set <fs> allow_standby_replay true` for every CephFilesystem with activeStandby (the mgr rook module creates them with activeStandby=True), and the mon then attaches standbys as followers that cannot refuse. | Accept up:standby-replay: beacon that state and request nothing else, keep no warm state if desired (cold follower), and on promotion run a normal cold replay (F-CLU-069). Warm following is F-CLU-068 (P1).<br>Risk: Never report down:damaged from a follower: the mon then blocklists the healthy active and marks the rank damaged, and if the followed rank is not up it terminates the leader monitor.<br>Question: D9 vs the Rook default: confirm that P0 tolerates the assignment (a follower still counts for ok-to-stop and Rook's liveness probe). |
| [F-CLU-042](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-042) | fs new | drop | `fs new` runs unmodified from mgr/volumes, cephadm and Rook and promotes standbys to up:creating for ranks 0..max_mds-1; the Rust part is up:creating (F-CLU-064). | `--recover` (rank 0 replays existing metadata) only matters for disaster recovery.<br>Question: With the recommended compat (F-CLU-014) the new fs gets default_compat and no Rust daemon is eligible until its compat is edited: choose an operator/automation step, a one-time default_compat strip, or an upstream `fs new` change. |
| [F-CLU-043](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-043) | fs rm | drop | Mon command used by mgr/volumes and Rook to delete a fs once no rank is up; no MDS involvement. |  |
| [F-CLU-044](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-044) | fs fail and the joinable flag | drop | `fs fail` and joinable are mon state changes used by cephadm (fail_fs upgrades) and Rook; Rust daemons only see removal (F-CLU-057) or fencing (F-CLU-036). | Deprecated `cluster_down` alias is legacy.<br>Risk: `fs fail` erases daemons in gid order, so a follower can reset last_failure_osd_epoch to 0 (F-CLU-037). |
| [F-CLU-046](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-046) | fs set down | drop | `fs set down` is mon state (max_mds 0, later restored); the Rust parts are stopping (F-CLU-102, where the last rank evicts and blocklists all clients) and up:starting on `down false` (F-CLU-065). |  |
| [F-CLU-048](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-048) | mds fail | drop | `mds fail` is used by operators, cephadm and Rook (standby-replay cleanup); the mon blocklists and replaces a holder, and the Rust daemon just leaves (F-CLU-057, F-CLU-105). |  |
| [F-CLU-049](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-049) | Damaged ranks and mds repaired | drop | The mon handles a down:damaged report (blocklist the holder, rank to damaged, MDS_DAMAGE) and `mds repaired`; the Rust part is deciding when to report it (F-CLU-104). | Risk: A damaged rank is out of service until an operator acts; D10 prefers per-shard containment. |
| [F-CLU-053](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-053) | Daemon and rank states: numeric values and names | contract | Beacons, the mon, both clients and every tool use the numeric state values and their `up:*`/`down:*` names; the kernel ignores entries with state <= 0. | Value -9 (replayonce) is dead code. |
| [F-CLU-054](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-054) | State transition validation on both sides | contract | The mon evicts (blocklisting rank holders) a daemon that requests an invalid transition, so the Rust MDS requests only legal successors and validates states it is assigned; REPLAY->RECONNECT is legal for any rank count, REJOIN cannot be skipped, CLIENTREPLAY can. | Risk: A standby must never request a state and a follower only its own state (DAMAGED is legal but dangerous, F-CLU-039). |
| [F-CLU-055](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-055) | Requesting the next state through beacons | contract | State changes are requested by repeated beacons and become real only when the map shows them: epoch and wanted state must be updated atomically, and messages arriving before the daemon sees its new state (early reconnects, retried requests, cap flushes) must be parked by comparing with the wanted state. | Risk: A beacon with the new epoch but the pre-assignment state is an invalid transition (eviction with blocklist); each step costs a beacon round trip plus a Paxos commit (3-4 epochs per single-rank failover). |
| [F-CLU-056](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-056) | Rank sets and derived predicates | subset | A Rust rank needs the rank sets to route to peers (shard owners, LOOKUPINO forwarding), detect failures and know when it is the last rank in; the mon and libcephfs compute their own predicates. | Decode in/up/failed/stopped/damaged and use up/active for routing and failure detection; the C++ is_resolving/is_rejoining/recovery-set predicates go with F-CLU-070. |
| [F-CLU-057](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-057) | Daemon-level map handling | contract | beacon-and-monitor 3.17 items 3 and 9: apply maps in epoch order, leave and return with a new identity when removed (or on the UINT32_MAX null map), keep up:boot until the standby map, start the MgrClient when first in a map, never change rank inside one identity. | Like the C++ self-check, exit when the map's fs compat is not writeable by the Rust compat (defence in depth for F-CLU-014).<br>Risk: A daemon that keeps serving after the map removed it undermines fencing until its writes fail. |
| [F-CLU-058](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-058) | Rank-level map handling and peer change detection | redesign | A per-rank driver must start each phase when the map (not the request) shows the state and detect peer failures or restarts by incarnation; several of its side effects are client-visible. | Keep: messenger entity name mds.<rank> while holding a rank (libcephfs takes the rank from the message source), inc handed to the RADOS client, OSD epoch barrier refresh while active, eviction of sessions lacking required_client_features. Drop: C++ resolve/rejoin triggers and journal fadvise hints.<br>Risk: With msgr2 the kernel checks that the server ident address and nonce equal the map entry. |
| [F-CLU-065](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-065) | Restarting a stopped rank (up:starting) | redesign | When the fs regrows into a previously stopped rank (`fs set down false`, max_mds raised after a shrink) the mon assigns up:starting; the Rust daemon must reopen the cleanly stopped rank state without replay and request up:active. | Risk: A dead up:starting holder returns the rank to stopped and its successor does not wait for fencing (rank-lifecycle gotchas 19, 26). |
| [F-CLU-067](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-067) | End of replay: journal reformat, writeable journal and the single-rank shortcut | subset | At the end of replay the rank makes its journals writeable and requests the next state; the mon accepts up:replay -> up:reconnect for any rank count. | Request up:reconnect directly (or up:resolve if kept, F-CLU-073). Journal reformat and snap-table format upgrade are old-data legacy. |
| [F-CLU-069](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-069) | Standby-replay takeover | subset | When a followed rank fails the mon promotes its follower in place to up:replay (preferred replacement), so the Rust daemon must handle STANDBY_REPLAY -> REPLAY even without warm state. | P0: wait for last_failure_osd_epoch, then a full cold replay; warm takeover from the follower's cache is P1 with F-CLU-068. |
| [F-CLU-094](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-094) | Rejoin on a single-rank file system | redesign | up:rejoin cannot be skipped (the mon only allows RECONNECT -> REJOIN), so every recovering rank passes through it; it is where the rank attaches reconnected caps, seeds lock state, identifies files needing size recovery and reconciles realms before serving. | Per-shard parallel work instead of full-cache scans; no messages to other ranks. |
| [F-CLU-104](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-104) | Reporting a rank as DAMAGED | subset | When rank-level state cannot be loaded or replayed the daemon must report down:damaged (beacon, wait for the ack) so no daemon is reassigned until `mds repaired`; D10 wants shard-level damage contained instead. | Report DAMAGED only for rank-wide failures (unreadable rank-level state, broken fencing invariant); a damaged shard returns EIO or goes offline while the rank keeps serving.<br>Risk: Wait for the ack without blocking the ack path (C++ can miss it while holding mds_lock).<br>Question: Which failures still take the whole rank down:damaged in the Rust design? |
| [F-CLU-105](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-105) | Respawn: re-exec as a fresh standby | redesign | Whenever the daemon may no longer act for its rank (removed from the map, rank change, invalid transition, -EBLOCKLISTED) it must drop all state and return as a new standby with a new gid and nonce (beacon-and-monitor 3.17 item 9). | Mechanism is free: exec, in-process re-init with a fresh messenger and MonClient, or exit (Rook and systemd restart with backoff, so frequent exits are costly). |
| [F-CLU-106](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-106) | Suicide and orderly shutdown | subset | SIGTERM/SIGINT from cephadm, Rook and systemd must end in a DNE beacon and a clean exit; fatal boot errors (auth, rotating keys) and an unwritable map compat must exit instead of serving. | Asok `exit` and the shutdown order are free; avoid the C++ shutdown deadlocks. |
| [F-PROTO-062](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-062) | Client reactions to MDSMap rank changes | contract | Clients decide when to talk to a rank from MDSMap states that the MDS drives through beacons: requests only to up:active (the kernel ignores states <= 0), renewals from up:reconnect, reconnect at up:reconnect (exactly, for libcephfs), metrics only to active ranks. |  |
| [F-PROTO-106](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-106) | Fencing identity: one address vector for clients, monitors and OSDs | contract | The monitor serves the MDS's mdsmap subscription by matching its address vector against the FSMap and fences a failed MDS by blocklisting that vector at the OSDs, so one addrvec must be beaconed, used for the mon session, presented to OSDs and listened on for clients. | msgr2 CLIENT_IDENT only; the msgr1 banner identity is out.<br>Risk: A mismatch leaves the MDS without its MDSMap, or unfenced after a failover (split brain). |
| [F-CACHE-070](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-070) | mkfs base hierarchy creation | redesign | Cache view of F-CLU-064: up:creating must create the root shard with the root inode and an empty root directory. | No ~mdsN/stray hierarchy; per-shard internal areas instead. |
| [F-CACHE-073](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-073) | Boot-time opening of the base hierarchy and the open gate | redesign | A rank must load its shards' base state before serving and hold requests until then; failures must be contained (D10). | Open each shard independently, recreate missing internal structures, and let a damaged shard fail alone rather than taking the rank down:damaged; the legacy 'stray' name is out. |
| [F-STORE-021](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-021) | Journal capping when a rank stops | redesign | Rank shrink through max_mds is P0 (D6): a stopping rank must hand off its shards and leave nothing to replay before beaconing down:stopped. | Hand off shards, close sessions, checkpoint and seal per-rank state so that up:starting needs no replay. |
| [F-STORE-042](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-042) | Single-writer fencing before reading rank state | contract | D9: before touching a failed rank's state the replacement waits for an OSDMap at least as new as last_failure_osd_epoch, so OSDs reject the old holder (single writer). | Risk: C++ gaps: FSMap::erase can zero the epoch, up:starting/creating never wait, wait_for_map does not re-check; consider waiting for the latest OSDMap at every takeover. |
| [F-STORE-044](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-044) | Journal creation and append-only open per rank state | redesign | The mon decides up:creating, up:starting or up:replay, and the MDS must create fresh state, reopen a cleanly stopped rank without replay, or replay. | Rank state in the new format; shard WALs belong to shards, not ranks; the legacy reformat step is out. |
| [F-STORE-055](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-055) | Object creation for a new rank (mkfs) | redesign | Storage view of F-CLU-064: `fs new` (on an empty metadata pool) followed by up:creating must write the initial state. | Root shard store, file-system-wide tables (inode-number allocator, shard map) and per-rank state; no C++ tables. |
| [F-STORE-056](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-056) | Boot-time object loading | redesign | Storage view of F-CLU-063: a booting rank loads its state, and errors must map to damage or respawn per D10. | Unreadable state damages the affected shard and -EBLOCKLISTED respawns; the C++ object list and the QA DAMAGED_ON_START expectations are out. |
| [F-STORE-095](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-095) | I/O completion path and self-respawn when fenced | redesign | A blocklisted instance must stop acting for its rank and come back as a fresh daemon with a new address, which the monitor's fencing assumes (D9). | Keep: -EBLOCKLISTED on any RADOS op abandons the rank and restarts with a new messenger nonce; drop the finisher/mds_lock completion model (D16). |
| [F-QOS-033](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-033) | MDS compat incompat feature 12 (quiesce subvolumes) | subset | The unmodified mon puts incompat 12 in the default compat of every new file system, and a daemon lacking any incompat bit of the fs compat is never assigned a rank, so bit 12 must be handled in the compat strategy (F-CLU-014, beacon-and-monitor 3.17). | Follow F-CLU-014: under its recommended {7, N} set, do not advertise bit 12 and strip it from the fs compat with bits 1-6 and 8-12; only a zero-touch strategy that advertises the C++ default bits advertises 12, with no quiesce behaviour behind it.<br>Risk: Mishandling it means no rank is ever assigned, or Rust standbys become eligible for C++ file systems; the compat set must also stay constant for the process lifetime (mon eviction). |

### Cluster membership, rank lifecycle and failover: P1 (3)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-068](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-068) | Standby-replay follower loop | redesign | Warm standby-replay (tailing the active's journals for fast takeover) is P1 by D9; with per-shard WALs it means tailing many journal streams. | P0 only tolerates the assignment (F-CLU-039). |
| [F-CACHE-069](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-069) | Standby-replay cache maintenance | redesign | Standby-replay is P1 (D9); a follower tailing shard WALs must keep its cache bounded. |  |
| [F-STORE-043](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-043) | Standby-replay journal tailing (journal side) | redesign | Standby-replay is P1 (D9); per-shard WALs must be tailable by a follower without racing trimming. | Legacy back-journal and reformat steps are out. |

### Cluster membership, rank lifecycle and failover: P2 (3)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-045](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-045) | fs reset | redesign | Disaster-recovery command, normally used after C++ offline tools (out by D2): it resets the fs compat to default_compat and makes rank 0 replay with every other rank stopped. | If supported: define rank-0 adoption of all shards and re-apply the Rust compat.<br>Risk: After `fs reset` no Rust daemon is eligible until the compat is edited again (F-CLU-014). |
| [F-CLU-047](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-047) | fs rename and fs swap | drop | Rename and swap of offline file systems are mon state changes made while no Rust daemon is attached. | Risk: Never persist fs_name or fscid in the Rust on-disk format; take them from the MDSMap (auth caps fsname=, metrics labels). |
| [F-CLU-050](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-050) | Hidden and debug rank commands: mds rm, rmfailed, freeze, set_state | drop | Hidden debug and repair commands (mds rm, rmfailed, freeze, set_state) edit mon records without MDS involvement. | Risk: `mds set_state` can push an invalid state: the Rust daemon must validate assigned states and restart rather than misbehave (F-CLU-054). |

### Cluster membership, rank lifecycle and failover: OUT (1)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-009](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-009) | FSMap sanity invariants enforced by the monitor | Monitor-internal assertions on committed FSMaps; valid beacons cannot violate them and there is nothing for the Rust MDS to implement. |

<a id="sessions"></a>

## Client sessions

### Client sessions: P0 (38)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-080](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-080) | up:reconnect: the client reconnect window and its timeout | contract | Clients act when the map shows the rank in up:reconnect and reconnect sessions, caps, realms and file locks; the rank waits for missing sessions at least mds_reconnect_timeout (45 s, extended while reconnect activity continues, ended early when all reconnected), then evicts them with blocklisting, including `timeout`-key sessions (decision 6). | Kill sessions whose address is already blocklisted at window start; deny late reconnects with CLOSE, unknown sessions with REJECT (F-PROTO-057).<br>Risk: libcephfs drops its session and caps if it never observes up:reconnect, a late kclient reconnect gets CLOSE (-EIO on dirty data); the window is per rank by protocol, so one silent client delays every tenant of the rank.<br>Reconciled: Decision 6 (2026-10-11): no recovery hold. A session that misses reconnect is evicted whether or not it carries `timeout`; every shard goes active at once. |
| [F-CLU-089](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-089) | Forced session open during rejoin | redesign | Whenever caps are attached on a rank where the client has no open session (F-CLU-088, migration), the rank must open and persist a session and send CEPH_SESSION_OPEN (F-PROTO-041); reconnected sessions are persisted before serving. | Legacy v1 SESSION_OPEN for pre-Mimic clients is out. |
| [F-PROTO-007](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-007) | Peer-reset detection and session-reset signalling | contract | Clients detect lost server state through SESSION_RESET(full=1) and react (kernel reconnects in up:reconnect, else tears the session down; libcephfs marks it stale); open sessions must survive connection loss, which Ganesha's abort_conn plus reclaim relies on. | msgr2 client-cookie detection and SESSION_RESET(full=1); on kernel msgr1 connections connect_seq detection and the RESETSESSION reply (decision 3).<br>Risk: SESSION_RESET(full=0) is a kernel protocol error; a kernel session torn down while the rank is active silently loses all its caps (dirty state -EIO).<br>Reconciled: Decision 3 (2026-10-11): a basic msgr1 listener for kernel clients is P0, so kernels mounted without ms_mode (Rook's default requireMsgr2=false for ceph-csi, the old device syntax, ms_mode=legacy) work unchanged. |
| [F-PROTO-015](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-015) | Connection-to-session binding (identity is the cephx global id) | redesign | Clients keep one session per rank keyed by their cephx global id (a remount is a new session), the session spans every shard on that rank (D4), and messages for a session whose client has not connected yet (force-opened by a shard import, D6) must be queued until it connects. | Risk: C++ flushes only the bootstrap queue when it re-points a connection to an imported session (sessions 8); the new design must not lose an import's OPEN or cap messages. |
| [F-PROTO-020](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-020) | CephFS feature bitmap exchange at session open | subset | Clients pick decoders and request formats from the MDS bitmap in every OPEN (the kernel keeps the first 64 bits), and the MDS keeps the client bitmap for its own per-bit behaviour. | Advertise bits 0-14 and 16-21 (not 15, 22, 23, 24 while fscrypt, charmap/subvolume metrics, blockdiff and quarantine are deferred; see the summary table), always send OPEN as v7 with supported_features and metric_spec, same bitmap on every rank. OPEN v1 for sessions without bit 8 is out.<br>Risk: Both clients overwrite their copy of the MDS bits on every OPEN from any rank and the kernel's subvolume-metrics switch is mount-wide, so ranks must never advertise different sets. |
| [F-PROTO-021](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-021) | Per-bit gated behaviour and feature names | subset | Current clients set bits 8, 9, 11, 13, 16 and 21 (libcephfs also 19), so only the current-client side of each MDS branch is needed; clients test MDS bits 9, 10, 12-15, 17-20, 22 and 23. | MDS behaviour: v7 session messages with error_string (8), versioned reply encodings (9), notable-caps-only re-issue on stale resume (11), openc_response_t (13), duplicate open answered with OPEN (16), snap-trace format by client bit 19, no charmap gate on bit 22, quarantine (24) P2. Out: branches for sessions lacking 8, 9, 11, 13, 16 or 21, and the broken-root_squash flag.<br>Question: Enforce a built-in client floor (REJECT a REQUEST_OPEN that lacks bits 8, 9, 13, 18, 20, 21) instead of MDSMap required_client_features, which v6.0+ kernels mis-skip (F-PROTO-023)? |
| [F-PROTO-024](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-024) | Connection-level feature bits consulted by the client protocol | subset | The 7.3 kernel picks the request head from the MDS connection's FS_BTIME (59) and the reconnect format from MDSENC (29), sends FLUSH_MDLOG only with SERVER_LUMINOUS (21), as libcephfs does, and requires MSG_AUTH in the server features. | Advertise the full CEPH_FEATURES_ALL of the baseline (MSG_AUTH, SERVER_LUMINOUS, MDSENC, EXPORT_PEER, FLOCK, REPLY_CREATE_INODE, MDS_QUOTA, FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2, FS_BTIME/MSG_ADDR2 ...). Assume FLOCK, EXPORT_PEER, MDS_QUOTA, FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2 and FS_BTIME on client connections (no fallback encoders); honour args.open.pool only from CREATEPOOLID connections (libcephfs); never create inline inodes (D12).<br>Risk: A messenger feature set missing FS_BTIME, MDSENC or SERVER_LUMINOUS silently moves the kernel to the legacy request head, reconnect v2 or no FLUSH_MDLOG. |
| [F-PROTO-025](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-025) | Feature bit 22 numbering conflict (Ceph CHARMAP vs kernel SUBVOLUME_METRICS) | subset | D15 requires an explicit decision: the 7.3 kernel reads MDS bit 22 as SUBVOLUME_METRICS (enables 1 Hz type-16 metric items) and sets its own bit 22 with that meaning, while libcephfs sets bit 22 meaning CHARMAP and ignores the MDS's bit 22. | P0: do not advertise bit 22 (charmap P2, subvolume metrics P1) and never read a client's bit 22 as charmap capability. With P1 subvolume metrics: advertise it on every rank, fill InodeStat v9 subvolume_id and decode the kernel's type-16 items. A future charmap must be gated on another signal.<br>Risk: Advertising it without a type-16 decoder or inconsistently across ranks toggles kernel metrics per OPEN; reading client bit 22 as charmap capability lets 7.1+ kernels create unfolded names in case-insensitive directories (kclient 7.1.4).<br>Question: Confirm: bit 22 off in P0, on with P1 subvolume metrics; how will a future charmap identify capable clients? |
| [F-PROTO-026](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-026) | Client generations and candidate minimum client floors | subset | The baseline is one kernel (7.3) and libcephfs at main; their current formats are session open v7, request v6 with head v3, single-message reconnect v3 (v5 parts above ~2 GiB), the old snap-trace format for the kernel, and no kernel bits 10, 19, 23, 24. | Serve these formats; older generations, the CentOS Stream rows and candidate floors F1-F7 are out (legacy).<br>Risk: CentOS Stream 9 kernels (5.14.0-757) match the 7.3 request formats but open sessions with MClientSession v4, which a v1/v7-only decoder refuses.<br>Question: Decode MClientSession by version (v4 costs nothing) to admit RHEL/CentOS 9 kernels? |
| [F-PROTO-027](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-027) | Session state machine | contract | Clients see the session lifecycle through OPEN, CLOSE, STALE, RENEWCAPS and REJECT, and session ls shows the state; open follows a durable record, close follows REQUEST_CLOSE with a matching seq, kill follows eviction. | State names and transitions as in C++; state_seq and projection mechanics are internal.<br>Risk: libcephfs keeps sending requests on a STALE session. |
| [F-PROTO-028](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-028) | Session open handshake (REQUEST_OPEN, journaled open, OPEN) | contract | Both clients open a session per rank with REQUEST_OPEN v7 and queue requests until OPEN; the MDS must persist the session (metadata, auth_name) before replying OPEN v7 with supported_features, metric_spec and cap_auths (D7, D9). | Persistence in the new session store (D2), not ESession/SessionMap; REQUEST_OPEN v2-v5 and OPEN v1 are out (see F-PROTO-026 for decoding by version). |
| [F-PROTO-029](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-029) | Session open admission checks and rejection reasons | subset | Security and Ganesha correctness depend on the open checks: fs-name caps, blocklisted address, mount root inside the cap path, duplicate uuid (reclaim) and refuse_client_session (required before fs rename). | In C++ order: refuse_client_session (REJECT), closing (wait), already open (F-PROTO-037), stopping (drop), blocklisted (REJECT + SESSION_BLOCKLISTED), missing required or floor bits, root empty, not '/'-prefixed or outside the cap path (REJECT), duplicate uuid (REJECT); skip the root check when the key is absent (Ganesha reclaim sessions). Out: feature inference and broken-root_squash flagging.<br>Risk: A 'sessionless' REJECT to a RENEWCAPS from an open kernel session trips a kernel WARN and marks the session REJECTED (requests then fail with -EACCES).<br>Question: For session ops other than REQUEST_OPEN on a connection without a session, mark the connection down (the kernel's peer-reset path reopens) instead of sending REJECT? |
| [F-PROTO-030](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-030) | REJECT encoding and blocklist signalling to clients | contract | A refused or blocklisted client learns it only from REJECT: the 7.3 kernel uses flags SESSION_BLOCKLISTED (v5+) for recover_session=clean, libcephfs logs error_string and fails with -EPERM. | Always v7 with flags and error_string; v1 REJECT for sessions without bit 8 and the kernel's v3/v4 'blacklisted' substring scan are legacy (keeping the C++ string costs nothing).<br>Risk: REJECT outside OPENING/RECONNECTING makes the kernel WARN. |
| [F-PROTO-031](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-031) | MClientSession encoding and the ops the MDS sends | contract | libcephfs ceph_abort()s on any session op other than OPEN, CLOSE, RENEWCAPS, STALE, RECALL_STATE, FLUSHMSG, FORCE_RO and REJECT; the kernel reads features only from v3+, flags from v5+ and cap_auths from v6+. | Emit v1 for head-only ops and v7 otherwise; decode client v1 and v7 (v2-v5 legacy, see F-PROTO-026).<br>Risk: cap_auths must use the exact MDSCapAuth v1 {MDSCapMatch v1 {uid, gids, path, fs_name, root_squash}, readable, writeable} layout; the kernel parses it positionally. |
| [F-PROTO-032](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-032) | Session op dispatch and rank-state gating | contract | Clients renew from up:reconnect (kernel) or up:rejoin (libcephfs) and expect answers; any client message counts as liveness for idle detection. | RENEWCAPS in every state from reconnect on; REQUEST_OPEN, FLUSHMSG_ACK and FLUSH_MDLOG once serving (clientreplay is P1, D7); REQUEST_CLOSE see F-PROTO-049. |
| [F-PROTO-033](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-033) | Unknown session op handling | tolerate | Unmodified clients send only the five client ops, so an unknown op only has to be harmless. | Log and drop (or REJECT) an unknown session op; the C++ eviction with blocklisting is optional; never crash. |
| [F-PROTO-034](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-034) | Client metadata capture and use | contract | The MDS uses root (cap check), uuid (duplicate check, reclaim), timeout (Ganesha) and hostname, and mgr modules read the metadata from client ls: stats needs hostname and root, volumes evicts by client_metadata.root, the dashboard shows versions. | Store and persist kv metadata, client features and metric spec; expose them in session ls with client_metadata.<key> filters; feature inference from ceph_version/kernel_version is out.<br>Risk: Ganesha's short-lived reclaim sessions carry only the timeout key (no root or hostname). |
| [F-PROTO-037](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-037) | Duplicate REQUEST_OPEN answered with OPEN (NOTIFY_SESSION_STATE) | contract | Both current clients set bit 16 and re-send REQUEST_OPEN after a client-side session re-init; the MDS answers with OPEN carrying push_seq and supported_features, which resynchronizes the client. | Unlike C++, include metric_spec in the duplicate OPEN (the C++ omission clears libcephfs's metric spec and stops its spec-gated metrics); the silent drop for clients without bit 16 is legacy. |
| [F-PROTO-038](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-038) | FORCE_RO: read-only MDS notification | contract | Clients learn that metadata is read-only only from FORCE_RO (then fail Fw/Fx cap waits with -EROFS); without it writers wait for caps the MDS will never grant. | Send FORCE_RO to open and stale sessions when a rank-wide read-only state is entered, and after every OPEN or reconnect in that state.<br>Question: FORCE_RO is session-wide (all shards of the rank); per-shard read-only (D10 containment) needs another path (-EROFS on W ops, no Fw/Fx in that shard). Does P0 have any rank-wide read-only state? |
| [F-PROTO-039](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-039) | FLUSHMSG / FLUSHMSG_ACK session barrier | contract | Shard migration between ranks (D6) needs the barrier that C++ subtree export uses: the client has processed every counted cap message and flushed its cap releases (FLUSHMSG_ACK) before caps move. | FLUSHMSG with seq = push_seq; waiters complete on FLUSHMSG_ACK >= seq, on stale or on close; EXPORT_PEER is assumed (all baseline clients). |
| [F-PROTO-042](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-042) | Cap renewal (REQUEST_RENEWCAPS / RENEWCAPS) | contract | Clients trust caps and leases only while RENEWCAPS replies arrive within session_timeout (kernel every timeout/4 with v7 and oldest_client_tid, libcephfs every timeout/3 with v1). | Risk: RENEWCAPS must echo the request seq (the kernel accepts only seq == s_renew_seq); a stale session is reopened with notable-caps re-issue (bit 11). |
| [F-PROTO-043](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-043) | Stale session detection and the STALE message | contract | A dead client's caps must stop blocking others after session_timeout: the MDS invalidates them, revokes without waiting, sends STALE, and evicts if a write-cap revoke was pending. | Includes the default deferred staling of F-PROTO-044. |
| [F-PROTO-044](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-044) | Deferred stale marking for quiet sessions (mds_defer_session_stale) | subset | With the C++ default a silent session goes stale only when the MDS needs something from it (a revoke or a flush), which is the behaviour clients observe today. | Implement the default (deferred) policy; the dev option mds_defer_session_stale itself is optional. |
| [F-PROTO-045](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-045) | Session autoclose eviction | contract | Silent sessions are evicted (blocklisted by default) after session_autoclose; this bounds dead clients, including Ganesha sessions whose timeout rule the defaults never reach. | Risk: The autoclose check runs before the timeout check, so a Ganesha restart that takes longer than session_autoclose loses the old session to eviction rather than to reclaim. |
| [F-PROTO-046](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-046) | Per-session timeout metadata | redesign | NFS-Ganesha V15.7 always sets timeout=300: while the MDS stays up, its sessions are never marked stale and are kept, with their caps and locks, until the successor reclaims them, they are killed, or autoclose evicts them. | Idle rules as in C++. At rank recovery a `timeout` session that misses reconnect is evicted like any other session (decision 6).<br>Reconciled: Decision 6 (2026-10-11): no recovery hold. A session that misses reconnect is evicted whether or not it carries `timeout`; every shard goes active at once. |
| [F-PROTO-049](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-049) | Client-initiated close with push-sequence handshake | contract | Unmount, Ganesha's ceph_set_uuid and the kernel manual reset close sessions with REQUEST_CLOSE carrying the push seq; the MDS persists the close, removes caps and leases and answers CLOSE. | Risk: seq < push_seq is dropped silently and the client resends only when another counted message arrives; a counting mismatch stalls unmount for mount_timeout or client_shutdown_timeout.<br>Question: Handle REQUEST_CLOSE before the rank is fully active? C++ waits for up:active, which stalls ceph_set_uuid while pending reclaims hold clientreplay. |
| [F-PROTO-050](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-050) | Counted messages and the session push sequence | contract | Both clients count CAPS, LEASE, SNAP and QUOTA messages received on the session, REQUEST_CLOSE is honoured only when the counts match, and both sides restart at 0 on reconnect. | Never send MQuarantineDisable in P0 (libcephfs does not count it).<br>Risk: When many shards send on one session, the count must be taken in send order on that session's connection. |
| [F-PROTO-051](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-051) | Client eviction: OSD blocklist, epoch barrier and session kill | contract | Operator client evict (D13) and mgr volumes and dashboard rely on eviction blocklisting the client through the mon, waiting for that OSDMap and raising the OSD epoch barrier before the session is killed. | Risk: Killing without the barrier lets other clients use objects the evicted client may still write; the session is killed without CLOSE (mark_down). |
| [F-PROTO-052](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-052) | OSDMap blocklist-driven session kill | contract | Blocklisting by anyone (mon, another rank, an admin) must kill the client's sessions on every rank, and blocklisted addresses are rejected at open and dropped before reconnect. |  |
| [F-PROTO-054](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-054) | Session termination at rank shutdown | contract | A stopping rank (max_mds shrink, D6) must hand off its shards and then close the remaining client sessions with CLOSE; when the last in rank stops (fs set down, max_mds 0) clients are evicted and must remount. |  |
| [F-PROTO-055](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-055) | Reconnect trigger and client-side ordering | contract | Cold-standby takeover (D9): clients re-send unanswered requests and early cap flushes, then MClientReconnect on the new connection once the rank shows up:reconnect; the MDS must gather all persisted sessions and accept these messages in that order. | With safe-only replies (D7) no request carries REPLAY in P0; retried requests are answered from the completed-request table.<br>Risk: libcephfs closes its session and drops caps if it never sees up:reconnect, so the rank must stay visibly in up:reconnect while clients reconnect. |
| [F-PROTO-056](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-056) | MClientReconnect encodings (v1 to v5, multi-message) | subset | The 7.3 kernel sends one v3 message (v5 parts above ~2 GiB) and libcephfs sends v5 with 'more' to an MDS with bit 12. | Decode v3 and v5 (compat 4, payload in the data segment, cap_reconnect_t v2 with flock blob and snap_follows, raw 24-byte realm records in v3, snaprealm_reconnect_t in v5); v1/v2 are out.<br>Risk: A session's reconnect completes only with the part that has no 'more' flag. |
