| [F-PROTO-057](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-057) | Reconnect processing and outcomes | contract | Clients act on OPEN (success), CLOSE (denied: caps and dirty state dropped with -EIO) or REJECT (no session) after a reconnect; the deny reasons (window over, read-only, refuse flag, missing bits) are client visible. | Reconnect OPEN always v7; feature inference is out. |
| [F-PROTO-063](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-063) | Session reclaim (MClientReclaim with CEPH_RECLAIM_RESET) | contract | NFS-Ganesha V15.7 calls ceph_start_reclaim(uuid, RESET) at every cmount creation and on nodeid takeover; libcephfs returns -EOPNOTSUPP without MDS bit 10, and FSAL_CEPH then fails the export (it ignores only -ENOENT). | RESET only: find the session by metadata uuid, require the same auth_name (-EPERM), kill it or evict and blocklist it, reply {0, OSDMap epoch, old addrs} after the kill; unknown uuid gives {0, epoch 0, no addrs}; -EINVAL for an empty uuid or other flags; FLAG_FINISH alone is a no-op. uuid, timeout and auth_name persist across MDS failover. The non-RESET path (dead code) is out.<br>Risk: Every rank 0..num_in-1 must answer (each searches its own sessions), and start_reclaim waits until each one serves; MClientReclaimReply.result is a raw host errno. |
| [F-PROTO-101](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-101) | Shared sessions: NFS-Ganesha and Samba libcephfsd | contract | By default a Ganesha daemon holds one session per rank carrying requests of many NFS clients and users for all exports below one cmount path, so one session spans shards (D4) and per-request credentials decide access. | Risk: Serializing a session's requests or cap flushes would defeat the goal of scaling synchronous NFS writes across cores; ceph_ll_open runs as uid 0, so root_squash grants refuse Ganesha write opens (C++ behaviour). |
| [F-STORE-028](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-028) | Session events: ESession and ESessions | redesign | Session open and close must be durable before the OPEN/CLOSE reply, and Ganesha reclaim needs uuid, timeout and auth_name to survive failover. | Per-rank session records (sessions span shards, D4), including sessions force-opened on the importer during shard migration; legacy type 11 is out.<br>Risk: Losing auth_name or uuid makes Ganesha reclaim fail (EPERM/ENOENT) after failover. |
| [F-STORE-075](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-075) | SessionMap object | redesign | Sessions must survive failover so that reconnect, eviction of clients that do not reconnect and Ganesha reclaim (client_metadata, auth_name, uuid, timeout) work. | Per-rank session store (sessions span shards, D4); completed-request and flush records may live with the shard (F-STORE-029/030); session preallocation is P1.<br>Question: Where completed-request records live: per session (rank) or per shard (atomic with the mutation, migrating with the shard)? |
| [F-STORE-098](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-098) | OSD epoch barrier in cap messages | contract | kclient and libcephfs pause OSD I/O until they have the osd_epoch_barrier carried in MClientCaps, so an evicted client's writes are fenced before others use new caps. | Risk: Keep the barrier monotonic (C++ setters can lower it). |

### Client sessions: P1 (1)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-053](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-053) | Eviction of sessions with oversized metadata | redesign | Per-session completed-request state can grow without bound (libcephfs never sends oldest_client_tid in RENEWCAPS), so the new session store needs a bound with warning (MDS_CLIENT_OLDEST_TID) and eviction; the 16 MiB omap threshold is C++-format specific. | Risk: Unbounded growth affects every tenant that shares the rank's session store. |

### Client sessions: P2 (3)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-023](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-023) | required_client_features enforcement and min_compat_client | subset | No mgr module, cephadm or Rook flow sets required_client_features (they use only the OSDMap require_min_compat_client), and a non-empty bitmap makes v6.0+ kernels misparse the MDSMap extension and lose max_xattr_size. | If built: REJECT at open, CLOSE at reconnect and evict on change for missing bits; the deprecated min_compat_client and inference before the test are out.<br>Risk: Ignoring the bitmap silently skips an operator's requirement.<br>Question: Rely on the built-in client floor of F-PROTO-021 instead? |
| [F-PROTO-047](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-047) | Eviction deferral while OSDs are laggy | contract | Opt-in (defer_client_eviction_on_laggy_osds default false); no default flow enables it. |  |
| [F-PROTO-048](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-048) | Cap-revoke non-responder eviction | contract | Opt-in (mds_cap_revoke_eviction_timeout default 0); by default slow revoke responders are never evicted, which Ganesha delegations rely on. |  |

### Client sessions: OUT (2)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-096](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-096) | Reclaim gating of the clientreplay to active transition | Decision 6: the rank-wide hold in up:clientreplay for `timeout` sessions that missed reconnect is not reproduced. Those sessions are evicted at the end of reconnect like any other, and their successor's reset-reclaim gets ENOENT, which FSAL_CEPH ignores. |
| [F-PROTO-022](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-022) | Feature inference for clients that send no feature bits (legacy) | Legacy: inference serves only clients that send no bitmap (kernels up to v4.18, Ceph up to 13.2.1); a REQUEST_OPEN without a bitmap is rejected instead. |

<a id="requests"></a>

## Request and reply protocol

### Request and reply protocol: P0 (37)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-060](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-060) | MDSMap epoch waits for client requests | subset | Requests carry the client's MDSMap epoch; a layout naming a pool just added with `fs add_data_pool` must wait until the MDS has that epoch instead of failing with EINVAL. | Epoch waits for layout pool validation (create with layout, setlayout, setdirlayout); the mksnap/ALLOW_SNAPS path is P1 (D8: P0 mksnap returns EPERM). |
| [F-CLU-084](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-084) | Admission of replayed and retried requests during reconnect | subset | During reconnect clients resend requests that never got a reply (retry > 0) before CLIENT_RECONNECT; a retried request already committed (in the session's completed requests) must be answered, with its created ino, before new requests can change its target. | P0: accept and queue retried requests and answer completed ones first; renewals in any state, other session ops wait, requests from sessions that are not open are dropped silently. REPLAY and ASYNC replays arise only with P1 early replies and async dirops (D7).<br>Risk: Answering a completed retry after another client unlinked its dentry trips libcephfs verify_reply_trace (ceph_abort). |
| [F-CLU-114](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-114) | Dispatcher chain, message priorities and ordering | subset | Clients rely on their messages being handled in send order where it matters (cap messages per inode in seq order, flushes and releases before later requests, requests sent before CLIENT_RECONNECT); fine-grained concurrency must keep that. | Per-connection ordering for causally related messages, metrics out of band; the C++ priority queue, dispatcher chain and single dispatch thread are internal.<br>Question: Define the per-session ordering contract the transaction engine must honour. |
| [F-CLU-116](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-116) | Outbound messaging helpers and local delivery | subset | Client-visible send rules: forwards are MClientRequestForward with strictly increasing num_fwd and must_resend, and counted pushes (CAPS, LEASE, SNAP, QUOTA) advance the session push_seq that REQUEST_CLOSE must match. | Queue counted pushes for a session without a connection; MDS-to-MDS send helpers (bootstrap deferral, map piggyback) and local delivery are free.<br>Risk: Kernel BUG_ON on a forward for a tid that already got a reply; -EMULTIHOP when num_fwd does not increase. |
| [F-PROTO-017](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-017) | Client message catalog and peer-type filtering | subset | Current clients exchange message types 22-29 and 0x310-0x314; taking client-type messages only from client peers keeps a client from injecting MDS-internal traffic. | Implement 22 MClientSession, 23 MClientReconnect, 24 MClientRequest, 25 MClientRequestForward (send), 26 MClientReply (send), 27/28 MClientReclaim/ReclaimReply, 29 MClientMetrics, 0x310-0x314; 0x315 MQuarantineDisable only with quarantine (P2). Inter-rank traffic uses the new MDS-to-MDS protocol, so MClientRequest/MClientReply between ranks are not needed and an MClientReply from a client is dropped.<br>Risk: The C++ MDS asserts on an unsafe MClientReply sent by a client; drop it instead. |
| [F-PROTO-040](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-040) | REQUEST_FLUSH_MDLOG and the client sync paths | redesign | Kernel fsync/syncfs/unmount and libcephfs fsync (every NFS COMMIT, stable WRITE and SETATTR through Ganesha) send FLUSH_MDLOG, then wait for safe replies and flush acks. | Accept op 12 without reply in any serving state; commit at once the WALs (shards) that hold pending work of that session; advertise SERVER_LUMINOUS so clients send it.<br>Risk: With safe-only replies (D7) nothing is unsafe, so the remaining sync cost is the FLAG_SYNC cap-flush commit; group-commit delay directly bounds Ganesha's synchronous throughput. |
| [F-PROTO-065](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-065) | MClientRequest versioned head (head v1, v2, v3) | subset | The 7.3 kernel and libcephfs send MClientRequest v6 with head v3 to an MDS that advertises bits 18 and 20 and FS_BTIME. | Decode head v3 (skip unknown trailing head bytes by struct_len); owner_uid/gid for CREATE, MKNOD, MKDIR, SYMLINK; caller_uid/gid and gid_list for cap checks; mdsmap_epoch ignored. Heads v1/v2 are out.<br>Risk: C++ asserts on owner -1 for a new-inode op; return -EINVAL instead. |
| [F-PROTO-067](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-067) | Request message tail: stamp, gid_list, alternate_name, fscrypt fields | subset | The v6 tail carries the client stamp (ctime/mtime/btime of mutations, POSIX timestamps), gid_list (supplementary groups for MDS cap checks), alternate_name and the fscrypt fields. | Decode the full v6 tail; use stamp and gid_list. Non-empty alternate_name or fscrypt fields come only with fscrypt or charmap (P2; bits 15 and 22 not advertised): reject (-EOPNOTSUPP) or store them opaquely; alternate_name > 8 KiB gives -ENAMETOOLONG. Message v2-v5 tails are out.<br>Question: Reject or store non-empty alternate_name/fscrypt fields while fscrypt and charmap are P2? libcephfs does not test bit 15, so it can still send fscrypt SETATTRs. |
| [F-PROTO-068](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-068) | Request flags REPLAY, WANT_DENTRY and ASYNC | subset | WANT_DENTRY is set on most namespace requests (null-dentry trace on -ENOENT); REPLAY and ASYNC only follow unsafe replies or Dc/Dr directory caps, which P0 never produces (D7). | WANT_DENTRY in full; REPLAY answered from the completed-request table (needed once early replies arrive in P1); ASYNC is P1 (never sent while Dc/Dr are not granted). |
| [F-PROTO-069](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-069) | Request argument union per op | subset | Every op's parameters travel in the 56-byte args union. | All members except snapdiff and blockdiff (P1, D8); args.open.pool only from CREATEPOOLID connections; read setattr uid/gid only for SETATTR (C++ reads them for every op). |
| [F-PROTO-071](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-071) | Request data segment per op | subset | Creates carry their initial xattrs (POSIX ACLs and LSM labels, D14) and SETXATTR its value in the data segment. | Initial-xattr map for CREATE/MKNOD/MKDIR/SYMLINK and SETXATTR values, decoded fallibly (-EINVAL); the fscrypt last-block SETATTR is P2; the MKSNAP payload is unused (MKSNAP refused with EPERM, D8); SNAP_METADATA is P1. |
| [F-PROTO-072](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-072) | Path encoding on the wire | contract | The kernel sends (ino, relative path) with .snap as an empty component and mounts by (1, server path); libcephfs and Ganesha address everything relative to cached inode numbers (#ino). | With snapshots P1, an empty first component still resolves to the snapdir (empty LSSNAP, ENOENT inside). |
| [F-PROTO-073](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-073) | Op code numbering and the write bit | subset | The op numbers and the write bit define what clients send and which ops are mutations (read-only refusal, completed-request recording). | All ops current clients send except READDIR_SNAPDIFF, FILE_BLOCKDIFF and SNAP_METADATA (P1) and LOOKUPHASH (not reachable through the libcephfs API); MKSNAP gives EPERM and LSSNAP is empty (D8); internal or unknown ops give -EOPNOTSUPP; may_write uses CEPH_O_* flags; SETFILELOCK is never recorded as completed. |
| [F-PROTO-076](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-076) | Request forwarding (MClientRequestForward) | contract | With several ranks (D6) a request that reaches a rank not owning the shard is answered with MClientRequestForward and the client resends; the MDS never relays. | num_fwd strictly increasing; the target is the shard owner from the shard map (one hop) instead of the C++ discover-or-forward logic.<br>Risk: Kernel BUG_ON if a forward arrives for a tid that already got a reply; -EMULTIHOP if num_fwd does not increase. |
| [F-PROTO-077](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-077) | Retry counters and attempt limits (32BITS_RETRY_FWD) | contract | Clients send 32-bit retry and forward counters in head v3 to an MDS with bit 18; a non-zero retry count makes the MDS consult the completed-request table. | The 8-bit widening and the 256-attempt client fallback are legacy. |
| [F-PROTO-078](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-078) | Request identity, tid ordering and requests without a session | contract | Requests are identified by (client, tid); Ganesha's shared sessions deliver many concurrent requests out of tid order, and a duplicate of an active request must be dropped. | Risk: oldest_client_tid lags behind the slowest outstanding non-SETFILELOCK request, which limits completed-table trimming. |
| [F-PROTO-079](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-079) | MClientReply head and error codes | contract | Every request is answered with MClientReply; the kernel selects the extra parser from head.op and both clients require the front to be consumed exactly (libcephfs asserts). | Linux errno values; never -EDQUOT (clients enforce quotas).<br>Risk: Trailing bytes abort libcephfs; replies must go over the session's current connection. |
| [F-PROTO-080](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-080) | Reply trace layout | contract | Traces let clients update their caches (dentries, inodes, null dentries on -ENOENT with WANT_DENTRY), and both clients assume the layout exactly. | snapbl carries the target realm's trace; traceless replies only for completed-request hits.<br>Risk: libcephfs hits ceph_abort_msg on a traceless reply whose dentry is no longer linked, and asserts an empty trace in a safe reply that follows an unsafe one. |
| [F-PROTO-081](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-081) | InodeStat versioned encoding (v9, REPLY_ENCODING) | subset | Clients with bit 9 parse InodeStat v9 (compat 1, struct_len skip) and the kernel's fixed 221-byte prefix must be byte-identical. | Send v9 with inline_version = CEPH_INLINE_NONE (inline OUT), empty fscrypt fields and optmetadata (P2), zero snap_btime, snap_metadata and rsnaps (P1), dir_pin as set, subvolume_id = the shard/subvolume id (immutable once non-zero).<br>Risk: Never emit struct_compat > 1. |
| [F-PROTO-083](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-083) | DirStat and LeaseStat records | subset | Every trace and readdir entry carries DirStat v1 and LeaseStat v2 (with alternate_name) for bit-9 clients. | DirStat trivial per D5 (frag 0, auth = owner or -1, dist empty); LeaseStat with real leases (VALID\|PRIMARY_LINK, 30 s) or null leases; the raw 10-byte lease and header-less DirStat are out. |
| [F-PROTO-087](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-087) | CREATE reply: created inode and delegated inode ranges | subset | Both clients choose the CREATE extra parser from the MDS's DELEG_INO bit, which the MDS must advertise to keep clients on their current path. | Always openc_response_t {created_ino, delegated_inos} for a CREATE that made an inode, with an empty delegated set in P0 (delegation P1, D7); empty extra when CREATE opened an existing file; no extra on MKNOD/MKDIR/SYMLINK replies (kernel -EIO). Raw u64 is out.<br>Risk: A raw u64 fails every kernel CREATE with -EIO and throws in libcephfs; delegated ranges must start at >= 0x1000 and stay <= 8192 per session. |
| [F-PROTO-088](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-088) | GETFILELOCK, GETVXATTR and FILE_BLOCKDIFF extra payloads | subset | GETFILELOCK (fcntl F_GETLK, D14) and GETVXATTR (MDS-served ceph.* vxattrs that mgr/volumes reads, e.g. ceph.dir.subvolume) need their exact extra formats; clients send GETVXATTR only to an MDS with bit 17. | GETFILELOCK raw 41-byte ceph_filelock; GETVXATTR ENCODE_START(1,1){string} filling the section exactly; FILE_BLOCKDIFF is P1 (bit 23 not advertised). |
| [F-PROTO-090](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-090) | Reply content invariants enforced by clients | contract | Clients enforce these properties on every trace, several with a kernel BUG_ON or a libcephfs abort. | Risk: Kernel BUG_ON: dentry-trace directory != locked parent (ino or snapid), LOOKUPNAME trace without target; -EREMOTEIO for inos in [0x100, 0x1000); directories must report nlink 1 (libcephfs aborts on POSIX-style counts); version, truncate_seq, time_warp_seq, xattr_version and change_attr monotonic; subvolume_id immutable once non-zero. |
| [F-NS-001](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-001) | Client op set and dispatch switch | subset | The op set is the client metadata API; every op that kclient, libcephfs, Ganesha and the mgr modules send in default flows must be handled (request-lifecycle 3.5, POSIX contract 5.1). | P0 full: LOOKUP, GETATTR, LOOKUPHASH, LOOKUPINO, LOOKUPPARENT, LOOKUPNAME, GETVXATTR, SETATTR, SETXATTR, RMXATTR, READDIR, OPEN, CREATE, MKNOD, MKDIR, SYMLINK, LINK, UNLINK, RMDIR, RENAME, SETFILELOCK, GETFILELOCK. P0 fixed answers (D8): LOOKUPSNAP -> ENOENT, LSSNAP -> empty list, MKSNAP -> EPERM. DUMMY and 0x1500-0x150f -> EOPNOTSUPP. P1: RMSNAP, RENAMESNAP, SNAP_METADATA (P0 ENOENT), READDIR_SNAPDIFF, FILE_BLOCKDIFF (P0 EOPNOTSUPP). P2: SETLAYOUT, SETDIRLAYOUT. Keep may_write() = write bit or OPEN with O_CREAT/O_TRUNC.<br>Risk: READDIR_SNAPDIFF and FILE_BLOCKDIFF carry the write bit (EROFS on a read-only MDS, recorded as completed). |
| [F-NS-002](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-002) | Request intake gating by rank state and session | redesign | Clients rely on requests being held (not failed) until the MDS can serve them and on silent drops for sessionless or closed sessions; gating should be per shard so one shard's recovery does not stall the others (multi-tenant replay goal). | Queueing of REPLAY/ASYNC requests during reconnect is P1 (D7: P0 sends no unsafe replies and grants no Dc/Dr). Retried requests (num_retry > 0) must be held and checked against completed requests in P0. |
| [F-NS-003](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-003) | Request identity and in-flight duplicate detection | contract | Requests are identified by (client entity, tid); a resent request that is still executing must not run twice, and the client's request stamp becomes the op stamp for every time the mutation sets. | The peer-request forward race is C++-internal and OUT. |
| [F-NS-007](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-007) | Request kill and abort | redesign | In-flight requests of a closed or evicted session, or of a shard being exported or stopped, must be terminated cleanly, while a mutation already committing still completes durably. | Admin 'op kill' is P2 (F-ADM-036, D13); quiesce- and quarantine-driven kills follow those P2 features. |
| [F-NS-010](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-010) | Journaled mutation commit and safe reply | redesign | D7: a mutation is acknowledged only after durable commit and other clients observe it only after commit; this safe reply is the durability point fsync, syncfs and NFS COMMIT rely on. | Per-shard WAL with group commit; only safe replies in P0.<br>Risk: Acknowledging before durability loses acknowledged mutations on failover, since P0 has no clientreplay. |
| [F-NS-012](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-012) | Completed-request table and idempotent retries/replays | redesign | Even with safe-only replies, clients resend unanswered requests after a failover or session reconnect; a committed but unacknowledged UNLINK, RENAME or O_EXCL CREATE must be answered, not re-executed (wrong ENOENT/EEXIST). | Record (client, tid, created ino) atomically with the mutation in the shard WAL; trim by oldest_client_tid (which excludes SETFILELOCK); the records must move with the shard on migration (C++ keeps them per rank, request-lifecycle 8 item 1).<br>Risk: kclient fails a reply with EIO if extra_bl is non-empty for MKNOD/MKDIR/SYMLINK (C++ bug, request-lifecycle 8 item 26): answer such retries with a trace or an empty extra. |
| [F-NS-014](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-014) | Per-op reply trace contents | contract | Traces are how clients learn results and receive caps and leases; kclient and libcephfs parse them strictly. | Risk: kclient BUG_ON if the dentry trace's directory is not the request's locked parent (rename: destination dir) or a LOOKUPNAME trace lacks the target; extra_bl only for CREATE/READDIR/LSSNAP/GETFILELOCK/GETVXATTR; the trace must be consumed exactly (EIO otherwise). |
| [F-NS-016](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-016) | On-demand journal flush for fsync, syncfs and sync writes | contract | fsync, syncfs and Ganesha's ceph_ll_sync_inode (NFS COMMIT) wait for durability through FLUSH_MDLOG and sync cap flushes (POSIX contract 3.3.10). | With safe-only replies a FLUSH_MDLOG has nothing unsafe to wait for but must be accepted; sync cap flushes commit at once. Flush only the shards involved, not the whole rank (NFS sync scaling goal). |
| [F-NS-018](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-018) | Full metadata pool gate | subset | Keeps a full metadata pool from wedging the file system while deletes still free space; the 'f' auth-cap flag (MAY_FULL) lets privileged clients proceed (F-STORE-099). | Refuse space-consuming ops (CREATE, SYMLINK, LINK, RENAME, SETXATTR, RMXATTR, SETATTR growth; MKNOD/MKDIR too if the team agrees) with one ENOSPC; do not copy the EACCES-then-ENOSPC double reply. |
| [F-NS-053](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-053) | Which rank serves each op (auth requirement, forward or discover) | redesign | D6: each request must reach the rank owning its shard; forwarding is client-resend with MClientRequestForward and strictly increasing num_fwd, and clients route by DirStat auth hints. | Shard-map routing replaces subtree auth and discover; no replicas except shard-root inodes visible in the parent shard (D5); forward at shard boundaries during multi-component traversal.<br>Risk: kclient BUG_ON on a forward for a request that already has a reply; a non-increasing num_fwd aborts the request with EMULTIHOP. |
| [F-CACHE-044](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-044) | Forward-or-discover for non-auth objects | subset | Clients route themselves and resend on MClientRequestForward; with multi-rank P0 (D6) misrouted requests must be forwarded to the shard owner. | Forward with num_fwd+1 to the owner of the target shard, never relay; no discover-based replication to break loops (D5); mds_forward_all_requests_to_auth not needed.<br>Risk: The kernel aborts with -EMULTIHOP if the forward count does not increase and BUG_ONs on a forward for a tid that already got a reply. |
| [F-CACHE-079](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-079) | DirStat delegation hints | subset | Clients route by the DirStat {frag, auth, dist} hints carried in every trace and readdir reply. | frag 0, auth = owner rank of the directory's shard (-1 when equal to the inode auth), empty dist (no replicas, D5); versioned envelope only. |
| [F-STORE-012](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-012) | Safe reply only after the journal commit (early unsafe reply) | subset | Journal side of F-NS-010/011: the safe MClientReply, FLUSH_ACK and session OPEN/CLOSE replies go out only after durable commit (D7). | P0 sends safe replies only; early (unsafe) replies, mds_early_reply and REPLAY of unsafe requests are P1. |
| [F-STORE-029](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-029) | Completed client request ids in journal and SessionMap | redesign | Retried requests after failover must be answered, not re-executed, and a create must return the same inode again (F-NS-012). | Record (client, tid, created ino) atomically with each mutation in the shard WAL, rebuild at replay, trim by oldest_client_tid, persist at checkpoint, move with the shard on migration. |

### Request and reply protocol: P1 (9)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-095](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-095) | up:clientreplay: ordered re-execution of replayed requests | redesign | D7: early (unsafe) replies and clientreplay are P1; without unsafe replies no client holds a request to replay, but the state machine and ino reservations must leave room for it. | When added: same results and same ino (head.ino), no unsafe reply for replays, before any new request; per-shard parallel replay instead of one-at-a-time is free. |
| [F-PROTO-074](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-074) | Client-chosen inode number (head.ino) | contract | head.ino is set only on replays of unsafe-replied requests and on kernel async creates with delegated inos, both P1 (D7); the inode-number reservations must leave room for them. | P0: never trust a non-zero head.ino blindly (C++ asserts); create exactly that ino only if the session owns it, else fail the request. |
| [F-PROTO-089](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-089) | Early (unsafe) and safe replies | contract | Early (unsafe) replies are a latency feature deferred by D7; P0 sends one safe reply after durable commit. | P0: exactly one safe reply per request, carrying trace and extra, with mdsmap_epoch set. P1: at most one unsafe then one traceless safe reply per tid, REPLAY handling. |
| [F-CAPS-059](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-059) | Replay of unsafe and async creates after failover | redesign | REPLAY requests follow only unsafe replies and ASYNC ones only Dc grants, both P1 (D7); in P0 clients merely retry unanswered requests. | P0: a retried completed CREATE is answered from the durable completed-request table with the created ino (namespace/membership entries). |
| [F-CAPS-092](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-092) | XLOCKDONE: read-your-own-unsafe-writes | redesign | Exists only with early (unsafe) replies, P1 (D7): the same client may build on its unsafe change while other clients wait for durability. | Risk: P0 invariant regardless: no client sees another client's non-durable metadata through replies or caps (hold write intents until commit; C++'s only exception is MClientQuota from projected rstat). |
| [F-CAPS-093](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-093) | Early-reply rdlock release and journal flush policy | redesign | Only meaningful with early replies (P1, D7): decides how long others wait behind an unsafe request and keeps layout or snapshot changes ordered after unsafe requests below. |  |
| [F-NS-011](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-011) | Early (unsafe) replies | contract | Latency feature (D7). Wire contract when added: one unsafe reply with the full trace, then a traceless safe reply; only the requester sees the uncommitted state. | The P0 recovery state machine and inode-number reservations must allow adding it (D7).<br>Risk: libcephfs asserts that a safe reply following an unsafe one has an empty trace (Client.cc:1781-1785). |
| [F-NS-013](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-013) | Replay of unsafe requests after an MDS restart | contract | Replays exist only for requests that got an unsafe reply and for kernel async creates, both P1 (D7). | When added: no early reply, reuse head.ino, traceless reply with the reconnected cap, completed ones answered from F-NS-012. |
| [F-NS-041](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-041) | Client-specified inode number (head.ino) on create | contract | Only replays of unsafe creates and kernel async creates carry a client-chosen head.ino, both P1 (D7). | Risk: Validate any client head.ino against the session's reservations and reject otherwise; C++ asserts on crafted values (request-lifecycle 8 item 27). |

### Request and reply protocol: OUT (2)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-066](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-066) | Legacy 88-byte request head (message version below 4) (legacy) | Legacy: only kernels up to v5.10, Ceph 10.2.x, or clients facing an MDS without FS_BTIME send the 88-byte head. |
| [F-PROTO-082](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-082) | Legacy (connection-feature-conditional) reply encodings (legacy) | Legacy: header-less encodings go only to sessions without bit 9 (kernels up to v5.0, Ceph up to 13.2.x). |
