## Namespace and POSIX operations

### Namespace and POSIX operations: P0 (67)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-086](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-086) | Readdir and LSSNAP reply format and ordering contract | contract | Directory listing is POSIX readdir; the kernel enforces max_entries and non-decreasing hash order from the continuation point and needs the extra on every successful READDIR/LSSNAP. | Entries ordered by rjenkins name hash, then name, even with one frag (D5); resume by path2 or offset_hash; FRAG_END/FRAG_COMPLETE/HASH_ORDER/OFFSET_HASH; LSSNAP empty with FRAG_END in P0. Readdir without REPLY_BITFLAGS (kernels before v4.7) is out.<br>Risk: Kernel BUG_ON for an LSSNAP reply with zero entries and no FRAG_END during NFS get_name, NULL dereference for a successful READDIR without extra, -EIO above max_entries. |
| [F-PROTO-098](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-098) | Mount flow requirements | contract | Mounting depends on GETATTR resolving a root-relative multi-component path (kernel, sent to any rank) and, for libcephfs, GETATTR of every ancestor up to / (quota realms). | Risk: The kernel sends the mount GETATTR to a random rank, so any rank must resolve or forward a path that crosses shard roots (D6). |
| [F-NS-005](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-005) | Per-op path resolution and locking helpers | redesign | Path resolution and per-op lock planning are needed (resolve -> plan -> lock in the transaction engine); the client-visible parts are the error codes and the null-dentry trace on ENOENT with WANT_DENTRY. | Must traverse multi-component and #ino-relative paths (kclient expectations 7 item 7; mounts of a subvolume path start at /) and forward at shard boundaries; two-path ops require depth-1 paths (EINVAL). C++ lock-ordering helpers are internal (D16). |
| [F-NS-017](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-017) | Read-only and snapshot EROFS gates | contract | EROFS for writes on a read-only MDS, for mutations under .snap paths (clients can send them in P0) and in MDS-private directories. | Read-only state should be per shard (D10). |
| [F-NS-019](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-019) | Create/remove name gates and their error order | contract | Fixed POSIX error order for created and removed names (EINVAL, EROFS, EEXIST or ENOTEMPTY for . and .., ENOENT, EEXIST) and exactly one winner among concurrent creators; libcephfs relies on the MDS for these errors. | Quarantine wait is P2 (D12); no NAME_MAX in the MDS (F-NS-088). |
| [F-NS-023](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-023) | GETATTR (stat by inode) | contract | stat/statx of any inode whose cached caps do not cover the fields; values must be at least as fresh as rdlock semantics (conflicting exclusive caps revoked or flushed first). | CEPH_STAT_RSTAT requests are served by the inode's owner (F-SNAP-039). |
| [F-NS-024](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-024) | LOOKUP of one name in a directory | contract | Core name resolution (kclient parent ino + name with WANT_DENTRY, libcephfs path walks) with parent, dentry, lease and target in one reply; lookups of names under uncommitted mutations wait for the commit. |  |
| [F-NS-025](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-025) | Negative lookup replies (null-dentry traces) | contract | Clients cache negative dentries from null-dentry traces, and the kernel splices its snapdir only when a LOOKUP of the snapdir name returns ENOENT (lookup 7.2). | Risk: Anything but ENOENT for '.snap' breaks kernel snapshot-dir access. |
| [F-NS-026](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-026) | LOOKUPINO and LOOKUPHASH (resolve an inode number) | redesign | NFS handles (Ganesha ceph_ll_lookup_inode, knfsd), libcephfs ll APIs and kclient fetches of quota-realm roots resolve bare inode numbers, including uncached ones (D6: answerable on any rank). | LOOKUPHASH = LOOKUPINO; inos in [0x100, 0x1000) and purged inos -> ESTALE; the snapid branch is P1 (F-SNAP-025).<br>Risk: C++ stalls while any rank recovers (open_ino waits for all ranks); per-shard resolution must not. |
| [F-NS-027](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-027) | LOOKUPPARENT (parent directory of an inode) | contract | Reconnecting NFS file handles (knfsd get_parent, libcephfs/Ganesha parent lookups); cheap once the ino index records the primary parent. |  |
| [F-NS-028](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-028) | LOOKUPNAME (name of an inode under its parent) | contract | knfsd get_name and libcephfs name lookups need the primary name of an inode under its parent; cheap once the ino index records (parent, name). | Question: Kernel NFS re-export is not a mgr default flow; LOOKUPNAME could move to P1 if the ino -> (parent, name) record is deferred. |
| [F-NS-030](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-030) | OPEN of an existing file or directory | contract | open(2) of an existing file: validate flags and register the mode's wanted caps, which revokes conflicting caps of other clients (Ganesha delegations rely on this). | CEPH_O_LAZY (Fl) is P2 (D12); legacy inline-data EPERM is OUT. |
| [F-NS-031](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-031) | O_TRUNC on open | contract | libcephfs sends OPEN with O_TRUNC for existing files (Ganesha, mgr/volumes rewriting auth metadata with O_CREAT\|O_TRUNC); sets mtime/ctime even at size 0 and truncates through F-NS-062. | Safe reply only in P0. |
| [F-NS-032](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-032) | READDIR paging of one dirfrag in hash order | contract | readdir, telldir/seekdir and Ganesha cookies derive offsets as (hash << 28) \| n, so entries must come in non-decreasing name-hash order with a stable order for equal hashes, resumable by last name or offset_hash. | One frag per directory (D5), but the directory index must still iterate in (hash, name) order using the hash function reported in InodeStat dir_layout. Replies without REPLY_BITFLAGS are legacy and OUT.<br>Risk: kclient fails with EIO on more than max_entries entries or an offset below its position. |
| [F-NS-033](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-033) | Readdir across dirfrags: frag selection and adjustment | subset | Clients send a frag and adopt the frag reported in DirStat; with a trivial fragtree this reduces to one leaf. | Always serve frag_t() (whole directory); accept any client frag value and map it to the single leaf; set FRAG_END/FRAG_COMPLETE; no split or merge handling (D5); legacy no-bitflags path OUT. |
| [F-NS-035](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-035) | CREATE (open with O_CREAT, optionally O_EXCL) | contract | open(O_CREAT[\|O_EXCL]) atomically creates and opens with exactly one winner; the created ino in the reply tells kclient and libcephfs (Ganesha open-with-create) whether the file was created. | Async create (ASYNC flag, delegated ino) is P1 (D7); legacy charmap EPERM is OUT.<br>Risk: If DELEG_INO is advertised the created-ino extra must be an openc_response_t, otherwise a bare u64 (kclient EIO, libcephfs misdecode). |
| [F-NS-036](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-036) | MKNOD (regular file, FIFO, device, socket) | contract | mknod/mkfifo, and for the kernel every vfs_create caller (knfsd, mknod of S_IFREG), send MKNOD; a regular file must get RDWR caps and a writable range. |  |
| [F-NS-037](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-037) | MKDIR | contract | mkdir: stored nlink 1, setgid inheritance, parent nsubdirs + 1, creator gets exclusive caps on the new directory. | Ephemeral random-pin evaluation is tolerate-or-OUT (D12); legacy charmap EPERM OUT.<br>Risk: libcephfs aborts if a directory reports nlink other than 0 or 1. |
| [F-NS-038](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-038) | SYMLINK (and readlink without an MDS op) | contract | symlink stores the target with size = rbytes = target length and readlink is served from InodeStat; mgr/volumes' clone index relies on symlink st_size == target length. | Legacy charmap EPERM OUT. |
| [F-NS-039](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-039) | New inode initialization | contract | New inodes: owner from the request head, setgid-directory inheritance, all times = op stamp, initial ACL/security xattrs stored verbatim; the MDS applies no umask (POSIX contract 7.1 item 2). | Legacy head < v3 owner fallback and inline data are OUT. |
| [F-NS-043](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-043) | Parent directory updates on link and unlink | contract | POSIX parent mtime/ctime on entry changes, exact nfiles/nsubdirs (directory nlink) and a change_attr (NFS change attribute) that moves on every entry change and never backwards. | Implement as commutative deltas (sum, max) so creates in one directory do not serialize; with one frag per directory every change bumps change_attr (fixes the C++ multi-frag miss). |
| [F-NS-044](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-044) | LINK (hard link) | contract | D14 hard links within a shard; LINK across subvolumes or shards returns EXDEV (D4, F-SNAP-052); target nlink + 1 and ctime. | Remote-target 2PC OUT (F-NS-054); global snaprealm on first link P1 (F-SNAP-011); legacy charmap EPERM OUT. |
| [F-NS-045](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-045) | UNLINK of a primary link | contract | unlink: nlink - 1, the inode stays in the shard's stray area while open (D14 unlink-while-open), parent counts and times updated. | Risk: Must not fail with ENOSPC for internal stray capacity (C++ stray-dirfrag limit). |
| [F-NS-046](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-046) | UNLINK of a remote (hard-link) dentry | contract | Removing a hard link inside a shard (no stray, nlink - 1); no cross-rank 2PC because links never cross shards (D4). |  |
| [F-NS-047](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-047) | RMDIR with emptiness checks | contract | rmdir must refuse non-empty directories exactly; libcephfs leaves ENOTEMPTY/ENOTDIR to the MDS and mgr/volumes rmtree relies on ENOTEMPTY. | Own-snapshot ENOTEMPTY check comes with snapshots (P1); witnesses OUT (F-NS-055); rmdir of a shard root (mgr/volumes purge in _deleting) uses the D4 shard-removal mechanism. |
| [F-NS-049](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-049) | RENAME of a file or directory (move, same-inode no-op) | contract | D14 rename atomicity; mgr/volumes needs atomic overwrite (.meta.tmp -> .meta), renames inside a subvolume and moves of a subvolume root between non-subvolume parents (trash to _deleting). | Shard-root moves within the root shard use the dedicated D4 mechanism; renames across shards -> EXDEV (F-SNAP-052). |
| [F-NS-050](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-050) | RENAME over an existing target | contract | rename over a file or an empty directory replaces the target atomically; the replaced inode goes to the shard's stray area (unlink-while-open). |  |
| [F-NS-051](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-051) | RENAME validation and error order | contract | Fixed rename error order (EBUSY, EINVAL for a directory moved below itself, ENOTEMPTY/EISDIR/ENOTDIR, same-inode no-op, EXDEV last); libcephfs callers rely on the MDS for several of them. | Legacy charmap EPERM OUT; witness-related skips not needed. |
| [F-NS-058](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-058) | SETATTR request: mask, locks, error order and common tail | contract | SETATTR carries chmod, chown, utimes and truncate when the client holds no exclusive cap (and Ganesha's setattr path); fixed mask semantics, lock and revoke behaviour, ctime/change_attr tail. | fscrypt bits P2 (F-NS-064); legacy < v6 branch OUT. |
| [F-NS-059](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-059) | chmod and chown, with set-user/group-ID clearing | contract | chmod/chown semantics including S_ISUID/S_ISGID clearing on chown of executables and libcephfs KILL_SUID/KILL_SGID on writes. |  |
| [F-NS-060](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-060) | Explicit times (utimes) and btime, with time_warp_seq | contract | utimes/utimensat set explicit atime/mtime (time_warp_seq + 1); libcephfs and Ganesha can set btime, which statx and mgr/volumes (created_at) read. | Legacy BTIME clearing for old heads OUT. |
| [F-NS-061](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-061) | Size growth through SETATTR | contract | truncate growth: size = rbytes = new size, mtime = op stamp, client ranges recomputed; EFBIG and EDQUOT stay client-side. |  |
| [F-NS-062](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-062) | Truncate (shrink) with asynchronous OSD truncation | contract | Shrinking must bump truncate_seq, send CEPH_CAP_OP_TRUNC to cap holders and truncate OSD objects with truncate_seq/size so OSDs discard stale writes; pending truncations survive failover. | Persistence of pending truncations is redesigned in the shard WAL; the realm SnapContext is empty in P0.<br>Risk: kclient applies TRUNC only if truncate_seq advances; a failing or stuck OSD truncate must not crash the MDS (C++ asserts) or block other shards. |
| [F-NS-063](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-063) | Timestamps from the client clock and change_attr | contract | Every ctime/mtime comes from the client's request stamp; change_attr (NFS change attribute, statx change cookie) increments on each metadata change. | Risk: Version fields (change_attr, time_warp_seq, truncate_seq, xattr_version) must be monotonic (kclient expectations 7 item 11). |
| [F-NS-066](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-066) | SETXATTR and RMXATTR of stored xattrs | contract | setxattr/removexattr with CREATE/REPLACE semantics for user, security, trusted and system names (Ganesha user xattrs, ACLs); mgr/volumes earmark/enctag need zero-length values stored and returned as such. | Other ceph.* names than vxattrs and the two ceph.mirror names -> EINVAL.<br>Question: C++ RMXATTR does no MDS-cap check at all; add MAY_WRITE (no peer depends on the gap)? |
| [F-NS-067](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-067) | Total xattr size limit (max_xattr_size) | contract | max_xattr_size comes from the MDSMap (fs set); the kclient uses it to choose local or synchronous xattr updates; larger maps get ENOSPC. |  |
| [F-NS-068](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-068) | xattr blob delivery to clients | contract | Clients cache the whole xattr map from InodeStat and cap messages and serve getxattr/listxattr locally, versioned by xattr_version. | Risk: When Xs is requested the reply must carry the xattrs (libcephfs client_debug_getattr_caps aborts otherwise). |
| [F-NS-069](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-069) | POSIX ACLs stored as opaque xattrs | contract | D14 POSIX ACL storage: opaque system.posix_acl_* xattrs and initial ACLs in create payloads stored verbatim; evaluation is client-side (Ganesha sets client_acl_type=posix_acl). |  |
| [F-NS-077](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-077) | SETFILELOCK: POSIX fcntl byte-range locks | contract | D14: POSIX fcntl and OFD record locks; kclient, libcephfs and Ganesha (NFSv4 locks) send SETFILELOCK; cluster-wide arbitration in MDS memory, owner = (session, owner[, pid]). | Lock tables are not journaled and must move with the shard on migration; fix the 32-bit merge overflow (flock.cc:452).<br>Risk: A resent SETFILELOCK is re-executed (never in completed requests), so same-owner relock and unlock must be idempotent. |
| [F-NS-078](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-078) | BSD flock whole-file locks | contract | flock(2): mgr/volumes authorize uses blocking LOCK_EX on auth metadata files and cephfs-mirror takes LOCK_EX\|LOCK_NB on directories (EWOULDBLOCK); D14. |  |
| [F-NS-079](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-079) | Blocking lock waits and wake-ups | contract | D14 blocking lock waits (F_SETLKW, blocking flock) parked in the MDS without timeout until granted or cancelled. |  |
| [F-NS-080](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-080) | Lock wait interruption (FCNTL_INTR / FLOCK_INTR) | contract | Signals interrupt blocked lock waits through *_INTR unlocks; the original request then returns EINTR. |  |
| [F-NS-081](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-081) | fcntl deadlock detection (EDEADLK) | contract | D14: the C++ MDS returns EDEADLK for fcntl wait-for cycles (up to 5 hops), which applications can observe. | Detection scope per rank as in C++ (cycles spanning ranks are not detected). |
| [F-NS-082](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-082) | GETFILELOCK (F_GETLK) | contract | F_GETLK returns the first conflicting lock; reply extra = raw 41-byte ceph_filelock, no trace. |  |
| [F-NS-083](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-083) | Lock cleanup on cap removal and session end | contract | Eviction and cap removal must release a client's locks so others can proceed; clients then fail lock calls and I/O with EIO. |  |
| [F-NS-084](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-084) | Lock reinstatement after an MDS restart | contract | D9 failover: held locks are resubmitted in MClientReconnect and must be reinstated; pending waits are resent and executed afresh. | Reinstate for every inode of the shard named in the reconnect, not only cached ones (C++ gap); legacy flockbl in MClientCaps OUT. |
| [F-NS-085](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-085) | Directory link count and size presentation | contract | Clients present directory nlink as 2 + nsubdirs and size as entries or rbytes, so the MDS must store directory nlink 1 (0 once unlinked) and report exact nsubdirs. | Risk: libcephfs calls ceph_abort() for a directory nlink other than 0 or 1. |
| [F-NS-086](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-086) | Access time is not maintained by the MDS | contract | Documented departure kept (D14): atime is stored and settable but never updated on reads; fstests already treat atime as not maintained. |  |
| [F-NS-087](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-087) | Timestamp range limited to unsigned 32-bit seconds | contract | Wire ceph_timespec seconds are unsigned 32-bit and the kernel sets s_time_min/max to match; keep the range. |  |
| [F-NS-088](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-088) | Limits enforced only by clients, and no hard-link limit | contract | Documented departures kept (D14): NAME_MAX, max_file_size (EFBIG) and quotas are client-enforced; no EMLINK. |  |
| [F-NS-090](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-090) | Data-path coherency departures outside the MDS | contract | Coherency comes from cap revocation driven by MDS lock states; the documented O_APPEND, mmap and object-boundary departures are kept (D14). | LazyIO (Fl) is P2 (D12). |
| [F-NS-091](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-091) | statfs is answered by the monitors | contract | statfs goes to the monitors; the MDS only serves libcephfs's quota-root GETATTR and supplies quota and rbytes (F-SNAP-048). Nothing else to build. |  |
| [F-NS-092](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-092) | Conformance suites in the tree (pjd-fstest, xfstests) | subset | pjd-fstest and xfstests are the acceptance gate for D14 (POSIX contract 7.1); nothing to implement in the MDS. | Run pjd-fstest (all directories except chflags) and xfstests with the existing ceph.exclude list on kclient and ceph-fuse mounts as release criteria. |
| [F-CACHE-003](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-003) | Refusal of client operations on private and system inodes | subset | Clients must never see MDS-private numbers and must get ESTALE for numbers that name nothing (kernel and libcephfs reject [0x100,0x1000)). | LOOKUPINO/LOOKUPHASH/LOOKUPPARENT/LOOKUPNAME on a number below 0x1000 (other than root 1) or not in use returns ESTALE; the new design exposes no system directories (~mdsN, strays, lost+found), so the EPERM/EROFS paths do not arise.<br>Risk: An exposed number in [0x100,0x1000) makes kernel lookups fail with -EREMOTEIO. |
| [F-CACHE-004](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-004) | Timestamps, time_warp_seq and change_attr | contract | POSIX timestamps and the NFS change attribute: the kernel exposes change_attr as i_version with STATX_ATTR_CHANGE_MONOTONIC, and NFS re-export relies on it. | Legacy InodeStat encoding and the pre-bit-59 cap-flush gates are out.<br>Risk: change_attr and time_warp_seq must never go backwards, including after failover, shard migration and in the shard-boundary copy (C++ replicas miss btime/change_attr updates). |
| [F-CACHE-010](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-010) | Inode version and client-visible version | contract | kclient and libcephfs apply attributes only from the auth and only when version > (cached & ~1); the MDS sends 2 x version (+1 when projected). | Mechanism free: a durable per-inode monotonic counter suffices (the C++ parent-dirfrag counter is internal); the +1 appears only with early replies (P1).<br>Risk: A version that goes backwards after failover or shard migration makes clients silently ignore attribute updates. |
| [F-CACHE-041](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-041) | Path traversal (path_traverse) | contract | Every client request names (base ino, relative path) and relies on the traversal error codes (ENOENT, ENOTDIR, EIO for damage, ESTALE for unknown bases, EINVAL for a misplaced snapdir). | Keep addressing relative to any inode and the empty first component for .snap (empty LSSNAP in P0, D8); C++ traversal flags are internal and lock-cache attachment is P1.<br>Question: Paths cross shard boundaries; the owning rank must also know the absolute path of its shard root for MDS auth-cap path checks, and that path changes on the mgr/volumes trash rename. |
| [F-CACHE-045](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-045) | Hard-link target resolution | redesign | Hard links within a shard are P0 (D14); since links never cross shards (D4), resolving a remote dentry is shard-local through the inode-number index. | Keep the C++ outcome for an unresolvable target: EIO on traversal, entry omitted from readdir, damage recorded. |
| [F-CACHE-071](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-071) | Root and system inode initial attributes and default layouts | contract | Clients and mgr/volumes see the root's initial attributes after `fs new`: S_IFDIR 0755, uid/gid mds_root_ino_uid/gid (0/0), ctime=mtime=btime, default layout on the first data pool, dl_dir_hash rjenkins. | Root inode only; system inodes are internal. |
| [F-CACHE-074](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-074) | Fragment identifier and fragment tree (frag_t, fragtree_t) | subset | Clients decode frag_t and fragtree encodings in InodeStat and readdir replies; D5 exposes one frag per directory. | Encode the trivial fragtree (no splits) and frag_t() everywhere; accept any frag value in requests and map it to frag_t(); no per-frag objects; the byte-swap decode fix is out. |
| [F-CACHE-075](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-075) | Dentry name hash per directory (dl_dir_hash) | contract | Clients hash names with dir_layout.dl_dir_hash to compute readdir positions and to route; the MDS must order and look up entries with the identical hash. | New directories use rjenkins (2); values 0/1 only make kernel routing and LOOKUPHASH disagree; the legacy pre-v4 decode is out. |
| [F-CACHE-076](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-076) | Fragtree and dir_layout in InodeStat | subset | Every directory InodeStat carries fragtree and dir_layout, and libcephfs and the kernel replace their tree from it. | Always the empty tree plus dir_layout, versioned InodeStat only (the legacy DIRLAYOUTHASH form is out). |
| [F-CACHE-077](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-077) | Hash-ordered dentry storage and readdir order | contract | With REPLY_BITFLAGS the kernel and libcephfs build readdir offsets from the 24-bit name hash and need entries in non-decreasing (hash24, name) order, resuming at offset_hash or after the last name. | The directory index (store and cache) must iterate in (hash24, name) order; names compare bytewise without folding (charmap is P2); legacy readdir without bitflags is out.<br>Risk: An entry below the current position makes the kernel's ceph_readdir return EIO. |
| [F-CACHE-078](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-078) | Readdir fragment mapping and resume across refragmentation | subset | Fragment side of readdir resume (F-NS-033); with one frag per directory (D5) it reduces to mapping. | Any requested frag maps to frag_t(); the reply names frag 0; resume by offset_hash or path2 (F-CACHE-077). |
| [F-STORE-099](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-099) | Metadata-pool-full ENOSPC policy | subset | A full metadata pool must stop metadata growth before the OSD fail-safe silently drops MDS writes; kclient and libcephfs surface the error. | Refuse growth ops (setlayout, setxattr, rmxattr, create, symlink, mksnap, link, rename) with ENOSPC unless the MDS cap grants `f`; size-growing setattr gets ENOSPC; deletes and truncates proceed.<br>Question: Return ENOSPC (intended, doc/cephfs/full.rst) instead of the EACCES clients see from C++ (double-reply bug)? |
| [F-BG-025](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-025) | Purging inodes are invisible to clients (ENOENT / ESTALE) | contract | NFS handles (kernel knfsd, Ganesha via ll_lookup_inode) resolve inodes by number: an unlinked but referenced inode must stay reachable by LOOKUPINO until its purge starts, then lookups fail with ENOENT/ESTALE. | LOOKUPINO of a not-yet-purging stray succeeds; after purge starts path lookups give ENOENT and ino lookups ESTALE; LOOKUPPARENT of an unlinked inode and internal inos give ESTALE; reconnect ignores caps on purged inodes.<br>Risk: The new ino-lookup index (D2 redesign, answerable from any rank per D6) must keep strays until purge. |
| [F-BG-026](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-026) | Namespace rules for stray and unlinked directories | subset | POSIX: creating in or renaming into a removed directory fails with ENOENT, and rename between two links of the same inode is a no-op returning 0. | Keep ENOENT for create/link/rename into an unlinked directory and the same-inode rename no-op; internal stray/system objects are simply unaddressable (ESTALE); MDS-originated rename validation is C++ internal and dropped. |

### Namespace and POSIX operations: P2 (7)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-NS-020](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-020) | Directory entry limits (ENOSPC) | subset | Admin ENOSPC limits: mds_dir_max_entries is off by default and the dirfrag-size limit is meaningless with one frag per directory (D5). | Optional mds_dir_max_entries only; drop mds_bal_fragment_size_max checks.<br>Risk: UNLINK, RMDIR and RENAME must never fail with ENOSPC because an internal stray area is full (C++ stray-dirfrag artifact). |
| [F-NS-064](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-064) | fscrypt inode fields and last-block truncate | contract | Kernel fscrypt needs opaque fscrypt_auth/fscrypt_file storage and the last-block read-modify-write truncate; fscrypt is P2 (D12) and no default mgr flow sets it. | P0 decodes the MClientRequest v6 fscrypt fields, encodes empty fscrypt fields in InodeStat and refuses the FSCRYPT_AUTH/FSCRYPT_FILE setattr bits; legacy < v6 branch OUT. |
| [F-NS-075](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-075) | Case-insensitive and normalized directories (charmap) | contract | Charmap/case-insensitivity is P2 (D12) and default mgr/volumes flows do not set it (only --casesensitive/--normalization do). | P0: no charmap metadata; charmap vxattr sets refused, GETVXATTR -> ENODATA; legacy EPERM gate OUT.<br>Question: D15: bit 22 means CHARMAP to libcephfs and SUBVOLUME_METRICS to the 7.3 kernel; decide what to advertise. |
| [F-NS-076](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-076) | alternate_name on dentries | contract | Only fscrypt (kernel) and charmap (libcephfs) clients produce alternate_name, both P2 (D12). | P0: LeaseStat v2 carries an empty alternate_name; reserve an optional per-dentry field in the format. |
| [F-CACHE-009](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-009) | fscrypt metadata fields | contract | fscrypt is P2 (D12) and only the kernel uses it. | Leave room in the inode record for opaque fscrypt_auth, fscrypt_file and last-block blobs and in dentries for alternate_name. |
| [F-CACHE-081](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-081) | Per-directory entry limit | contract | Optional admin cap (default off) that no peer sets; cheap to keep. |  |
| [F-ADM-095](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-095) | mgr/volumes: charmap (case sensitivity, normalization, encoding) | contract | Charmap and case-insensitivity are P2 (D12); used only when an operator asks for them at create or with `fs subvolume charmap`. | Question: What does P0 return for ceph.dir.casesensitive/normalization/encoding (EOPNOTSUPP or EINVAL)? Confirm mgr/volumes sets them only on request. |

### Namespace and POSIX operations: OUT (2)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-NS-089](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-089) | Unsupported Linux extensions: renameat2 flags and O_TMPFILE | No MDS op exists: kclient and ceph-fuse refuse renameat2 flags and the kernel offers no O_TMPFILE for ceph; adding them would need client changes. |
| [F-CACHE-080](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-080) | Per-dirfrag size limit (ENOSPC) | The 100000-entry per-dirfrag ENOSPC is an artifact of C++ fragment storage; with one client-visible frag (D5) it would cap every directory, so the new store scales directories internally (F-STORE-047). |

<a id="caps"></a>

## Capabilities and leases

### Capabilities and leases: P0 (42)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-082](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-082) | Recording caps and snaprealms from reconnect messages | contract | Reconnected caps keep the client's cap_id and wanted bits, are issued without revoke with seq reset, and dirty cap flushes received during reconnect must be remembered; kclient and libcephfs match later cap messages by cap_id. | Record realms {ino, seq, parent} for reconciliation (F-CLU-092); the parent ino + name hint may be used to find the inode. |
| [F-CLU-087](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-087) | Matching reconnected caps to inodes: OpenFileTable prefetch and open by number | redesign | After failover the new holder's cache is cold, yet every cap a client reports must be attached to its inode, so open inodes must be loadable by number quickly; the OpenFileTable format is out (D2) but the purpose is P0. | Per-shard open-file index plus the D4 ino-lookup mechanism; the reconnect's parent ino + single-name hint may be used. |
| [F-CLU-090](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-090) | Choosing lock states from reconnected caps | redesign | After reconnect the Rust lock/cap engine must start from the caps clients actually hold (exclusive or buffered write caps mean an exclusive writer) so caps and metadata state agree before serving; C++ SimpleLock state choice is out (D16). |  |
| [F-CLU-098](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-098) | Leftover reconnect caps and cap reissue at activation | subset | At activation caps are re-evaluated and may be granted or revoked with the current OSD epoch barrier (libcephfs 7.1, rank-lifecycle 7), and caps whose inode was never found must be answered. | Reissue/revoke all reconnected caps with the OSD epoch barrier; for caps whose inode cannot be found, log a cluster warning and send an EXPORT.<br>Question: C++ sends an EXPORT with cap_id 0 that both clients ignore; send one with the client's cap_id and peer.mds = -1 so clients really drop the cap? |
| [F-CLU-099](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-099) | File size and mtime recovery for writers that did not come back | redesign | Data written by a client that never came back must be preserved up to its granted max_size: size and mtime recovered by probing data objects (never shrinking), stat/read/write of the file blocked until done, which needs client_ranges persisted (F-CAPS-022). |  |
| [F-PROTO-070](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-070) | Embedded cap and dentry-lease release records | contract | Both clients piggy-back cap and dentry-lease releases on requests and drop the bits locally before sending, so the MDS must apply them before the op instead of waiting for its own revocation. | Ignored for REPLAY requests and for stale mseq or another cap_id. |
| [F-PROTO-084](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-084) | Caps, xattrs and inline data carried inside InodeStat | subset | InodeStat carries the cap issued with the reply, the xattr blob and max_size, and clients rely on their versioning rules. | Caps and xattr rules per F-CAPS-009 and F-NS-068; inline_version always CEPH_INLINE_NONE, never 0 (D12 inline OUT; libcephfs asserts inline_version > 0 after an inline GETATTR); readdir stops on the max_bytes estimate. |
| [F-PROTO-091](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-091) | MClientCaps encoding (v14, xattrs in the middle segment) | contract | Caps are the core client consistency protocol; the kernel sends v12 and parses up to v12, libcephfs at main sends and expects v14. | Emit v14 compat 1; decode v12 and v14 (xattr blob in the middle segment, EXPORT/IMPORT peer records for D6); v1/v2 encodings and other decoded versions are legacy; inline fields empty.<br>Risk: Validate xattr_len against the middle segment without asserting (C++ asserts). |
| [F-PROTO-092](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-092) | MClientCapRelease encoding and the OSD epoch barrier | contract | Both clients batch cap releases in MClientCapRelease v2 with their OSD epoch barrier, which the MDS must honour before further RADOS ops. |  |
| [F-PROTO-093](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-093) | MClientLease encoding | tolerate | Dentry leases let clients cache lookups: the MDS revokes them with REVOKE, the kernel renews and acks, libcephfs releases. | P0 never sends MClientLease (every reply carries the null LeaseStat of F-CAPS-038) and drops incoming lease messages without aborting. The REVOKE/RENEW/RELEASE contract (libcephfs accepts only REVOKE) comes with real leases in P1 (F-CAPS-039..044).<br>Risk: Revoke uses mask 1 (userspace numbering) and last = CEPH_NOSNAP.<br>Reconciled: Reconciles PROTO (P0 contract) with CAPS (real dentry leases are P1; directory Fs is the P0 dentry cache). |
| [F-CAPS-001](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-001) | Cap bit model and composite masks | contract | Fixed wire layout of cap bits and composite masks shared with kclient and libcephfs (ceph_fs.h); every cap message, reply cap and getattr mask uses it (capabilities 3.1). | Risk: Never grant Fa. On directories Fc and Fr mean Dc and Dr; the whole CEPH_CAP_ANY_DIR_OPS mask (Fc\|Fr\|Fa\|Fl) must stay off directory caps until async dirops (P1). |
| [F-CAPS-002](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-002) | Open mode to wanted caps | contract | The MDS must derive wanted caps from the open mode exactly as clients do (ceph_flags_to_mode, ceph_caps_for_mode); the kclient re-registers missing wanted bits with OPEN requests every 5 s (capabilities 3.8). | Risk: libcephfs lists Fr\|Fc in wanted for a directory opened with open(); on directories these are Dr\|Dc and must not be granted in P0. |
| [F-CAPS-003](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-003) | Capability record, cap ids and sequence numbers | contract | cap_id, seq, issue_seq and mseq rules are the client-visible consistency protocol: kclient treats seq <= cap->seq as pre-IMPORT, takes issue_seq only from replies and IMPORT, and messages with another cap_id or older mseq are stale (kclient 7 item 13, capabilities 7 items 1-2). | Contract on the counters and confirm_receipt semantics; the in-memory record is free and is never persisted.<br>Risk: Seqs must rise per cap in delivery order: in a multi-core MDS all messages for one (inode, client) must be serialised onto the session connection in seq order. New cap_ids must exceed every id reported in reconnects. |
| [F-CAPS-004](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-004) | Lock-state-derived allowed caps | redesign | The C++ lock tables are internal (D16), but the cap combinations they produce are the coherency contract: at most one holder of any x bit, s bits revoked before a conflicting change, shared writers get Fr\|Fw without Fc/Fb, Fl only if wanted, no dir-op bits on directories (lock-types 7 item 1). | Compute allowed caps from the new engine's per-inode state. Legacy NOINLINE/NOPOOLNS step out (F-CAPS-007); replica column OUT (F-CAPS-035); quiesce and quarantine masks come with QOS (P2).<br>Risk: Granting an x bit to two clients, or Fc/Fb while several clients write, silently corrupts client caches. |
| [F-CAPS-005](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-005) | Loner selection (exclusive client) | redesign | Loner selection is internal, but its outcome is the main single-client performance contract: a sole writer must get Fx\|Fb\|Fc (buffered I/O, local size and mtime), otherwise every write goes synchronously to the OSDs (capabilities 3.3). | Risk: Do not carry over the C++ rule that directories with subtree-root dirfrags never get a loner (no Fx on pinned subvolume roots): a shard root is never split. |
| [F-CAPS-006](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-006) | Wanted-cap-driven stable-state policy | redesign | Internal policy that decides cached, buffered or synchronous I/O (one writer buffered, writers plus others uncached Fr\|Fw, readers Fs\|Fc\|Fr); clients observe only the caps they get (capabilities 3.3). | Policy is free as long as F-CAPS-004 combinations hold; a read-only file system grants only shared caps. |
| [F-CAPS-009](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-009) | Caps in request replies | contract | Every reply trace inode and readdir entry carries ceph_mds_reply_cap (caps, wanted, cap_id, seq, mseq, realm, AUTH flag); clients take max_size only from an AUTH cap and rstat or dir_pin only from AUTH or newer replies (kclient 3.8.2), and a reply never revokes. | Legacy inline-data payload out (always CEPH_INLINE_NONE). Pre-granting liked caps is policy but cheap to keep.<br>Risk: InodeStat inline_version must be CEPH_INLINE_NONE, never 0 (libcephfs asserts). A reply must never remove bits. |
| [F-CAPS-010](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-010) | New caps on open, create, mknod and mkdir | contract | Open, create, mknod and mkdir must create or extend the client's cap (wanted = caps for the mode) and return it in the reply; the creator owning the new inode's x caps and an initial [0, stripe_unit] write range avoids an immediate revoke and max_size round trip. | With D7 the reply follows the durable commit, so the C++ exception of a range granted before the create is durable disappears.<br>Risk: cap_id 1 for a new inode matches the kclient's fabricated async-create cap (P1). |
| [F-CAPS-011](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-011) | Cap grant and revoke messages (GRANT/REVOKE) | contract | Clients require REVOKE whenever bits are removed and answer every REVOKE with a cap message, possibly only after writeback or page-cache invalidation; a GRANT may carry only max_size or field updates (capabilities 7 item 3). | Keep the nlink 0 rule: add Ls to wanted and push a grant so clients drop the unlinked inode (F-BG-008).<br>Risk: A message that revokes must never add bits. Every cap message carries osd_epoch_barrier and counts toward the session push_seq. |
| [F-CAPS-013](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-013) | Client cap message processing (handle_client_caps) | contract | UPDATE, FLUSH, DROP, RELEASE and RENEW are all cap updates: revoke acks (confirm_receipt with seq), wanted changes and dirty flushes; clients rely on stale messages (other cap_id, older mseq, non-owner rank) being dropped and re-send flushes after IMPORT or reconnect. | Mask the client's caps with issued before confirm_receipt (the kernel sends its implemented set). C++ frozen-inode deferral becomes the shard-migration quiesce; snapflush branches P1.<br>Risk: A lost or mis-applied revoke ack stalls every conflicting operation with no timeout. |
| [F-CAPS-014](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-014) | Wanted caps tracking (issue_seq rule) | contract | Wanted is replaced only when issue_seq == last_issue, otherwise only extended, so a stale update cannot undo a newer open; otherwise the kclient falls back to OPEN retries every 5 s (capabilities 3.8). | OpenFileTable logging is C++ storage (D2); reopening files with caps after failover belongs to the reconnect design. |
| [F-CAPS-015](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-015) | Xattr blob and inline data carried on caps | contract | Clients serve getxattr/listxattr locally under Xs and setxattr locally under Xx when the blob fits max_xattr_size, so the blob and xattr_version must travel on grants and replies and be accepted from flushes (capabilities 3.7). | Xattr half only; the inline-data half is legacy (deprecated inline_data flag) and out.<br>Question: C++ ignores an oversize flushed blob but still bumps xattr_version, so the client's change is lost silently; keep that or reject differently? |
| [F-CAPS-016](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-016) | Truncate notification (CEPH_CAP_OP_TRUNC) | contract | A shrinking truncation must be pushed to every cap holder (CEPH_CAP_OP_TRUNC with truncate_seq/size) after it commits; clients truncate their page caches from it. | Risk: The kernel WARNs if truncate_seq rises while it still holds Fr or Fl: revoke everything but Fc\|Fb before a shrinking truncate commits (capabilities 7 item 13). |
| [F-CAPS-018](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-018) | Dirty metadata flush and FLUSH_ACK | contract | Buffered writers and Ax/Xx/Fx holders report size, times, mode, owner and xattrs only through cap flushes; FLUSH_ACK must follow the durable commit (D7) and the kernel treats it as cumulative per inode (capabilities 7 item 5). Ganesha's setattr-then-sync path depends on it. | Field rules kept (size only grows, Fw times only newer, Fx may move mtime back, xattr version newer). Legacy parts out: inline data, feature gating of change_attr/btime (always apply).<br>Risk: Acks for one inode must leave in commit order; flushed values must not be visible to other clients before commit.<br>Question: C++ applies flushed Ax/Xx fields without checking that the cap holds those bits or the client's MDS auth caps, and drops atime unless Fx is dirty (kernel atime-forward under Fw is lost). Validate dirty against issued x bits and fix atime? |
| [F-CAPS-019](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-019) | Flush idempotency (completed_flushes, oldest_flush_tid) | redesign | Clients re-send unacked flushes with their original tids after IMPORT and reconnect, and Fx/Ax fields are not monotonic, so applied flushes must be recognised exactly once across retransmission and failover. | Durable completed flush tids per session, trimmed by oldest_flush_tid, recorded with the cap update in the shard WAL; MDS_CLIENT_OLDEST_TID health and clog warning as in F-ADM-075. SessionMap format out (D2).<br>Question: Flush tids are per client session while updates land in per-shard WALs: the completed set must be rebuilt after a rank failure and follow a shard on migration (or all acks must precede EXPORT on the connection). |
| [F-CAPS-020](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-020) | Synchronous flush and journal-flush heuristics | redesign | fsync, Ganesha COMMIT and the sync Ganesha runs after every SETATTR wait for a FLAG_SYNC flush ack whenever metadata is buffered in caps; with per-shard WAL group commit (D7) the ack comes at commit, not after the C++ 5 s tick (motivating use case: synchronous NFS writes across cores). | Commit FLAG_SYNC flushes, and flushes someone waits on, immediately; the C++ _need_flush_mdlog and tick heuristics are dropped. |
| [F-CAPS-021](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-021) | max_size and per-client writeable ranges (client_ranges) | contract | A client may write only below max_size, takes it only from its AUTH cap (from a GRANT only when it includes a file-write bit) and blocks until a GRANT raises it; the range must be durable before it is granted so size recovery knows how far to probe (capabilities 3.9). | client_ranges persisted in the new inode format (D2); the growth formula is policy.<br>Risk: Never advertise a max_size above the durable range; a raised range is pushed with a GRANT (seq+1) to every Fw\|Fb holder. |
| [F-CAPS-022](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-022) | File size recovery after loss of a writer | redesign | When a client holding a writeable range dies or is evicted, or a rank fails over, size and mtime must be recovered by probing data objects up to the range, otherwise acknowledged writes past the recorded size disappear (durability, D9). | Outcome: size = max(recorded, probed), never shrink, block conflicting access until done, bounded parallel probes. C++ PRE_SCAN/SCAN lock states are internal.<br>Risk: C++ marks the whole rank damaged on a probe error; contain it per shard (D10). |
| [F-CAPS-028](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-028) | Batched cap release (MClientCapRelease) | contract | Clients give up caps through MClientCapRelease (and embedded releases); a release whose issue_seq < last_issue must not remove a cap re-issued meanwhile, and the kclient also releases caps named by GRANT/REVOKE/IMPORT for inodes it lacks (capabilities 3.12). | Removing a cap also drops that client's fcntl/flock locks and writeable range (needs size recovery if the client was killed).<br>Risk: Removing a cap on a stale issue_seq loses a cap the client believes it holds. |
| [F-CAPS-029](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-029) | Cap and dentry-lease releases embedded in requests | contract | kclient and libcephfs drop caps (and dentry leases) inside requests so the requester's own Fs or lease need not be revoked; the MDS applies them before locking and re-issues suppressed grants at cleanup (lock-types 7 item 9). | Skipped for replayed requests. The dentry-lease part is a no-op until real leases exist (P1). |
| [F-CAPS-030](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-030) | Stale-session cap handling | contract | CEPH_SESSION_STALE makes clients treat all caps as p; revoking write caps from a stale client evicts (blocklists) it, other bits are force-revoked, and RENEWCAPS re-issues caps with a GRANT then a REVOKE (capabilities 3.13, kclient 7 item 4). | Legacy full re-issue for clients without LAZY_CAP_WANTED out. Writer loss triggers size recovery (F-CAPS-022).<br>Risk: Other clients must not use caps before the blocklist epoch: stamp the new osd_epoch_barrier on later cap messages. |
| [F-CAPS-031](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-031) | Late revocation warnings, health and eviction | subset | With no lock-level timeout, the late-revoke health warning is the operator's only signal that one client blocks others; mon, mgr and dashboard show MDS_CLIENT_LATE_RELEASE (operator essential, D13). | P0: per-client late-revoke tracking, MDS_CLIENT_LATE_RELEASE(_MANY) in the beacon health (F-ADM-075), clog warning with backoff. Eviction after mds_cap_revoke_eviction_timeout (default off) is P2 (F-PROTO-048). |
| [F-CAPS-032](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-032) | Cache-pressure cap recall (CEPH_SESSION_RECALL_STATE) | redesign | Caps pin MDS memory; RECALL_STATE with max_caps is how the MDS bounds per-client cap counts with many clients, and MDS_CLIENT_RECALL is a standard health warning; clients shrink only as fast as applications drop inodes (libcephfs 7.1 item 18). | Wire: CEPH_SESSION_RECALL_STATE max_caps, release accounting, MDS_CLIENT_RECALL(_MANY). Thresholds and decay throttles redesigned for per-shard memory accounting (D3).<br>Risk: Recall never evicts: the MDS must keep working with clients that cannot shrink. |
| [F-CAPS-034](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-034) | Cap re-establishment after MDS failover (cap side) | contract | Cold standby takeover with reconnect is P0 (D9): caps are rebuilt from reconnect records (client cap_id, reported issued, seq/issue_seq/mseq zero), cap ids are raised above every reported id, and flushes re-sent early during reconnect are accepted. | Mask reported issued bits with what the new policy allows (strip dir-op bits and Fa). Choosing lock states from caps (F-CLU-090) is redesigned. |
| [F-CAPS-038](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-038) | Dentry lease issue | tolerate | Every trace dentry and readdir entry must carry a LeaseStat, but correctness does not need real leases: clients fall back to directory Fs with shared_gen or to a LOOKUP, which is what they already do whenever C++ sends mask 0. | Always encode the no-lease record (mask 0, duration 0, seq 0, alternate_name); real 30 s leases P1.<br>Question: Real leases help only when several clients share a directory that is being modified (no Fs): path walks there re-LOOKUP every component. They add lease revocation to every namespace mutation; measure before P1. PROTO rates MClientLease (F-PROTO-093) P0 contract; with this choice P0 only drops unexpected lease messages. |
| [F-CAPS-046](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-046) | Directory-wide dentry validity via Fs | contract | Without real leases this is the clients' only dentry cache: kclient and libcephfs trust cached dentries and answer ENOENT locally while holding Fs on a complete directory, so the MDS must revoke Fs from every other client before any namespace change in that directory (leases 3.1.6). | Risk: A missed revoke gives other clients stale lookups or false ENOENT (silent POSIX violation). Directory caps must never include Fc/Fr/Fa/Fl. |
| [F-CAPS-067](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-067) | Gather engine (eval_gather) and cap revocation gather | redesign | Its contract is client-visible: a conflicting operation waits until every client's issued (not merely pending) conflicting bits are acked or force-revoked, with no lock-level timeout, and clients keep only what the transition allows meanwhile (lock-types 7 items 1 and 7). | Risk: Completing on pending instead of issued lets two clients hold conflicting caps. |
| [F-CACHE-008](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-008) | Inline data | tolerate | Inline data is OUT (D12), but InodeStat and MClientCaps must still carry inline_version = CEPH_INLINE_NONE, which libcephfs requires and which keeps both clients off their inline-data paths. | Always send inline_version CEPH_INLINE_NONE with an empty blob; ignore inline data in cap flushes; ignore the deprecated MDSMap inline_data flag.<br>Risk: Any other value makes clients treat files as inline (empty) data. |
| [F-CACHE-060](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-060) | Pin reasons that block eviction | redesign | The MDS can shrink only as clients release caps after RECALL_STATE; kclient, libcephfs and Ganesha (handle cache, ino_release_cb) release at their own pace. | Cap state for every inode a client holds stays valid (resident or reloadable); shrink through per-session recall; never evict a client for not shrinking.<br>Risk: A client holding caps on millions of inodes exhausts memory if cap state cannot be bounded or spilled. |
| [F-STORE-030](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-030) | Completed cap flush ids in journal and SessionMap | redesign | Clients re-send dirty cap flushes after failover and expect exactly-once application (F-CAPS-019). | Record (client, flush tid) with the cap-flush transaction, trim by oldest_flush_tid, move with the shard. |
| [F-STORE-066](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-066) | File size and mtime recovery by probing data objects | contract | When a writer is lost, size and mtime must be recovered by probing the data objects (F-CAPS-022); clients and OSDs offer no other source. | Risk: C++ marks the whole rank damaged on any probe error; contain per shard and retry. |
| [F-BG-008](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-008) | Prompt cap release for unlinked inodes (nlink 0 grant with Ls) | contract | A grant carrying nlink 0 with Ls makes the kernel prune dentry aliases and libcephfs trim the inode, so caps go away at last close and the purge is not held up by client caches (strays-and-purge 7). | Also honour the cap releases the kernel embeds in UNLINK (ceph_drop_caps_for_unlink).<br>Risk: Without it unlinked inodes stay pinned by idle client caches until cap recall, and pool space is not reclaimed. |

### Capabilities and leases: P1 (14)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CAPS-039](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-039) | Negative dentry leases | contract | Performance only: without a lease or directory Fs the kclient simply does not keep the negative dentry (kclient 3.8.3); comes with real leases. |  |
| [F-CAPS-040](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-040) | CEPH_LEASE_PRIMARY_LINK hint | contract | The kclient uses it only for async unlink (P1, D7) and for reconnect path hints; with no real leases in P0 it is never set (C++ also omits it from mask-0 records). | Risk: Without it the kclient omits regular-file paths from CLIENT_RECONNECT; reconnect must locate inodes by number. |
| [F-CAPS-042](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-042) | Lease renewal (CEPH_MDS_LEASE_RENEW) | contract | Renewal (kclient only) exists only for real leases (P1); with none issued in P0 no MClientLease arrives. | Risk: Never abort on an unexpected lease action (C++ calls ceph_abort): drop it. |
| [F-CAPS-043](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-043) | Lease revocation on dentry change | contract | Needed once real leases are issued: every lease on a dentry must be revoked and acked (kclient REVOKE_ACK, libcephfs RELEASE) or expire before its linkage changes. | Risk: libcephfs asserts the action is REVOKE; a silent client delays a dentry change up to the lease duration. |
| [F-CAPS-044](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-044) | Lease expiry and cleanup | redesign | Server-side lease state, TTL expiry and cleanup on stale or closed sessions come with real leases (P1); memory-only. |  |
| [F-CAPS-047](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-047) | Directory-operation cap bits Dc and Dr | contract | Kernel async dirops are P1 (D7); the C++ MDS itself never grants Dc/Dr today (leases 8 G1). | Risk: P0 must mask CEPH_CAP_ANY_DIR_OPS out of every directory cap: the kclient (nowsync default) and libcephfs (directories opened with open()) put these bits in wanted. |
| [F-CAPS-048](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-048) | Lock cache creation | redesign | Async dirops are P1 (D7). MDLockCache is a C++ mechanism (D16); what to redesign is the promise behind Fx\|Dc/Dr: the holder's creates and unlinks in that directory proceed without waiting on other clients. |  |
| [F-CAPS-050](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-050) | Lock cache invalidation and lifecycle | redesign | The client-visible part (Dc/Dr revoked when another client touches the directory, on shard migration, on cap loss) comes with async dirops (P1, D7). |  |
| [F-CAPS-051](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-051) | Forced auth pins for lock-cache requests | redesign | The mechanism is internal (D16), but its hazard stays with P1 async dirops: the kclient acks a Dc/Dr revoke only after its in-flight async ops are answered, so a shard quiesce or migration must keep serving those ops. |  |
| [F-CAPS-052](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-052) | Forcing async-operation flush for readers | redesign | With async dirops (P1) another client's access to the directory must first revoke Fx/Dc/Dr from the async client, whose ack waits for its in-flight ops, so readers see their results. |  |
| [F-CAPS-054](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-054) | Inode-number delegation to clients | redesign | Delegated inode ranges serve only kclient async create (P1, D7); libcephfs discards them. | P0: DELEG_INO (bit 13) is advertised and every CREATE reply carries openc_response_t with an empty delegated set (F-PROTO-087).<br>Risk: Delegated ranges must start at >= 0x1000, stay <= 8192 per session and never repeat. |
| [F-CAPS-057](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-057) | Asynchronous create (kclient) | contract | Async create is P1 (D7) and never happens against the C++ MDS today (leases 8 G1); it starts only when an MDS grants Fx\|Dc with delegated inos. | Risk: Once enabled, the reply must create exactly head.ino with caps consistent with the kclient's fabricated inode (cap_id 1, max_size = stripe_unit). |
| [F-CAPS-058](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-058) | Asynchronous unlink (kclient) | contract | Async unlink is P1 (D7); it needs Fx\|Dr plus PRIMARY_LINK from real leases, and never happens against the C++ MDS today. |  |
| [F-NS-034](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-034) | Readdir cap-acquisition throttle | redesign | Admission control against a client acquiring millions of caps through readdir protects shard memory (robustness goal); no peer requires it. | Per-session or per-shard cap-acquisition throttling; the C++ knobs need not be kept. |

### Capabilities and leases: P2 (3)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-102](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-102) | NFS-Ganesha delegations built on caps | tolerate | D12: NFS delegations are cap-based; the MDS has no delegation code, only cap revocation (P0) that tolerates acks delayed up to the delegation timeout. | Nothing delegation-specific; do not evict slow revoke responders by default (F-PROTO-048). |
| [F-CAPS-008](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-008) | Lazy I/O cap (Fl) | contract | LazyIO is P2 (D12). Fl is issued only when wanted (CEPH_FILE_MODE_LAZY), so never granting it is safe: clients keep normal coherent I/O. | Risk: Until implemented, never grant Fl. |
| [F-CAPS-065](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-065) | XSYN: rdlock while a loner keeps buffered data | redesign | Performance refinement without contract: when another client stats a file a sole writer buffers, C++ revokes Fs/Fx/Fr/Fw but lets the writer keep Fc\|Fb, so no writeback is forced; the new cap policy can add an equivalent later. |  |

| [F-CAPS-007](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-007) | Cap restrictions for old clients (connection feature bits) (legacy) | Legacy (old-clients): only connections lacking feature bits 40, 58 or 47 are restricted; the 7.3 kernel and libcephfs advertise all three. |

<a id="metadata-store"></a>

## Metadata store: WAL, directories, inode numbers

### Metadata store: WAL, directories, inode numbers: P0 (28)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-063](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-063) | Boot stages for up:starting, up:replay and up:standby-replay | redesign | Before replaying or serving, a rank must load its persistent rank- and shard-level state with a defined error mapping (damaged, restart or read-only); the C++ objects (InoTable, SessionMap, JournalPointer, PurgeQueue, SnapServer, OpenFileTable) are out by D2. | Load shards in parallel (multi-tenant replay); a validate_sessions-like check (session ino reservations vs the allocator) should damage only the affected shard (D10). |
| [F-CLU-064](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-064) | Creating a new rank (up:creating) | redesign | `fs new` and growth assign up:creating: the Rust daemon creates its rank-level state and, for rank 0, the root shard with root inode 1 (mode 0755, mds_root_ino_uid/gid, default layout on the first data pool), then requests up:active. | A new non-zero rank creates only rank-level state and receives shards by placement (D6).<br>Risk: If the creating holder dies the mon forgets the rank and the next holder creates it again: creation must be idempotent and should also wait for fencing (F-CLU-037). |
| [F-CLU-066](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-066) | up:replay: journal replay of a failed rank | redesign | D9: cold takeover replays the failed rank's durable state; with per-shard WALs shards replay in parallel (multi-tenant replay goal), rebuilding sessions, completed request ids, completed cap flushes and in-progress truncates (F-STORE-037). | No leader/peer updates, ambiguous imports or fragment operations to rebuild (D4, D5). Report MDS_ESTIMATED_REPLAY_TIME after 30 s (F-ADM-081). |
| [F-PROTO-100](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-100) | Inode number ranges clients accept | contract | The kernel refuses inode numbers in [0x100, 0x1000) and ceph-fuse aliases inode numbers >= 2^48, so the new allocator (per-shard range leases, D3) must stay inside these bounds. | The 65534-snapids-per-inode ceph-fuse limit matters only with snapshots (P1). |
| [F-NS-040](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-040) | Inode number allocation (session preallocation and InoTable) | redesign | Create-type ops need unique, stable inode numbers (NFS handles keep them); D3: per-shard range leases from an FS-wide allocator. | Session preallocation and delegation serve only P1 early replies and async create (D7), but the allocator must support them.<br>Risk: User inodes must be >= 0x1000 (root is 1; the kernel refuses inos in [0x100, 0x1000) with a warning); keep inos within 48 bits (ceph-fuse); never reuse an ino a client may still hold (NFS handles). |
| [F-CACHE-001](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-001) | Inode record field set (inode_t) | redesign | Every client-visible attribute (InodeStat, MClientCaps, vxattrs) derives from this record, so its field set and semantics are needed; the C++ inode_t encoding is OUT (D2). | Keep every field that reaches clients: ino, mode/uid/gid, nlink, rdev, times, time_warp_seq, change_attr, size, client_ranges/max_size, truncate_*, layout+pool_ns, dir_layout, dirstat, rstat, quota, pins/flags, xattrs, symlink, version, xattr_version; reserve room for fscrypt/optmetadata blobs (P2); drop backtrace_version, scrub stamps (P1), file_data_version, oldest_snap.<br>Risk: Directories must report nlink 1 and size 0 (libcephfs ceph_abort()s on any other directory nlink). |
| [F-CACHE-016](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-016) | Write-back only at journal segment expiry | redesign | Cache view of F-STORE-016/018: dirty state must reach the backing store so WALs can be truncated and clean state evicted. | `flush journal` equivalent is P0 (F-STORE-020); `flush_path` is P2; C++ segment-count triggers are internal. |
| [F-CACHE-023](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-023) | Directory completeness and authoritative negative lookups | redesign | A wrong ENOENT or an incomplete readdir is a POSIX violation visible to every client. | Answer misses only from a complete directory index (in memory, or a keyed read of the store); C++ completeness flags are internal. |
| [F-STORE-007](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-007) | Per-rank write-ahead metadata journal with one submission order | redesign | D7: a mutation is safe only after durable commit; the C++ single ordered journal per rank couples all subvolumes (an fsync flushes everyone's events). | Per-shard WAL stream(s) in RADOS: one atomic record per transaction, concurrent submission, ordering by transaction dependencies, acknowledgement only after commit.<br>Question: Stream granularity (one per shard or several) and how the D4 cross-shard operations order their records. |
| [F-STORE-008](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-008) | Group commit: flush and wait_for_safe | redesign | Group commit carries synchronous NFS writes and fsync throughput (D7, use case 3); in C++ one flush writes every subvolume's buffered events. | Batch concurrent commits per shard stream without waiting for an explicit flush, so FLUSH_MDLOG and sync cap flushes cost nothing extra and no tenant waits for another. |
| [F-STORE-009](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-009) | Periodic journal flush on tick | contract | The kernel assumes un-flushed journal entries become durable within about 5 s (mds_tick_interval) when it waits for safe replies after fsync. | Mechanism free: commit-as-soon-as-possible group commit meets the bound; it must also hold while the monitor link is laggy (the C++ tick then skips its flush). |
| [F-STORE-016](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-016) | Log segments as units of lazy writeback (segment expiry) | redesign | WALs must be truncatable and the cache shrinkable, so dirty state must be checkpointed to the backing store; in C++ one slow object holds back every subvolume's trimming. | Per-shard checkpoint of directory entries, inodes, allocator and session state that records the WAL position it covers; only clean state is evicted. |
| [F-STORE-018](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-018) | Background journal trimming with throttle | redesign | Unbounded WAL growth lengthens replay and fills the metadata pool, so trimming must run in the background for every shard. | Size or age budget per shard WAL and an MDS_TRIM-equivalent warning when behind; C++ segment counts and decay throttle are not needed.<br>Risk: While MDS_TRIM is reported the mon refuses Rook's unconfirmed `mds fail`/`fs fail`; raise it only when a WAL is really behind. |
| [F-STORE-036](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-036) | End-of-journal probe and torn-tail handling | redesign | After a crash the true end of each WAL must be found and a torn last record discarded, never acknowledged. | Self-describing records (sequence, length, checksum) instead of the C++ prezero-and-probe scheme. |
| [F-STORE-037](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-037) | Journal replay pipeline | redesign | Takeover replays the WAL to rebuild state; multi-tenant journal replay is a motivating use case, while C++ replays one ordered journal per rank under mds_lock. | Per-shard replay from the last checkpoint, in parallel and idempotent against checkpointed state; one shard's slow or damaged WAL must not hold back the others. |
| [F-STORE-045](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-045) | Metadata object naming and placement | redesign | The new on-disk format needs its own object naming in the metadata pool (D2); C++ names are read only by C++ tools. | Use names or a dedicated RADOS namespace that cannot collide with mgr, cephfs-mirror or ceph-csi objects (F-STORE-102); per-shard prefixes allow shard teardown; byte-swapped frag decode is out. |
| [F-STORE-046](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-046) | Special inode numbers and per-rank ranges | subset | The kernel and libcephfs reject [0x100,0x1000), treat 1 as the mount root and 3 as the global realm, and ceph-fuse needs numbers below 2^48 (F-PROTO-100). | Keep: root 1, 3 reserved, no user number below 0x1000, every number below 2^48; drop: per-rank 2^40 ranges, the 0x200-0x600 C++ object numbers, .ceph (2) and lost+found (4).<br>Risk: Kernel lookups fail with -EREMOTEIO on [0x100,0x1000); ceph-fuse builds inode numbers as (stag << 48) \| ino. |
| [F-STORE-047](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-047) | Dirfrag omap object and fnode header | redesign | Directory contents and per-directory stats must persist (D2 purpose), and with one client-visible frag (D5) large directories must still scale. | Per-directory entry index partitioned internally across RADOS objects (OSD LARGE_OMAP_OBJECTS at 200000 keys or 1 GiB), iterable in (hash24, name) order for readdir (F-CACHE-077), with a dirstat/rstat header.<br>Risk: C++ users keep millions of entries per directory through fragmentation. |
| [F-STORE-049](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-049) | Primary dentry value with embedded inode (InodeStore) | redesign | Every inode's persistent state (F-CACHE-001 fields, symlink target, user and ACL xattrs, realm node with quota and subvolume flags) must be stored; embedding it in the primary dentry is a C++ choice. | Store it so the inode-number index can find it (F-STORE-062); the legacy 'I' marker is out. |
| [F-STORE-050](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-050) | Remote (hard link) dentry value | redesign | Hard links within a shard are P0 (D14): the extra names store target ino and d_type. | alternate_name comes with fscrypt/charmap (P2); legacy 'L' decode is out. |
| [F-STORE-053](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-053) | Base inode objects (.inode) | redesign | Inodes without a parent dentry in their own shard need a home: the file-system root in the root shard and each shard's root inode (D5 boundary). | The legacy 'inode' xattr fallback is out.<br>Question: Is a shard-root inode owned by its shard (C++ base-inode style) with a copy in the parent shard (F-CACHE-055)? |
| [F-STORE-062](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-062) | Lookup by inode number through backtraces (open_ino storage path) | redesign | NFS handles (kernel nfsd, Ganesha) and libcephfs ll_lookup_inode carry only the 64-bit number and must resolve after restarts and cache trimming. | Durable ino -> (shard, location, primary parent dirino and name) index updated in the same transaction as create/link/unlink/rename, serving LOOKUPINO, LOOKUPPARENT and LOOKUPNAME.<br>Risk: C++ writes backtraces lazily at segment expiry; a lazy index would leave new files unresolvable by number. |
| [F-STORE-064](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-064) | Data object naming and striping shared with clients | contract | Clients read and write data objects `<ino hex>.<objno %08x>` in (pool, namespace) with the Striper mapping themselves, so the inode numbers and layouts the MDS hands out define that namespace. | Risk: Purge must remove object 0 in the file's pool and namespace even for empty files (the client pool-permission probe creates it). |
| [F-STORE-069](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-069) | InoTable object and per-rank inode ranges | redesign | Inode numbers must be unique per file system and durable before use; D3 asks for per-shard range leases from a file-system-wide allocator. | Durable FS-wide allocator issuing never-reused ranges inside [0x1000, 2^48); exhaustion returns ENOSPC instead of aborting (C++ asserts).<br>Question: Encode the shard in the range lease for cheap ino -> shard routing, and decide how inodes that join a new shard keep resolving (see F-CACHE-047). |
| [F-STORE-070](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-070) | Inode number allocation in create paths | redesign | Create-type ops need a number allocated and journaled atomically with the create and returned again on retry. | Allocate from the shard's leased ranges; a client-supplied head.ino is honoured only from that session's preallocated or delegated set (P1 async create and early-reply replay) and otherwise validated, never trusted.<br>Risk: An unexpected head.ino crashes the C++ MDS (strict-erase assert). |
| [F-STORE-073](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-073) | Inode numbers are never reused after deletion | contract | NFS handles carry no generation number and data-object names derive from the number, so a deleted inode's number must never be reused. | Risk: With range leases, never re-issue a range, including those of deleted shards. |
| [F-STORE-084](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-084) | OpenFileTable tracking and Anchor records | redesign | After failover the MDS must find inodes clients had open for write, to recover sizes of writers that do not reconnect (F-CAPS-022) and to resolve reconnected caps quickly. | A per-shard persisted set of inodes with client write ranges or open caps (or recovery triggered when an inode with stale client_ranges is first loaded); C++ anchors and the OFT format are out. |
| [F-STORE-102](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-102) | Metadata pool shared with mgr-owned objects | contract | mgr snap_schedule (`snap_db_v0.db.*`), mgr mirroring and cephfs-mirror (`cephfs_mirror*`, watch/notify) and Rook's ceph-csi (namespace csi) keep objects in the metadata pool. | Never create, modify, delete or report as damage any object outside the MDS's own names.<br>Risk: Collisions or deletions break snapshot schedules, mirroring and CSI provisioning. |

### Metadata store: WAL, directories, inode numbers: P1 (3)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CAPS-053](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-053) | Per-session inode-number preallocation | redesign | Per-session preallocated numbers exist for early create replies and delegation, both P1 (D7); P0 allocates from per-shard range leases (D3). D7 asks to design these reservations now. | SessionMap and InoTable formats out (D2).<br>Risk: C++ asserts on a client-named ino it does not own (leases 8 G4); never trust head.ino blindly. |
| [F-STORE-071](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-071) | Session inode preallocation and refill | redesign | Per-session preallocation is needed only for async-create delegation and early-reply replays (D7, P1), not for P0 throughput once shards hold range leases. |  |
| [F-STORE-072](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-072) | Release of preallocated and delegated numbers at session close (EPurged) | redesign | Release and purge of delegated numbers comes with async create (D7). | Never re-issue a number once delegated, or purge its object 0 in the right pool and namespace (C++ FIXME, mds-tables G23). |
