| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-STORE-001](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-001) | Journal pointer object (front/back journal inode) | C++ journal pointer format (D2), read only by cephfs-journal-tool and QA; the new WAL locates its per-shard streams by its own naming. |
| [F-STORE-002](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-002) | Journaler header object | C++ Journaler header (D2); WAL positions belong to the new WAL design (F-STORE-007, F-STORE-036). |
| [F-STORE-003](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-003) | Striped journal byte stream and its RADOS objects | Striped byte-stream layout of the C++ journal (D2). |
| [F-STORE-004](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-004) | RESILIENT journal entry framing | C++ entry framing read by cephfs-journal-tool (D2); the new WAL should frame records with length, sequence and checksum (C++ has no checksum). |
| [F-STORE-005](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-005) | LogEvent envelope and event type ids | C++ event envelope and type ids, read only by C++ tools (D2). |
| [F-STORE-006](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-006) | Legacy-format journal rewrite through the back pointer (legacy) | Legacy (old-data) rewrite of format-0 journals; the new MDS never opens C++ journals (D1, D2). |
| [F-STORE-013](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-013) | Journal prezeroing ahead of the write position | Prezeroing exists only for the C++ probe-based end-of-journal detection; the new WAL finds its end from record sequence and checksums (F-STORE-036). |
| [F-STORE-014](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-014) | Journal header refresh | C++ header refresh policy (D2). |
| [F-STORE-017](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-017) | Segment boundaries and rotation policy | The segment structure of the C++ journal and the stray rotation coupled to it are internal (D2). |
| [F-STORE-019](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-019) | Major-group trimming and journal object deletion | Major-group trimming and Journaler object deletion are C++ format details. |
| [F-STORE-022](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-022) | EMetaBlob after-image container | C++ after-image container (D2); the new WAL record format is free (logical records avoid journaling whole inodes plus ancestors). |
| [F-STORE-023](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-023) | EMetaBlob path context and ancestor images | Ancestor path context serves C++ replay and eager rstat propagation; D4 makes ancestor aggregation a separate, eventually consistent mechanism. |
| [F-STORE-025](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-025) | ESubtreeMap: major boundary with the subtree map | ESubtreeMap anchors replay of a rank-wide journal; per-shard WALs carry their own identity and the shard map replaces subtree claims (F-CACHE-036). |
| [F-STORE-026](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-026) | EUpdate: generic mutation record | C++ generic event type (D2); journaling of mutations is F-STORE-007 and session force-open on cap import is part of F-STORE-033. |
| [F-STORE-027](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-027) | EOpen: open-file journaling | EOpen keeps open inodes in the C++ journal; the outcome is provided by per-shard open-file tracking (F-STORE-084). |
| [F-STORE-031](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-031) | Destroyed inode records | Bookkeeping for C++ replay and OpenFileTable prefetch; the new store simply must not resurrect purged inodes. |
| [F-STORE-035](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-035) | Journal open and recovery thread | Pointer, back-journal and format checks of the C++ journal (D2); opening and recovering a shard WAL is F-STORE-036/037, error policy F-STORE-040, fencing reaction F-STORE-095. |
| [F-STORE-038](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-038) | Cache reconstruction from after-images | Replay of C++ after-images (D2); covered by F-STORE-037. |
| [F-STORE-039](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-039) | Version-gated redo of table operations | Version-gated redo of C++ tables; the new checkpoint records its WAL position so replay is idempotent (F-STORE-016, F-STORE-037). |
| [F-STORE-041](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-041) | Post-replay session and InoTable consistency check | Consistency assertion between the C++ SessionMap and InoTable. |
| [F-STORE-048](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-048) | Dentry omap keys, including snapshotted dentries | C++ omap key format (D2); snapshotted dentry versions are F-CACHE-011 (P1) and names are stored bytewise as sent (F-CACHE-077). |
| [F-STORE-051](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-051) | Dirfrag fetch from RADOS | C++ dirfrag read path; reading directory entries is part of F-STORE-047 and damage on load is F-BG-053. |
| [F-STORE-052](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-052) | Dirfrag commit to RADOS | C++ dirfrag write-back ops; checkpoint writes are F-STORE-016 and F-STORE-047. |
| [F-STORE-057](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-057) | Legacy on-disk formats still decoded | Legacy C++ formats (D1, D2); the new MDS reads none of them. |
| [F-STORE-058](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-058) | Backtrace parent xattr on object 0 | No current peer reads backtraces (clients never do; only C++ tools, OUT by D2); lookup by number moves to an index (F-STORE-062). |
| [F-STORE-059](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-059) | Layout and symlink xattrs on object 0 | Read only by cephfs-data-scan (D2). |
| [F-STORE-060](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-060) | Backtrace copies in the default data pool and old pools | Backtrace copies serve C++ open_ino and tools only; no peer reads them. |
| [F-STORE-061](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-061) | Directory backtrace verification on fetch | Self-check of C++ directory backtraces. |
| [F-STORE-068](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-068) | MDSTable: versioned single-object table persistence | C++ whole-object table persistence; allocator, session and shard-map state get new formats (F-STORE-069, F-STORE-075, F-CACHE-036). |
| [F-STORE-074](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-074) | InoTable replay, gap repair and the taken-number guard | Repairs for C++ InoTable replay gaps; the new allocator must be crash-consistent by construction. |
| [F-STORE-085](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-085) | OpenFileTable objects and commit with redo journal | C++ OpenFileTable format (D2). |
| [F-STORE-086](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-086) | OpenFileTable load and redo-journal recovery | C++ OpenFileTable load and redo journal (D2). |
| [F-STORE-087](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-087) | OpenFileTable-gated segment expiry | Couples C++ segment expiry to OpenFileTable commits. |
| [F-STORE-088](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-088) | EOpen suppression (should_log_open) | C++ EOpen suppression. |
| [F-STORE-089](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-089) | OpenFileTable prefetch during rejoin | Prefetch from the C++ OpenFileTable; reconnect resolves inodes through the inode-number index (F-CACHE-047) and the open-file set (F-STORE-084). |
| [F-ADM-119](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-119) | Offline journal and table tools (cephfs-journal-tool, cephfs-table-tool) | D2: bound to the C++ journal and table encodings; cannot operate on the new on-disk format. |
| [F-ADM-120](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-120) | Offline data and metadata repair tools (cephfs-data-scan, cephfs-meta-injection, first-damage.py) | D2: cephfs-data-scan, cephfs-meta-injection and first-damage.py parse C++ backtrace, dirfrag omap and snaptable encodings. |

<a id="lifecycle"></a>

## Strays, purge, truncation and damage

### Strays, purge, truncation and damage: P0 (31)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-097](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-097) | Activation (recovery_done and active_start) | redesign | On becoming active the rank must start post-recovery work (truncates, size recovery, stray/purge processing), reissue caps and release every request that waited; the C++ populate_mydir and stray-directory layout are out. | The dead-code branch of populate_mydir is legacy. |
| [F-CLU-100](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-100) | Re-executing interrupted truncates after recovery | redesign | A shrinking truncate whose metadata is durable must finish trimming the data objects after a failover (idempotent via truncate_seq), otherwise stale data reappears beyond EOF (F-STORE-065). |  |
| [F-CLU-107](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-107) | Write error policy | subset | RADOS write errors need defined outcomes: -EBLOCKLISTED means fenced (restart, F-CLU-105); other metadata or journal write errors go read-only or damaged, preferably per shard (D10). | Keep -EBLOCKLISTED -> restart; mds_action_on_write_error semantics may be redesigned per shard. |
| [F-NS-048](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-048) | Unlinked-but-open inodes stay usable until the last cap is released | redesign | D14 unlink-while-open: the inode stays usable until the last cap goes, then its objects are purged; D3 makes strays and the purge queue per shard. | Snapshot-aware stray retention (past-parent snapshots) is P1. |
| [F-CACHE-006](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-006) | Truncation state fields | contract | Clients pass truncate_seq/truncate_size on every OSD data op and OSDs apply TRIMTRUNC semantics, so truncation ordering between MDS, clients and OSDs rests on these fields. | Risk: New inodes must start with truncate_seq 1 and truncate_size -1 (OSD 'no truncation'; the kernel fabricates the same values for async creates); other values let OSDs truncate client data. |
| [F-CACHE-018](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-018) | Commit error handling and read-only mode | redesign | Write-back failures must never be silently acknowledged; the outcome (respawn when blocklisted, otherwise read-only with FORCE_RO and MDS_READ_ONLY) is the policy of F-STORE-100. | Per-shard containment preferred (D10); the stray ENOENT special case is C++ internal. |
| [F-STORE-015](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-015) | Journal write-error policy (damaged or respawn) | redesign | A WAL write failure must stop acknowledgements and be visible to the monitor and operator; D10 asks for containment instead of taking every tenant down. | -EBLOCKLISTED abandons the rank (F-STORE-095); other errors stop the affected shard with health and cluster-log reporting; rank-level down:damaged (cleared by `ceph mds repaired`) only for rank-wide state. |
| [F-STORE-040](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-040) | Replay error policy and the damaged rank | redesign | D10: unreadable or inconsistent metadata must be contained (EIO, damaged shard) while the rest of the rank keeps serving. | A corrupt WAL record or checkpoint damages only its shard; never assert on decoded data (C++ aborts on trailing bytes and subtree mismatches); rank down:damaged only when rank-wide state is unreadable; dev skip options are out.<br>Question: How a damaged shard is reported (MDS_DAMAGE, damage ls) and repaired, given that the monitor only knows rank states. |
| [F-STORE-054](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-054) | Per-rank mdsdir and stray directories | redesign | Unlink-while-open and hard-link primaries need a hidden holding area (D14), and D3 makes stray handling per shard. | Per-shard stray area instead of ~mdsN/stray0-9 and stray rotation; unlinked inodes leave the subvolume's rstat and quota at unlink as in C++; the legacy 'stray' name is out. |
| [F-STORE-065](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-065) | Data-object truncation (truncate start/finish and TRIMTRUNC) | contract | POSIX truncate: once the truncate is durable clients get CEPH_CAP_OP_TRUNC and the MDS trims objects with TRIMTRUNC and the new truncate_seq, which OSD semantics make idempotent and safe against racing client I/O. | Restart after failover (F-CLU-100); the fscrypt last-block write is P2.<br>Risk: Never abort on OSD errors (C++ asserts on single-object truncate results). |
| [F-STORE-100](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-100) | Metadata write-error policy and read-only mode | redesign | D10: backing-store write failures must be contained and never acknowledged; the kernel marks sessions read-only on FORCE_RO and the mon shows MDS_READ_ONLY. | -EBLOCKLISTED respawns; otherwise read-only (EROFS for writes, write caps revoked, FORCE_RO, MDS_READ_ONLY), ideally per shard; no aborts on RADOS errors (C++ asserts on several).<br>Question: Per-shard read-only? FORCE_RO is per session (the kernel marks the whole session read-only), so a per-shard mode must rely on EROFS and write-cap revocation. |
| [F-BG-001](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-001) | Per-rank stray directories (~mdsN/stray0..stray9) | redesign | Unlink-while-open (D14) needs a durable home for unlinked but still referenced inodes; per D3 it is a per-shard stray area, not ten per-rank ~mdsN/strayK dirfrags (C++ layout OUT, D2). | Legacy 'stray' dentry-name fallback is out. Internal objects are never exposed to clients (their inos stay unaddressable, ESTALE).<br>Risk: The stray area must sit outside the shard root's rstat and quota tree, otherwise unlink stops freeing quota and ceph.dir.rbytes (F-BG-002). |
| [F-BG-002](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-002) | Deferred deletion: unlink, rmdir and rename-over move the inode to a stray | redesign | POSIX unlink/rmdir/rename-over must return without deleting data while openers keep using the inode (F-NS-048); rstat and quota of the old parent drop at unlink, pool usage only after purge (strays-and-purge 7). | Keep: reply after durable commit (D7) without waiting for purge; parent rstat/quota decrement at unlink time; remember the prior path (or shard) for MDS auth-cap path checks of open unlinked files (stray_prior_path).<br>Risk: Path-restricted clients (CSI subvolume mounts, Ganesha exports) lose access to their open unlinked files if the prior path is not kept for auth checks.<br>Question: When mgr/volumes removes a subvolume (rename into /volumes/_deleting, rmtree, rmdir of the shard root), where do the shard's remaining strays (files still open) and pending purge items go: keep a zombie shard until drained, or move them to the root shard? |
| [F-BG-006](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-006) | Purge eligibility decision (eval_stray) | redesign | POSIX unlink-while-open: data may be purged only when no client holds caps or leases on the inode, including stale sessions not yet evicted, and no file-size recovery is pending (F-NS-048). | P0 conditions: caps of any session (stale included), leases, pending size recovery, in-flight internal references. Snapshot conditions (past-parent snaps, old_inodes) come with P1 snapshots (F-BG-009).<br>Risk: Purging while a stale or reconnecting session still holds caps loses that client's data. |
| [F-BG-007](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-007) | Stray re-evaluation triggers (NOTIFYREF hooks and delayed list) | redesign | The purge decision must re-run when the last blocker goes away (cap release, session close or eviction, lease expiry); NOTIFYREF hooks and the delayed list are the C++ mechanism (D16). | C++ misses snapshot deletion and migrated strays as triggers; the new trigger set should not.<br>Risk: Without event-driven re-evaluation space is reclaimed only by scans; some C++ strays wait for cache pressure or a restart. |
| [F-BG-011](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-011) | Hard-link reintegration (silent link-merge rename) | redesign | Hard links within a shard are P0 (D14): removing the primary name must leave the inode reachable through its other links, with nlink and ctime from the unlink and no further visible change. | Links never cross shards (EXDEV, D4), so any relink is shard-local; if inodes are stored by ino rather than embedded in a primary dentry, reintegration disappears.<br>Risk: C++ leaks the stray when its internal rename is lost (no timeout) and finds remote links only through cached dentries; the redesign must not depend on either. |
| [F-BG-013](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-013) | PurgeQueue journal of PurgeItems | redesign | Purge needs a durable per-shard queue of pending object deletions (D3) that decouples fast unlinks from slow RADOS deletes; the Journaler PurgeItem format and cephfs-journal-tool support are OUT (D2). | Legacy PurgeItem v1 decoding and the 'assuming this is an upgrade' recreation are out. |
| [F-BG-014](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-014) | Durable deletion intent before stray metadata removal | redesign | The deletion intent must be durable before the stray record is removed, or a crash leaks the file's RADOS objects with nothing left to find them (cephfs-data-scan is OUT, D2). | Risk: Never recycle purged inode numbers: kernel NFS handles carry only the ino (no generation, mds-tables 7), so reuse makes stale handles resolve to another file. |
| [F-BG-015](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-015) | File data and backtrace object deletion (PURGE_FILE) | subset | Data objects are named and striped by the client-visible layout (<ino hex>.%08x in the layout pool and namespace), so purge must delete exactly the objects clients may have written. | Delete objects 0..N-1 with N from max(size, largest client writable range, max_size_ever) and the file's striping, in the layout pool/namespace; -ENOENT counts as done; ops carry FULL_FORCE so purge works on full pools; -EBLOCKLISTED stops the daemon. Realm SnapContext with P1 snapshots; backtrace and old-pool object-0 deletes only if the new MDS writes such objects.<br>Risk: Using size alone leaks objects a crashed writer created up to its granted max_size; without FULL_FORCE nobody can free space on a full cluster. |
| [F-BG-016](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-016) | Directory object deletion (PURGE_DIR) | redesign | Whatever per-directory RADOS objects the new format creates must be deleted when an empty unlinked directory is purged, or metadata-pool objects leak with every rmdir; C++ dirfrag/backtrace object names are OUT (D2). |  |
| [F-BG-017](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-017) | Purge throttling and pacing | redesign | mgr/volumes trash purge turns subvolume removal into mass unlinks; unthrottled deletes would overload OSDs for every tenant, so purge needs a budget scaled to OSD capacity and fair across shards (D3 isolation). | Honouring mds_max_purge_files (0 pauses) and mds_max_purge_ops[_per_pg] is desirable for operator runbooks; mechanism free.<br>Question: Purge budget per rank split fairly across its shards, or cluster-wide? C++ divides PGs by max_mds and has no per-subvolume fairness. |
| [F-BG-019](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-019) | Purge recovery and at-least-once execution | redesign | Pending purges must resume after restart or failover without operator action (D9); at-least-once execution with idempotent deletes (-ENOENT tolerated) is the outcome to keep. | Standby-replay specifics are P1 (D9).<br>Risk: After replay an inode whose purge was already durable must not become reachable by LOOKUPINO again (C++ gap). |
| [F-BG-020](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-020) | PurgeQueue error handling (read-only queue) | redesign | Purge-queue storage or decode errors must be contained to the shard (D10) instead of forcing the whole rank read-only, and -EBLOCKLISTED must stop the daemon (fencing). | Retry or report OSD delete errors other than ENOENT instead of silently leaking objects; mds_action_on_write_error semantics are free.<br>Risk: C++ can self-deadlock pushing to a read-only queue under mds_lock; do not copy the path. |
| [F-BG-021](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-021) | Activation stray scan | redesign | After recovery each shard must find its unpurged strays and re-run the purge decision; C++ loads every stray of the rank under mds_lock at activation. | Per shard, incremental, not blocking activation.<br>Risk: Never evaluate strays before the shard's reconnect phase has ended: reconnecting clients may still hold unlinked files open (C++ waits until after rejoin). |
| [F-BG-023](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-023) | Rank stop: drain the PurgeQueue | redesign | No pending purge may be lost when a rank stops (D6); with per-shard queues the items move with their shard (F-BG-022) and the new owner resumes them at least once (F-BG-019), so the C++ full drain before down:stopped is not needed. | Hand off rather than drain: finish or abandon in-flight deletes (idempotent), move queued items with the shard; any rank-level purge work (e.g. delegated inos, F-STORE-072) must finish or move first. The clog progress line is optional; peers only wait for down:stopped.<br>Risk: A full drain can keep a max_mds reduction (cephadm and Rook upgrades) waiting for hours after a large subvolume deletion.<br>Question: F-CLU-102 says 'drain purge work' before down:stopped; confirm that handing off the per-shard queues is enough. |
| [F-BG-052](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-052) | DamageTable registry | redesign | Damage containment is P0 (D10): known-bad metadata must be recorded so requests fail fast with EIO, MDS_DAMAGE is raised and operators can list and clear entries; keep the registry per shard. | UNINLINE_FILE entries are legacy. Registry overflow must not take the rank down (C++ makes every report fatal past mds_damage_table_max_entries).<br>Risk: MDS_DAMAGE is sent at HEALTH_ERR: Rook stops reconciling while it is raised and the mon refuses unconfirmed 'fs set max_mds' while any MDS reports a health metric.<br>Question: Report one tenant's shard damage at HEALTH_ERR (C++, alerts) or WARN so it does not stall Rook for all tenants (same question as F-ADM-078)? |
| [F-BG-053](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-053) | Dirfrag load damage detection | redesign | Metadata that is missing or does not decode must be recorded as damage and fail only the affected requests with EIO (D10), never assert or take the rank down. | Risk: C++ asserts on unexpected read errors and malformed omap keys; a Rust panic on bad on-disk data would stop every shard of the rank. |
| [F-BG-056](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-056) | Duplicate primary inode detection on dirfrag load | redesign | An ino found under two primary records is inconsistent metadata; contain it as damage (D10) instead of silently hiding a name as C++ does (clog only). | Part of F-BG-053's load-time validation; impossible by construction if inodes are stored by ino. |
| [F-BG-057](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-057) | Fatal damage takes the rank down:damaged | redesign | D10 prefers per-shard isolation: damage to one shard's essential structures (WAL, stray area, purge queue, root) should fence only that shard, keeping down:damaged and 'ceph mds repaired' for rank-level damage. | Registry overflow is not fatal.<br>Risk: A down:damaged rank takes all its shards offline until 'ceph mds repaired'.<br>Question: How does an operator mark a single damaged shard repaired? 'ceph mds repaired' is per rank, so a per-shard tell command is needed. |
| [F-BG-060](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-060) | EIO for requests that touch known damage | contract | D10: requests whose path crosses known damage fail with EIO while the rest of the rank and shard keeps serving; clients pass EIO through unchanged. |  |
| [F-BG-061](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-061) | readdir behaviour on damaged directories | redesign | C++ lists a damaged directory as complete and (normally) empty without error; under D10 a damaged listing should fail with EIO, while individually undecodable entries may be skipped. | Risk: Presenting a damaged directory as empty invites rmdir or rm -rf to succeed and lose the remaining entries.<br>Question: Confirm EIO for readdir of a damaged directory instead of the C++ silent partial listing. |

### Strays, purge, truncation and damage: P1 (14)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-101](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-101) | Purging orphaned preallocated and delegated inodes | redesign | Orphaned objects of lost creates exist only when clients write before the create is durable (async creates with delegated inos, or unsafe replies), both P1 (D7). | With P1 async dirops: purge unused delegated/preallocated inos of sessions that did not come back (all objects, not only object 0 as in C++). |
| [F-SNAP-041](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-041) | Stat mismatch detection and in-place repair | redesign | Stat verification and repair belong to online scrub and repair of the new format (D10: P1). | Risk: Never assert on a stat mismatch or negative count in production (C++ asserts under debug options); clamp and report. |
| [F-BG-009](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-009) | Snapshot-aware purge decision (past-parent snaps, realm SnapContext) | redesign | Keeping strays that snapshots still need and deleting with the realm SnapContext (so OSDs keep clones) comes with snapshots (D8). | Risk: P0 purges with an empty SnapContext, which is safe only while MKSNAP is refused (D8).<br>Question: C++ never re-evaluates strays when a snapshot is deleted; choose that trigger in the P1 snapshot design. |
| [F-BG-010](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-010) | Truncate-only purge for multiversion files (TRUNCATE_FILE) | redesign | Truncate-only purge of multiversion files exists only for files that snapshots still reference (D8). |  |
| [F-BG-031](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-031) | Scrub traversal order and scheduling (ScrubStack) | redesign | Carrier of the P1 online scrub engine (D10): a per-shard walk that validates what the new format stores. | Avoid the C++ breadth-first walk that loads and pins whole trees; the uninline step is legacy (inline data OUT, D12). |
| [F-BG-032](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-032) | Scrub concurrency throttle | redesign | Scrub work must be bounded so it does not starve client traffic of the shard's tenants; mds_max_scrub_ops_in_progress is the C++ knob. | Keeping the option name is optional. |
| [F-BG-039](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-039) | Backtrace validation | redesign | Lookup by inode number (NFS handles, hard links) depends on correct ino-to-location records; the P1 scrub checks whatever the new format uses, backtraces or an index (D2, D10). |  |
| [F-BG-040](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-040) | Backtrace repair | redesign | P1 repair of the new format's ino-lookup records (D10); the C++ rewrite through dirty_parent is format-specific. |  |
| [F-BG-042](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-042) | InoTable "inode wrongly marked free" check and repair | redesign | Inode numbers must never be handed out twice; the P1 scrub should check in-use inos against the fs-wide allocator and per-shard range leases (D3) instead of a per-rank InoTable. |  |
| [F-BG-044](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-044) | Directory dirstat/rstat validation and repair | redesign | Directory sizes, ceph.dir.r* vxattrs and client-side quota enforcement read dirstat/rstat; the P1 scrub must verify and repair them, including the eventually consistent aggregation across shard boundaries (D4). |  |
| [F-BG-047](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-047) | Hard-link resolvability check | redesign | Hard links within a shard (D14) must resolve by ino; the P1 scrub verifies that link targets exist and link counts match. |  |
| [F-BG-049](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-049) | Scrub work forwarding to authoritative ranks (MMDSScrub) | redesign | A scrub started on rank 0 for a path that spans shards on several ranks must reach the owning ranks; whole-shard ownership (D3) turns this into per-shard dispatch with a free protocol. |  |
| [F-BG-050](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-050) | Scrub completion protocol (MMDSScrubStats epochs) | redesign | scrub status must know when a multi-rank scrub has finished; the MMDSScrubStats epoch protocol, with its liveness gaps, is free to replace. | MMDSScrubStats v1 decoding and the uninline payload are legacy. |
| [F-BG-051](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-051) | Scrub behaviour on rank failure and restart | redesign | Scrubs need defined behaviour on failover; with shards moving between ranks a scrub could follow its shard instead of being aborted as in C++. |  |

### Strays, purge, truncation and damage: P2 (1)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-BG-033](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-033) | Incremental scrub and the force option | redesign | Skip-unchanged (incremental) scrub is an optimisation; full scrubs, with 'force' accepted as a no-op, suffice until there is demand. |  |

### Strays, purge, truncation and damage: OUT (5)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-STORE-063](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-063) | Scrub tag xattr on object 0 | C++ scrub tags are OUT unless a peer reads them (D10); only cephfs-data-scan does. |
| [F-STORE-067](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-067) | Inline data uninlining and the inline_version xattr (legacy) | Legacy: inline data is OUT (D12) and exists only under the deprecated flag. |
| [F-BG-004](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-004) | ENOSPC on unlink when the current stray dirfrag is full | ENOSPC on unlink is an artifact of the shared C++ stray dirfrag layout, not a POSIX unlink error; no peer depends on receiving it. |
| [F-BG-029](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-029) | Scrub tags and the scrub_tag object xattr | The scrub_tag xattr on first objects only serves cephfs-data-scan --filter-tag (OUT, D2/D10); tags as scrub names live on in F-BG-028/F-BG-037. |
| [F-BG-048](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-048) | Data uninlining during scrub (legacy) | Legacy: inline data is deprecated and OUT (D12), and the new MDS never reads inodes written with inline data. |

## Subvolumes as shards, quotas, statistics, layouts, auth caps

### Subvolumes as shards, quotas, statistics, layouts, auth caps: P0 (46)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-011](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-011) | Data pool list of a file system | contract | The MDS must validate layout pools (create with layout, setlayout, setdirlayout) against the MDSMap data_pools after waiting for the client's map epoch; clients use the same list for statfs and layout ioctls. | Risk: Validating against a stale map gives EINVAL for a pool just added with `fs add_data_pool` (F-CLU-060). |
| [F-PROTO-095](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-095) | MClientQuota encoding | contract | Quota enforcement is client side (the MDS never returns EDQUOT), so clients holding caps on a quota realm inode need MClientQuota pushes of usage and limits; subvolume quotas are P0. | The noquota cap state (connections without MDS_QUOTA) is out.<br>Risk: No trailing bytes (libcephfs asserts); counted in push_seq. |
| [F-NS-021](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-021) | Permission checking split: clients enforce POSIX, the MDS enforces auth caps | contract | Clients enforce mode bits, ACLs, sticky bit and ownership; the MDS enforces MDS auth caps against client-asserted ids at fixed points (F-ADM-018). Keeping this split is a documented departure (D14). | Legacy EIO branch for clients without FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2 is OUT. |
| [F-NS-042](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-042) | File layout selection and validation at create | contract | New files take the nearest ancestor layout or the default; mgr/volumes sets pool and pool_namespace layouts on subvolume dirs and libcephfs can pass a layout in CREATE; invalid layouts -> EINVAL. | Data pools must belong to the file system (MDSMap); wait for the client's osdmap epoch for newly named pools. |
| [F-NS-071](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-071) | Virtual xattr set/remove path (common rules, p flag) | subset | mgr/volumes, Rook and admins change layout, quota, subvolume and pin policies through ceph.* setxattr, gated only by the MDS-cap p flag (MAY_SET_VXATTR, not MAY_WRITE). | P0 names: ceph.dir.layout*, ceph.file.layout*, ceph.quota*, ceph.dir.subvolume, ceph.dir.pin, ceph.dir.pin.distributed; ceph.dir.pin.random tolerate (validate EDOM/EINVAL, store, ignore; D12). P1: ceph.dir.subvolume.snaps.visible. P2: ceph.dir.charmap/casesensitive/normalization/encoding, ceph.quiesce.block (refused until then). CREATE/REPLACE flags ignored; realm-changing names reply after commit and notify realm changes.<br>Risk: Recover uncached inodes instead of returning ESTALE (C++ gap, attributes 8 item 14). |
| [F-NS-072](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-072) | Layout virtual xattrs (ceph.dir.layout*, ceph.file.layout*) | contract | mgr/volumes sets ceph.dir.layout.pool/pool_namespace and walks ancestors up to / for ceph.dir.layout.pool, so the root must carry a layout; clients compute composite layout names from InodeStat and GETVXATTR serves pool_name, pool_id and json. | Legacy EIO for clients without FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2 OUT.<br>Risk: C++ aborts when GETVXATTR reaches the root without a layout: create the root with the default layout and never crash. |
| [F-NS-073](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-073) | GETVXATTR op | subset | kclient and libcephfs send GETVXATTR for any ceph.* name missing from their tables and only if CEPHFS_FEATURE_OP_GETVXATTR is advertised; mgr/volumes reads ceph.dir.subvolume and layout json through it. | Serve ceph.dir.subvolume ('0'/'1'), ceph.{dir,file}.layout.{json,pool_name,pool_id}, ceph.dir.pin, ceph.dir.pin.distributed, ceph.dir.pin.random; ENODATA for every other ceph.* name (charmap, quiesce, unknown) so libcephfs falls back to stored xattrs. Reply = ENCODE_START(1,1) + string, no trace.<br>Risk: kclient requires value_len == remaining bytes; any error but ENODATA for an unset name breaks callers that only tolerate NoData (mgr/volumes info).<br>Question: C++ does no access check here, so path-restricted clients read values outside their path; add the MDS-cap path check? |
| [F-NS-074](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-074) | Client-synthesized ceph.* vxattrs (InodeStat contract) | contract | Most ceph.* names users and mgr/volumes read (rstats, dirstats, quota, layouts, pin) are computed by clients from InodeStat v9 fields, so every field must be present and correct. | P0 encodes zero or empty snap_btime, snap_metadata, fscrypt fields and optmetadata; a set of ceph.dir.rctime or ceph.caps (kclient forwards them) -> EINVAL. |
| [F-CACHE-007](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-007) | File layout field and layout inheritance | contract | Clients compute data placement from the layout (pool, namespace, striping) and mgr/volumes sets pool layouts on subvolumes and groups; inheritance from the nearest ancestor and the size-0 rule for changes are client-visible. | Legacy 28-byte encoding for old daemons and Fr/Fw withholding for clients without FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2 are out; InodeStat keeps the 28-byte layout plus pool_ns.<br>Question: Inheritance crosses the shard boundary (group-level ceph.dir.layout from `subvolumegroup create --pool_layout`): a shard needs the effective layout of its root-shard ancestors. |
| [F-CACHE-055](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-055) | Replica subtrees at delegation boundaries | redesign | D5 keeps replicas only at shard boundaries: the parent shard must answer lookup, getattr and readdir entries for a shard-root inode with current attributes (mode, times, rstat, quota, realm). | A boundary copy of each shard-root inode in the parent shard, refreshed on change (including btime and change_attr, unlike C++ lock payloads).<br>Question: Who issues caps on a shard-root inode reached through the parent rank (C++ replicas issue non-auth caps and the auth gathers revocations), and how fresh must the copy be? |
| [F-SNAP-033](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-033) | Directory entry statistics (dirstat / fragstat) | contract | Directory nlink (2 + nsubdirs), kclient directory size (entries), mtime/ctime and the NFS change attribute come from dirstat, which must be exact when read with Fs; rmdir emptiness depends on it. | Exact at once within a shard with one frag per directory; remote dentries count in nfiles. |
| [F-SNAP-034](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-034) | Recursive subtree statistics (rstat) | contract | ceph.dir.rbytes/rfiles/rsubdirs/rctime, directory st_size = rbytes (libcephfs default; mgr/volumes bytes_used and resize --no_shrink) and quota usage all come from rstat. | rsnaps stays 0 until P1. Keep the semantics: rbytes = logical size, rsubdirs counts the directory itself, a hard link counts once, unlinked files leave at once. |
| [F-SNAP-035](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-035) | Eager upward stat propagation (predirty_journal_parents) | redesign | Stat changes must reach every ancestor inside the shard up to the shard root within a bounded lag (C++: the parent at once on one rank, each higher level within about 1-5 s); quota enforcement and mgr/volumes bytes_used read the subvolume data directory. | Commutative deltas (sum, max) so concurrent creates in one directory do not serialize; crossing the shard root is F-SNAP-037. |
| [F-SNAP-037](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-037) | Scatter-gather of dirstat and rstat across dirfrags and ranks | redesign | D4: shard-root rstat must be aggregated into root-shard ancestors (group dirs, /volumes, /), eventually consistent as in C++: group bytes_used and Rook's subvolumegroup resize --no-shrink, fs volume info (statx /volumes), group quotas, root_r* counters. | A cross-shard rstat delta channel from each shard root to the root shard; dirfrag-level scatter-gather and MLock payloads are OUT (D5, D16). |
| [F-SNAP-039](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-039) | rstat reads routed to the auth rank (GETATTR CEPH_STAT_RSTAT) | contract | Clients send CEPH_STAT_RSTAT getattrs to the inode's auth and apply directory rstat (libcephfs also quota) only from replies with CEPH_CAP_FLAG_AUTH or a newer version. | The shard owning an inode answers.<br>Question: Which shard is authoritative for a shard-root inode (attributes, quota, rstat, caps, vxattrs): the subvolume shard or the parent shard that holds its dentry? |
| [F-SNAP-042](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-042) | Quota vxattrs (ceph.quota, ceph.quota.max_bytes, ceph.quota.max_files) | contract | mgr/volumes (subvolume --size, resize, group quotas, clones) and admins set ceph.quota.max_bytes/max_files (IEC suffixes, 0 removes) with the MDS-cap p flag. | Broadcast the new quota after commit (C++ broadcasts before); fix removexattr of the combined ceph.quota returning EINVAL. |
| [F-SNAP-043](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-043) | Quota directories become snaprealm roots | contract | D8 hard client requirement: after a successful quota setxattr the directory must be its own realm root, else kclient and libcephfs return EOPNOTSUPP and never enforce the quota. | Risk: The new realm must reach the requester before it processes the setxattr reply (SPLIT sent before the reply). |
| [F-SNAP-044](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-044) | Quota and usage in InodeStat | contract | Quota and recursive usage travel in every InodeStat (rsnaps from v4, subvolume_id in v9); clients enforce from them. | Legacy encoding without REPLY_ENCODING is OUT.<br>Risk: The quota sub-struct must have struct_compat == 1 (kclient EIO). |
| [F-SNAP-045](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-045) | MClientQuota usage pushes with thresholds | contract | Clients learn usage near the limit and quota changes by other clients only through MClientQuota pushes from the quota inode's owner; without them writers overrun quotas. | Thresholds (7/8 of the limit, 1/16 of headroom) may be tuned; group-quota pushes go from the root shard to clients holding caps on the group dir; NOQUOTA caps (legacy) OUT.<br>Risk: Exact packed layout: the kclient parses fixed offsets and libcephfs asserts no trailing bytes; the message counts toward push_seq. |
| [F-SNAP-047](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-047) | Client-side quota enforcement (EDQUOT, cross-quota EXDEV) | contract | Quotas are enforced by clients (EDQUOT, EXDEV across quota realms) from MDS-supplied realms and usage; the MDS must keep realm roots resolvable by LOOKUPINO and usage current enough. | Question: Keep client-only enforcement (C++ parity) or add MDS-side checks (max_files on create, max_size grants capped at max_bytes) for per-subvolume SLAs? |
| [F-SNAP-048](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-048) | Quota-based statfs | contract | df inside a quota directory reports the quota; the MDS side is the quota and rbytes in InodeStat plus one forced GETATTR per libcephfs statfs. |  |
| [F-SNAP-050](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-050) | Subvolume flag (ceph.dir.subvolume) | redesign | D3: ceph.dir.subvolume marks a shard root; mgr/volumes sets it on every subvolume it creates, re-applies it on every subvolume open and reads it back through GETVXATTR ('0'/'1'). | Contract kept: idempotent cheap no-op when already set, EINVAL for non-directories and nested subvolumes, realm with the SUBVOLUME flag, SPLIT notification. New: setting it creates a shard from an existing, non-empty directory (<uuid> already exists).<br>Question: C++ allows clearing the flag (shard merge) and marking a directory above an existing subvolume (nested shards; changes the kclient-sticky subvolume_id). Refuse both? |
| [F-SNAP-051](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-051) | No nested subvolumes; outermost subvolume wins | contract | Nested subvolumes are refused with EINVAL; the outermost subvolume defines the subvolume (shard) of an inode. |  |
| [F-SNAP-052](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-052) | Cross-subvolume link and rename rejected with EXDEV | contract | D4: link and rename between different subvolumes (shards) return EXDEV, which clients already handle; a subvolume root can still be renamed between non-subvolume parents (mgr/volumes trash to _deleting). | The rule applies at every shard boundary, including admin-marked shard roots; shard-root moves use the D4 mechanism. |
| [F-SNAP-054](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-054) | Subvolume id on the wire (InodeStat, MClientCaps) | subset | InodeStat v9 and flush acks carry subvolume_id (the outermost subvolume root, i.e. the shard root; 0 outside subvolumes); kclient and libcephfs key per-subvolume metrics on it. | Encode subvolume_id in InodeStat v9 and cap flush acks: the shard root ino for inodes inside a subvolume (outermost ceph.dir.subvolume root), 0 elsewhere. A non-zero value never changes for an inode (kernel WARN_ON_ONCE). Its use for subvolume metrics is P1 (F-QOS-015, F-QOS-017).<br>Risk: The kclient keeps the first non-zero subvolume_id of an inode and ignores later different values (WARN_ON_ONCE): an inode's id must never change, so shard split or merge must respect it.<br>Reconciled: Reconciles F-SNAP-054 (P0 contract), F-PROTO-025 and F-QOS-015 (P1): the field is part of the P0 encodings; the metrics that consume it are P1. |
| [F-ADM-006](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-006) | MDS cap string grammar, parser and canonical forms | contract | The monitor validates and stores mds cap strings with the C++ parser, so the MDS must accept exactly that language (byte-exact `allow` = rwps, flags in order r w f p Q q s, fsname/path/root_squash/uid/gids/network qualifiers) or refuse the connection. | Risk: A stricter parser refuses entities the mon accepted; a looser one can grant unintended access. |
| [F-ADM-007](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-007) | Grant evaluation: union of grants in a fixed step order | contract | Union-of-grants evaluation in the fixed step order is the server-side multi-tenant authorization for kclient, libcephfs, Ganesha and mgr; denial is -EACCES. |  |
| [F-ADM-008](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-008) | path= restriction and the inode path used for checks | contract | Subvolume isolation: mgr/volumes, mgr/nfs and fs authorize produce path grants checked against the inode's primary path from the fs root (stray_prior_path for unlinked-open inodes). | Risk: With shards on different ranks the serving rank must know the shard root's current absolute path (ancestor renames such as the trash rename into /volumes/_deleting) and the prior path of unlinked-open inodes; a stale path is a cross-tenant leak or a false EACCES. |
| [F-ADM-009](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-009) | fsname= restriction and file-system gating of session messages | contract | Multi-fs isolation: grants are scoped by fsname and every MClientSession from an entity without a grant for the MDSMap fs gets CEPH_SESSION_REJECT with error_string (libcephfs mount fails EPERM). |  |
| [F-ADM-010](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-010) | uid=/gids= restriction with simplified mode-bit check | contract | The monitor accepts uid=/gids= grants; ignoring them would widen access, so the simplified owner/group/other mode-bit check (and the chown/chgrp rules) must be enforced as in C++. | Risk: C++ intersects the caller gid list assuming it is sorted; sort it first. |
| [F-ADM-011](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-011) | root_squash grants | contract | fs authorize and the dashboard generate root_squash grants; the MDS skips them for writes by uid 0 or gid 0 (vxattr ops excluded) and clients enforce them locally from cap_auths. |  |
| [F-ADM-012](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-012) | p flag: permission to set ceph.* vxattrs and non-default layouts | contract | Tenant isolation: subvolume authorize grants `allow rw path=...` without p, so tenants cannot change quotas, layouts, pins or ceph.dir.subvolume; mgr (allow *) and mirror bootstrap (rwps) rely on holding p. | Risk: Without the p check a tenant can raise its own quota or repin its subvolume. |
| [F-ADM-014](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-014) | f flag: metadata-pool-full bypass | subset | When the metadata pool is full only grants with f (allow *: mgr, admin) may run growth ops, so operators can clean up; part of the full-pool policy (F-STORE-099). | MAY_FULL check on the request's base inode for SETLAYOUT, SETDIRLAYOUT, SETXATTR, RMXATTR, CREATE, SYMLINK, MKSNAP, LINK, RENAME while the metadata pool is FULL; a single reply (no C++ duplicate reply).<br>Question: Which errno for the denial: -EACCES (what clients see from C++ today) or -ENOSPC (documented in full.rst)? |
| [F-ADM-016](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-016) | network restriction | contract | The monitor accepts `network CIDR` qualifiers; ignoring them would grant access from any address, so the session address must be matched (same family, an unparseable network makes the grant unusable). |  |
| [F-ADM-017](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-017) | Mount root check at session open and auth_name check at reclaim | contract | REQUEST_OPEN with a `root` outside every grant path gets REJECT ('non-allowable root'), and Ganesha's reclaim of another session requires the same cephx entity (-EPERM otherwise). | REJECT without error_string to pre-MIMIC sessions is legacy and out. |
| [F-ADM-018](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-018) | Per-request access-check matrix | contract | Defines which inode and mask each client op is checked against; this is the server-side enforcement behind path-restricted tenants (CSI, Manila, NFS exports). | The -EIO for clients without FS_FILE_LAYOUT_V2 and the undefined Server::_check_access are legacy and out.<br>Risk: Do not share one access decision across sessions (C++ batched getattr/lookup sends the head's result to other sessions); LOOKUPINO answered on any rank needs the inode path for the mask-0 match (Ganesha handles).<br>Question: Keep the C++ unchecked ops (RMXATTR of user xattrs, GETVXATTR, file locks, FILE_BLOCKDIFF) or close the RMXATTR gap? SNAP_METADATA must stay caps-unchecked for cephfs-mirror. |
| [F-ADM-021](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-021) | cap_auths export in CEPH_SESSION_OPEN and client-side checks | contract | The 7.3 kernel (bit 21) and libcephfs enforce read-only and root_squash locally from the cap_auths list carried in every CEPH_SESSION_OPEN; the kernel parses the v1 MDSCapAuth layout positionally and replaces its mount-wide list on each OPEN from any rank. | OPEN header v1 without cap_auths for pre-MIMIC clients is legacy and out.<br>Risk: Appending a field to MDSCapAuth/MDSCapMatch desynchronizes the kernel; an OPEN with an empty list (C++ force-opened import sessions) disables kernel checks, so sessions opened by shard migration (D6) must carry the real grants. |
| [F-ADM-023](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-023) | ceph fs authorize: cap generation and merge on the monitor | contract | The unchanged monitor generates and merges mds caps (fsname=, path=, root_squash, r w p s q Q) for operators, the dashboard and mirror bootstrap; the MDS must accept and enforce every string it produces. | No MDS code beyond parser and evaluator compatibility (F-ADM-006, F-ADM-007). |
| [F-ADM-024](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-024) | mgr/volumes subvolume authorize, deauthorize and authorized_list | contract | Default mgr/volumes flow for tenants: grants `allow r\|rw path=<data path>` without fsname and serializes its /volumes/*.meta bookkeeping with blocking flock(LOCK_EX), O_CREAT opens and in-place rewrites. | Risk: The bookkeeping files live in the root shard (D3), so blocking flock must work there. |
| [F-ADM-025](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-025) | mgr/nfs export user caps | contract | Each NFS export runs as a dedicated user with `allow rw path=<cmount_path>`; Ganesha's handle-based access (LOOKUPINO) is constrained only by the mask-0 path match. | Risk: LOOKUPINO answered on any rank (D6) must still evaluate the path match. |
| [F-ADM-089](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-089) | mgr/volumes: in-filesystem layout, .meta files, versions, user metadata, open side effects | contract | mgr/volumes keeps all state as files under /volumes in the root shard (D3): .meta rewritten via .tmp + fsync + rename over the old file, symlink indexes, auth .meta files; every subvolume open re-sets ceph.dir.subvolume=1 and stats paths. | v1 to v2 .meta upgrade (old data) is out.<br>Risk: setxattr ceph.dir.subvolume=1 on an existing shard root (every `fs subvolume info`) must be an idempotent cheap no-op, not a shard re-creation. |
| [F-ADM-090](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-090) | mgr/volumes: subvolume groups | contract | Rook creates group csi and CephFilesystemSubVolumeGroup groups (create, resize --no-shrink, pin distributed, rm); groups are root-shard dirs needing mkdir, rmdir ENOTEMPTY, chown/chmod, quota, layout pool and pin vxattrs. | Group charmap options are P2 (D12).<br>Risk: Group create copies the nearest ancestor's ceph.dir.layout.pool, so / must expose a layout. |
| [F-ADM-091](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-091) | mgr/volumes: subvolume create | contract | The core default flow (ceph-csi under Rook, Manila, dashboard): mkdirs, setxattr ceph.dir.subvolume=1 (shard root creation, D3/D4), quota, layout pool/namespace, chown, chmod, an earmark write, then .meta files. | Charmap and enctag only when requested; fscrypt attrs only for clones (P1).<br>Risk: Fails if a zero-length user xattr cannot be stored or / exposes no layout; verify in the mgr code that normalization/casesensitive are set only on request (charmap is P2). |
| [F-ADM-092](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-092) | mgr/volumes: removal, trash, snapshot retention and asynchronous purge | subset | `fs subvolume rm` renames the subvolume base dir into /volumes/_deleting (a shard-root rename in the root shard, D4) and 4 purge threads delete its contents with client unlink/rmdir traffic. | rm without snapshots, trash rename and rmtree purge; --retain-snapshots and .trash incarnations come with snapshots (P1).<br>Risk: rmtree relies on rmdir of a directory with snapshots failing ENOTEMPTY (P1); path= grants of the old location stop matching after the trash rename. |
| [F-ADM-093](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-093) | mgr/volumes: subvolume resize via quota | contract | `fs subvolume resize` sets ceph.quota.max_bytes (0 removes) and reports bytes_used from directory st_size (rstat rbytes through libcephfs); Rook resizes groups with --no-shrink. |  |
| [F-ADM-096](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-096) | mgr/volumes and mgr/nfs: earmark and enctag xattrs | contract | Subvolume create always writes user.ceph.subvolume.earmark (empty if none) and mgr/nfs checks or sets it at export creation, so zero-length user xattr values must be stored and returned. |  |

### Subvolumes as shards, quotas, statistics, layouts, auth caps: P1 (1)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-ADM-013](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-013) | s flag and snapshot uid range | contract | The s flag and the snap uid range only gate MKSNAP/RMSNAP/RENAMESNAP and SNAP_METADATA, which come with snapshots (D8: P0 refuses MKSNAP with EPERM); the flag is parsed in P0 (F-ADM-006). | Risk: SNAP_METADATA must stay caps-unchecked (uid range only) because cephfs-mirror runs with `allow r`. |

### Subvolumes as shards, quotas, statistics, layouts, auth caps: P2 (4)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-NS-065](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-065) | Layout ops SETLAYOUT and SETDIRLAYOUT (kernel layout ioctls) | contract | Only the kernel layout ioctls send SETLAYOUT/SETDIRLAYOUT; the layout vxattrs (F-NS-072) give the same function and are what mgr/volumes uses. | Until implemented, answer EOPNOTSUPP. |
| [F-SNAP-040](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-040) | Snapshot rstats (mds_snap_rstat) | contract | mds_snap_rstat defaults to false (snapshot views show rstats as copied at COW time); optional once snapshots exist. |  |
| [F-ADM-015](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-015) | q/Q flags: access to quarantined subtrees | contract | q/Q only matter for quarantined subtrees (D12: quarantine P2); the letters are parsed in P0 (F-ADM-006) and have no effect until then. | Risk: C++ first-match pitfall: a matching grant lacking q denies before later grants are consulted. |
| [F-ADM-020](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-020) | fscrypt attribute changes require allow * | contract | fscrypt is P2 (D12); the allow_all gate on SETATTR with FSCRYPT_AUTH/FILE comes with it (mgr/volumes clones, P1, copy ceph.fscrypt.* attrs). | Risk: Until then decide what a SETATTR carrying fscrypt flags returns. |
