| [F-SNAP-049](../mds-rust-map/features/08-snapshots-quotas-subvolumes.md#f-snap-049) | Legacy pre-Mimic quota realm creation (create_quota_realm) (legacy) | Legacy (old-data) path for pre-Mimic quota directories without a realm, ineffective since v16.1. |

<a id="scale-out"></a>

## Multiple ranks, shard placement and migration

### Multiple ranks, shard placement and migration: P0 (47)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-040](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-040) | Growing the number of active ranks (max_mds) | drop | Growth towards max_mds is mon-driven, one rank at a time; the Rust parts are up:creating/up:starting (F-CLU-064, F-CLU-065) and placing shards on the new rank (D6). |  |
| [F-CLU-041](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-041) | Shrinking: monitor-driven up:stopping and FSMap::stop | drop | The mon sets the highest active rank to up:stopping when in > max_mds and removes it on down:stopped; cephadm and Rook upgrades rely on it (max_mds 1); the Rust part is the drain of F-CLU-102. | Risk: Only rank in-1 is stopped and only when up:active; after STOPPED the mon answers with a null map and the daemon must leave (F-CLU-057). |
| [F-CLU-071](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-071) | Survivor reactions to a peer failing and recovering | redesign | With multi-rank P0 (D6), surviving ranks keep serving their own shards while a peer recovers and park or retry the cross-rank work aimed at it (LOOKUPINO forwards, shard migrations, rstat/quota propagation), detecting restarts by incarnation. | Invalidate shard-boundary copies held from the failed rank instead of a rejoin exchange (F-CLU-085). |
| [F-CLU-072](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-072) | Deferring messages to bootstrapping peers and dropping stale peer messages | redesign | The new MDS-to-MDS protocol must hold messages for peers that are still booting and reject messages from an old incarnation of a rank (a fenced holder may still be connected). |  |
| [F-CLU-074](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-074) | Subtree authority resolution (subtree resolves) | redesign | After any failure all ranks must agree which rank owns each shard (D3: one owner at a time; D6: per-rank failover); C++ claims subtrees with MMDSResolve, Rust needs a durable, epoch-versioned shard map. |  |
| [F-CLU-075](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-075) | Ambiguous subtree import disambiguation | redesign | Live shard migration is P0 (D6), so a migration interrupted by the failure of either side must resolve deterministically: exactly one owner, with client caps and sessions ending at that owner. |  |
| [F-CLU-088](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-088) | Moving reconnected caps between ranks (IMPORT/EXPORT during rejoin) | redesign | With live shard migration and per-rank failover a client can report a cap to a rank that no longer owns the inode; the cap must reach the owner and the client be told with CEPH_CAP_OP_IMPORT/EXPORT matched by cap_id (F-CAPS-036). | Risk: Forwarded caps that are never answered leave clients holding caps no rank tracks (C++ gotcha). |
| [F-CLU-102](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-102) | Stopping a rank (up:stopping to down:stopped) | redesign | D6: a rank stopped by a max_mds decrease (cephadm and Rook upgrades use max_mds 1) must hand off all its shards with their client caps, strays and per-shard purge queues (F-BG-022/023), keep processing requests meanwhile, close remaining sessions (the last rank evicts and blocklists all clients), and only then request down:stopped. | Export whole shards (no stray export to rank 0, no C++ journal capping); ignore new session opens while stopping; keep rank-level state for a later up:starting.<br>Risk: Report STOPPED only when the rank owns no shard and no session: the mon then removes the rank and kernels force-close sessions to ranks >= size(in).<br>Reconciled: Reconciles CLU ('drain purge work') with BG (per-shard purge queues travel with their shard; no full drain). |
| [F-PROTO-041](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-041) | Force-opened sessions (imports, rejoin, cross-MDS rename) | redesign | When a shard moves to another rank (D6), caps are imported for clients that may never have opened a session there; they receive an unsolicited OPEN before the cap IMPORT messages. | Contract kept: the importing rank opens and persists the session, sends OPEN (queued until the client connects) and FORCE_RO if needed; importing sessions are not closed by REQUEST_CLOSE, kill or idle eviction until the import ends; a failed import closes sessions the client never opened. Mechanism: the new MDS-to-MDS migration protocol, not EImportStart/ESessions. |
| [F-PROTO-075](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-075) | Client-side rank selection and the hints the MDS provides | subset | Clients route each request themselves from caps, DirStat auth hints and random choice, so hints must name the rank that owns the shard (D6) and the directory hash must match readdir ordering. | Trivial fragtree (D5): DirStat {frag 0, auth = owning rank or -1, dist empty}, no fragtree splits in InodeStat, dl_dir_hash = 2 (rjenkins) on new directories; no replica dist hints.<br>Risk: Requests still reach any rank (random choice, LOOKUPINO), see F-PROTO-076 and F-PROTO-099. |
| [F-PROTO-099](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-099) | Inode-number addressed access (NFS file handles, #ino paths, LOOKUPINO) | contract | Ganesha file handles and the kernel NFS export resolve inode numbers with LOOKUPINO, LOOKUPPARENT and LOOKUPNAME sent to a random rank (D6: answerable from any rank). | Risk: -ESTALE for purged or private inodes; Ganesha handles carry {ino, snapid, fscid} and must stay valid across MDS restarts. |
| [F-PROTO-107](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-107) | Peer-MDS connections | redesign | Multi-rank is P0 (D6), so ranks need a transport between them, but only Rust daemons speak it (constraint 3), so policies and protocol are free. | msgr1 protocol 36 and its race rules are out. |
| [F-CAPS-036](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-036) | Cap migration to clients (EXPORT / IMPORT) | contract | Live whole-shard migration and per-rank failover are P0 (D6, D9); EXPORT/IMPORT is the only way to move a client's auth cap between ranks without losing dirty state (also used when reconnect caps reach a non-owner, F-CLU-088). | EXPORT peer {importer cap_id, importer last_seq+1, mseq+1, rank}; IMPORT seq equal to that issue_seq, FLAG_AUTH, sent by the owner only; peer.mds = -1 drops the cap. Cross-rank rename use disappears (EXDEV, D4).<br>Risk: kclient adds every IMPORTed cap as AUTH and ignores an EXPORT whose cap_id does not match its cap from that rank. |
| [F-NS-022](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-022) | ESTALE recovery of unknown inode numbers | redesign | Clients address inodes by number, so any ino a client still holds must be found even when it is not cached on the receiving rank or its shard moved (D6: lookup by ino from any rank). | Replace find_ino_peers/open_ino backtrace chasing with an FS-wide ino -> shard index (D2: purpose in scope); also recover on the vxattr path, which in C++ returns ESTALE at once. |
| [F-CACHE-035](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-035) | Authority of cache objects and the auth bit | redesign | Every object needs exactly one authoritative rank; with D3 the authority of any inode, dentry or directory is the owner rank of its shard. | Per-dirfrag authorities, the STATE_AUTH bit and filelock/nestlock scattered across ranks are dropped (D5, D16). |
| [F-CACHE-036](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-036) | Subtree map | redesign | The partition of the namespace among ranks becomes the shard map (D3, D6): shard -> owner rank, file-system-wide, durable and epoch-versioned, replacing per-rank subtree maps and ESubtreeMap. |  |
| [F-CACHE-047](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-047) | Lookup by inode number (open_ino) | redesign | NFS file handles (kernel nfsd, Ganesha), libcephfs ll_lookup_inode and kernel quota-realm lookups resolve bare inode numbers with LOOKUPINO/LOOKUPPARENT/LOOKUPNAME sent to any rank (D6). | Durable ino -> shard routing readable on every rank (e.g. range leases plus exceptions), forward to the owner, which resolves through its per-shard index (F-STORE-062); LOOKUPHASH comes with snapshots (P1).<br>Risk: Return ESTALE only for numbers not in use; while the owning shard recovers, wait (as C++ does): ESTALE makes NFS clients treat the file as deleted.<br>Question: Directories that become shard roots after creation (mgr/volumes v2 creates <base>/<uuid> before setting ceph.dir.subvolume on <base>) bring inodes numbered from the root shard's ranges. |
| [F-CACHE-050](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-050) | ESTALE recovery via find_ino_peers | redesign | Requests addressed by an inode number this rank does not cache must still be served or forwarded (F-NS-022); ESTALE reaches clients only for unknown numbers. | Resolve through the inode-number index (F-CACHE-047): load locally if the shard is owned here, else forward; no find_ino peer queries. |
| [F-STORE-032](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-032) | Cross-rank two-phase-commit records | redesign | General cross-rank 2PC is out (D4: EXDEV), but the cross-shard operations D4 keeps (create or mark, trash-rename and remove shard roots, between the root shard and a subvolume shard) still need crash-safe coordination. | Only these shard-root operations (e.g. an intent record in the root-shard WAL plus idempotent completion); no link/rename/rmdir 2PC. |
| [F-STORE-033](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-033) | Subtree migration records and rename subtree hints | redesign | Live shard migration is P0 (D6); with per-shard WALs in shared RADOS, migration hands over WAL and store ownership instead of journaling a copy of the subtree (EImportStart). | Durable ownership change (shard-map epoch), exporter drained before the importer writes, client sessions force-opened on the importer for imported caps; a crash at any step resolves to exactly one owner. |
| [F-MULTI-001](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-001) | Subtree authority hand-off protocol (export/import state machines) | redesign | Live shard migration is P0 (D6); the dirfrag/bounds state machines and MExportDir* messages are MDS-to-MDS and free. Needed: a shard-granular, crash-safe ownership transfer with one commit point. | The shard is the unit (no dirfrags, bounds or bystanders). Legacy unused message ids out.<br>Question: Where does the shard-to-rank map live (epoch-fenced, readable by every rank for forwarding and LOOKUPINO): an MDS-private RADOS object, the root shard, or a new MDSMap field (needs a mon change)? |
| [F-MULTI-002](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-002) | Export preconditions and silent refusals | redesign | A shard migration needs explicit preconditions (target active, no failover in progress, shard not already moving; later not quiesced or quarantined); C++ refuses silently, the new placement engine should report it. | C++ system-inode and stray-directory rules disappear (strays are per shard). |
| [F-MULTI-006](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-006) | Metadata and cap transfer encoding (MExportDir) | redesign | The importer must receive the shard's volatile state consistently with its durable metadata: caps (cap_id, seq, mseq, issued, pending, wanted), client ranges, fcntl/flock tables and client identities; with per-shard journals the durable part can be handed over in RADOS instead of copied (D2, D3). | MExportDir encoding out. File locks must survive migration; dentry leases are not migrated (revoke first, P1).<br>Risk: The dashboard and Grafana read mds.exported_inodes and mds.imported_inodes: keep counting inodes moved with shards (ADM).<br>Question: C++ does not migrate per-session completed requests; should per-shard idempotency tables move with the shard? |
| [F-MULTI-012](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-012) | Cap EXPORT message to clients (CEPH_CAP_OP_EXPORT) | contract | Live migration is P0 (D6); kclient and libcephfs move or drop a cap only on EXPORT (placeholder cap toward the importer, session open, in-flight flushes moved). | head.cap_id = current exporter cap id, migrate_seq = m, seq and caps 0, peer {importer cap_id, importer last_seq+1, m+1, importer rank, 0} at the size offset, counted, osd_epoch_barrier; peer.mds = -1 to drop.<br>Risk: kclient ignores an EXPORT whose cap_id does not match; libcephfs keys on peer.cap_id (0 = drop and set I_CAP_DROPPED). |
| [F-MULTI-013](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-013) | Cap IMPORT message to clients and auth-cap switch (CEPH_CAP_OP_IMPORT) | contract | Live migration is P0 (D6); only IMPORT makes the importer's cap the AUTH cap (higher mseq) and makes clients re-send in-flight flushes and max_size requests. | Legacy IMPORT without peer block (no EXPORT_PEER) out. Queue IMPORT until the client's session is connected.<br>Risk: IMPORT seq must equal the issue_seq announced in EXPORT (kclient placeholder seq = issue_seq - 1), it must come from the owner only and carry the snap trace of the cap's realm. |
| [F-MULTI-014](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-014) | Migrate sequence (mseq) and stale cap-message handling | contract | mseq orders a cap's incarnations across ranks (D6): clients switch the AUTH cap only to a higher mseq (ceph_seq_cmp) and stamp it on updates and releases; the MDS drops messages with an older mseq. |  |
| [F-MULTI-015](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-015) | Writeable-range reconciliation on cap import | redesign | If a client listed in client_ranges arrives without an imported cap (blocklisted, no session), the importer must schedule size recovery (F-CAPS-022), else bytes written up to max_size can be lost. |  |
| [F-MULTI-016](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-016) | Client session flush barrier before shipping (FLUSHMSG) | contract | Before cap state moves (D6), every client with caps in the shard must flush its pending cap releases to the exporter: FLUSHMSG with seq = push_seq, FLUSHMSG_ACK echoing it. | Legacy skip for connections without EXPORT_PEER out (every baseline client has it).<br>Risk: A client that never acks must not stall the migration forever: complete on stale or eviction, or cancel (F-MULTI-022). |
| [F-MULTI-017](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-017) | Forced client session open on the importer | redesign | The importer must hold a session for every client whose caps it takes before sending IMPORT; clients accept an unsolicited CEPH_SESSION_OPEN and queued messages (F-PROTO-041). | Persisted in the new session format; an importing session cannot be closed or evicted mid-migration; forced sessions the client never used are closed if the migration is reversed. |
| [F-MULTI-018](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-018) | Request blocking and forwarding around migration | contract | Requests on a migrating shard must block without error and, once ownership moved, be bounced with MClientRequestForward (the client resends), never relayed between ranks; DirStat auth in replies updates client routing. | Risk: Kernel BUG_ON if a forward is sent for a tid that already got a reply; num_fwd must strictly increase (else -EMULTIHOP). |
| [F-MULTI-019](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-019) | Export-target publication (MMDSLoadTargets, MDSMap export_targets) | subset | Unmodified kclients reconnect to an export target in up:reconnect even without a session and libcephfs pre-opens sessions to targets; that is what makes an importer crash right after taking a shard recoverable. | Send MMDSLoadTargets naming the destination before migrating a shard, wait until the MDSMap lists it, drop it after completion. Decay counters and bystander hits dropped.<br>Risk: Needs mon cap mds x (profile mds); every change bumps the FSMap epoch; an export target >= 256 in the MDSMap shuts kernel mounts down. |
| [F-MULTI-021](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-021) | Migration journaling (EImportStart, EExport, EImportFinish) | redesign | A crash at any point of a shard migration must resolve to exactly one owner (D6, D9); EImportStart/EExport/EImportFinish are C++ formats (D2; cephfs-journal-tool OUT). | New durable hand-off record with one commit point (for example epoch-fenced shard ownership) and per-shard WAL handover instead of re-journaling the metadata. |
| [F-MULTI-022](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-022) | Export cancellation and reversal | redesign | A migration must be abortable until its commit point, with clients keeping the exporter's caps (nothing was sent to them yet), for example when the target fails or a client does not ack FLUSHMSG. | Add timeout-driven cancel (C++ cancels only on failures and the freeze timeout). |
| [F-MULTI-023](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-023) | Rank failure during migration and resolve interplay | redesign | Per-rank failover (D9) combined with live migration (D6): every in-flight migration must end with one owner when the exporter, the importer or another rank fails; the C++ resolve and ambiguous-import machinery is MDS-to-MDS and replaceable by the commit record. | Risk: C++ has an assert after an early reverse (subtree-migration 8); reuse its crash points (mds_kill_export_at, mds_kill_import_at) as test cases. |
| [F-MULTI-038](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-038) | Set and remove ceph.dir.pin | contract | mgr/volumes (fs subvolume pin, fs subvolumegroup pin export) and Rook subvolume-group pinning set ceph.dir.pin; they expect the C++ validation and errors (EACCES without p, EINVAL for root, non-directory or parse error, EDOM for >= 256, negative unsets, rank >= max_mds accepted) (D6). | Persist on the directory and bump ctime/change_attr; placement effect only for shard roots and their root-shard ancestors (F-MULTI-040).<br>Risk: Only RMXATTR removes; a SETXATTR without value is EINVAL in C++.<br>Question: Pins on directories that are not shard roots (classic deployments pin /home/* to spread load) cannot split a shard: store and report only, or make the pinned directory an implicit shard root (then renames and links across it get EXDEV)? |
| [F-MULTI-039](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-039) | Pin read-back (InodeStat dir_pin, GETVXATTR) | contract | Clients serve getfattr ceph.dir.pin from InodeStat dir_pin (own pin, -1 when unset) and send GETVXATTR for ceph.dir.pin.random and .distributed; admins and test_volumes read them back. | Legacy parts out (InodeStat without REPLY_ENCODING, client fallback without OP_GETVXATTR). |
| [F-MULTI-040](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-040) | Pin inheritance and precedence resolution | redesign | Placement hints (D6): a shard's target rank comes from its own pin, else the nearest ancestor pin or distributed flag (group, /volumes, /), the documented closest-parent-wins rule; only shard roots are placed. | Evaluated per shard root over root-shard ancestors; pins inside a shard have no placement effect; a distributed flag only spreads child shard roots; mds_bal_export_pin=false disables all hints.<br>Risk: The mgr/volumes trash rename moves a subvolume under /volumes/_deleting and changes its inherited hint: do not migrate shards that are being purged. |
| [F-MULTI-041](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-041) | Pin enforcement and aux subtrees (handle_export_pins) | redesign | The placement engine (D6) must re-evaluate shard targets on pin commits, shard creation, MDSMap changes and failover, and migrate shards whose target is another active rank; C++ aux subtrees and per-tick queue walks disappear. | Retry while the target is not active.<br>Question: When a pin is removed, C++ leaves the subtree where it is unless an ancestor pin applies; with a default placement for unpinned shards, should removal move the shard back to its default rank? |
| [F-MULTI-042](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-042) | Delayed pins (target rank >= max_mds) | redesign | Pins to ranks >= max_mds are accepted (operators and Rook may pin ahead of scaling) and must take effect when max_mds grows (qa test_phantom_rank); falls out of a placement engine that re-evaluates on every MDSMap. |  |
| [F-MULTI-043](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-043) | Pins veto balancer exports | redesign | Explicit pins must override any automatic placement of shards (the C++ balancer veto becomes: automatic placement never moves a pinned shard); only a stopping rank overrides pins (F-MULTI-049). | Question: Default placement of unpinned shards: C++ with the balancer off leaves them where their parent lives (normally rank 0); should the new MDS spread them automatically? |
| [F-MULTI-045](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-045) | Distributed ephemeral pins (ceph.dir.pin.distributed) | redesign | Rook sets ceph.dir.pin.distributed=1 on every CephFilesystemSubVolumeGroup by default and mgr/volumes exposes it (D6): the subvolume shards of such a group must be spread over ranks by a consistent hash. | Hash each child shard (not dirfrags), no forced fragmentation (D5); vxattr stored and validated as in C++ (strict_strtob, EINVAL); on directories without child shards store and report only. |
| [F-MULTI-046](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-046) | Random ephemeral pins (ceph.dir.pin.random) | tolerate | D12 (tolerate or OUT): mgr/volumes (fs subvolumegroup pin random) and Rook pinning.random set it; rejecting the vxattr would fail those commands and reconciles. | Accept, validate (EINVAL on parse error or above mds_export_ephemeral_random_max, EDOM outside [0,1]), persist and return through GETVXATTR; no placement effect.<br>Question: Treat a random policy on a group as a spread hint like distributed? |
| [F-MULTI-047](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-047) | Consistent hash into a rank bucket (hash_into_rank_bucket) | redesign | Hash placement must move few shards when max_mds changes (qa grow and shrink tests check minimal movement); the exact C++ function is not a contract (balancer 7.7). | Any jump-style consistent hash of the shard id over max_mds (as in C++, hints may then name ranks that are not up yet). |
| [F-MULTI-048](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-048) | Ephemeral pin redistribution on max_mds and config changes | redesign | Rank grow and shrink driven by max_mds are P0 (D6): hash-placed shards must be re-placed with minimal movement when max_mds changes. | Honour runtime toggles of mds_export_ephemeral_distributed/random; C++ frag-bit recomputation drops. |
| [F-MULTI-049](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-049) | Subtree hand-off by a stopping rank (max_mds decrease) | redesign | A stopping rank (max_mds decrease) must hand off every shard, ignoring pins to itself, before the monitor can mark it stopped (D6). | Targets come from the placement engine over the remaining ranks (C++: parent's authority or rank 0).<br>Question: Keep the root shard (/, /volumes, groups) on rank 0, which never stops? |
| [F-BG-022](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-022) | Rank stop: export all strays to rank 0 | redesign | Reducing max_mds must complete (mon, cephadm and Rook wait for down:stopped; D6); with per-shard strays, the strays and purge queue travel with each shard the stopping rank hands off, replacing the rank-0 sink. | Risk: C++ can stay in up:stopping forever when a stray migration fails; the hand-off must always make progress. |
| [F-ADM-094](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-094) | mgr/volumes: subvolume and group pinning | subset | D6: export and distributed pins on subvolumes and groups are shard placement hints; Rook pins every CephFilesystemSubVolumeGroup `distributed 1` by default. | ceph.dir.pin (rank, negative unsets, >= 256 EDOM) and ceph.dir.pin.distributed honoured as placement hints with the C++ validation errors; ceph.dir.pin.random accepted and ignored (D12). |

### Multiple ranks, shard placement and migration: P1 (2)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-MULTI-010](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-010) | Export queue and in-flight size throttle | redesign | Rank shrink and re-placement can move many shards at once; bounding concurrent migrations per rank is prudent but not needed for correctness. |  |
| [F-ADM-051](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-051) | Multi-MDS inspection and control: get subtrees, dump loads, export dir, dump_export_states | subset | No peer uses them (qa only); with whole-shard placement an equivalent shard-map dump and manual shard move help operators check pins and rebalance. | get subtrees -> shard placement dump (shard root, owner rank, pins); export dir -> move one shard to a rank; dump loads and dump_export_states dropped (no balancer, D5). |

### Multiple ranks, shard placement and migration: P2 (2)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-MULTI-025](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-025) | Quiesce and quarantine guards on migration | redesign | Quiesce and quarantine are P2 (D12); once quiesce exists a migration must not let a shard escape an active quiesce (refuse or carry the state, F-QOS-049), while the persistent quarantine flag simply travels with the shard (F-QOS-066). |  |
| [F-MULTI-035](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-035) | bal_rank_mask (balancer target ranks) | redesign | Monitor-owned MDSMap field used only by the balancer; optionally it can restrict which ranks the default placement uses for unpinned shards. |  |

### Multiple ranks, shard placement and migration: OUT (13)

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-070](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-070) | Recovery set and cluster-wide resolve/rejoin barriers | Cluster-wide resolve/rejoin barriers exist because C++ ranks share caches, locks and 2PC state; with whole-shard ownership (D3, D5) and no cross-rank 2PC (D4) each rank recovers independently (the multi-tenant recovery goal), and neither clients nor the mon see the barrier. |
| [F-CLU-073](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-073) | up:resolve entry and exit | The mon accepts up:replay -> up:reconnect for any rank count, and what C++ settles in up:resolve (2PC outcomes, ambiguous imports, root dirfrag authority, snap-table resync, fragment rollback) disappears with D4/D5; shard-ownership settlement is F-CLU-074/075. |
| [F-CLU-076](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-076) | Cross-rank two-phase update outcomes (peer resolves and ECommitted) | D4: no cross-rank 2PC; with whole-shard placement and EXDEV across shards no link, unlink, rmdir or rename spans ranks, so there are no peer resolves or ECommitted to settle. |
| [F-CLU-077](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-077) | Rename-specific resolve handling | Ambiguous rename witnesses and peer_inode_cap re-export exist only for C++ cross-rank renames, which D4 removes (EXDEV across shards). |
| [F-CLU-085](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-085) | Rejoin message exchange: weak rejoin, strong rejoin and acks | Weak/strong rejoin rebuilds replica nonces, lock states and remote auth pins between ranks; with no replicas beyond shard-boundary copies (D5) and no cross-rank locks (D4, D16), survivors just invalidate boundary copies (F-CLU-071); MDS-to-MDS only. |
| [F-CLU-086](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-086) | Recreating survivors' in-flight lock state on the recovering rank | Survivors' in-flight remote locks and auth pins on a recovering rank exist only with cross-rank operations and C++ lock machinery (D4, D16). |
| [F-CAPS-035](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-035) | Replica caps and MInodeFileCaps | With whole-shard ownership only the owner rank issues caps (D5, no replicas) and MInodeFileCaps is MDS-to-MDS; clients accept a reply whose cap record is empty and reach the owner through routing and forwards. |
| [F-NS-057](../mds-rust-map/features/04-namespace-and-posix-operations.md#f-ns-057) | Inode authority and cap migration inside a cross-rank rename | Inode and cap migration inside a cross-rank rename disappears with F-NS-056; cap EXPORT/IMPORT for shard migration is owned by the multi-MDS category (D6). |
| [F-MULTI-020](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-020) | Non-auth cap migration to the auth rank (MExportCaps, MGatherCaps) | Exists only because replica ranks issue caps (F-CAPS-035 OUT); the owner issues all caps and sends quota pushes itself. |
| [F-MULTI-030](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-030) | balance_automate file-system flag | The default balancer is out (D5); the flag lives in the monitor's MDSMap, is off by default, and the new MDS ignores it. |
| [F-MULTI-036](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-036) | Mantle Lua balancer policy | Mantle is out (D12); experimental and off by default. |
| [F-MULTI-037](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-037) | Mantle cluster log messages | Mantle is out (D12). |
| [F-MULTI-050](../mds-rust-map/features/07-multi-mds.md#f-multi-050) | Hot-directory replication (REP_ALL, MDirUpdate) | No hot-directory replication (D5); DirStat dist stays empty, so clients never send USE_ANY_MDS reads to replicas. |

<a id="operations"></a>

## Operations: admin commands, counters, health, config, orchestrators

### Operations: admin commands, counters, health, config, orchestrators: P0 (54)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CLU-028](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-028) | Health metrics carried in beacons | subset | Beacons carry the daemon's health metrics, which the mon turns into health checks (F-ADM-073) and uses to gate `mds fail`, `fs fail` and `fs set max_mds` (F-ADM-083). | MDSHealth v1 with known metric ids 1..17 only, never 0; standbys send none; keep message texts stable (a changing text costs an MDSMap epoch per beacon to all clients). Which metrics are P0 is decided in F-ADM-073.<br>Risk: Metric type 0 aborts the monitor's decoder. |
| [F-CLU-051](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-051) | Read-only fs and mds commands and the fs dump layout | drop | fs dump, fs get and mds stat are produced by the mon from beacon data and parsed by cephadm (`up:active`), Rook (fs get, liveness probe on fs dump names), prometheus and dashboard; the Rust part is correct names, states and addrs. | Deprecated `mds compat show` is legacy. |
| [F-CLU-052](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-052) | Orchestration helpers: mds ok-to-stop, last-seen, metadata, versions | drop | ok-to-stop, last-seen, metadata and versions are answered by the mon from the FSMap and boot metadata and used by cephadm and Rook; nothing to implement beyond accurate states and sys_info (F-CLU-025). |  |
| [F-CLU-062](../mds-rust-map/features/01-cluster-membership-and-ha.md#f-clu-062) | File-system health checks derived from the map | drop | FS_DEGRADED, MDS_ALL_DOWN, MDS_INSUFFICIENT_STANDBY and related checks are computed by the mon from rank states, and Rook and cephadm gate on them; the Rust part is accurate states. |  |
| [F-CAPS-095](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-095) | Cap and lease counters and dumps | subset | Default mgr flows read cap counters: fs status and telemetry use mds_mem.cap, the dashboard and the shipped Grafana dashboard use mds.caps, and session ls num_caps is an operator essential (D13). | P0: mds.caps and mds_mem.cap (priority high enough to reach the mgr), session ls num_caps and num_leases. Other counters and the dump inode cap view P2. |
| [F-CACHE-061](../mds-rust-map/features/05-metadata-cache-and-dirfrags.md#f-cache-061) | Memory-based soft cache limit | subset | Rook sets mds_cache_memory_limit to 0.5 x the pod memory limit and cephadm's autotuner subtracts it from host memory; MDS_CACHE_OVERSIZED is a mon-visible health check. | Honour mds_cache_memory_limit (with mds_cache_reservation and mds_health_cache_threshold) as the budget of all cached metadata and caps and raise MDS_CACHE_OVERSIZED above it; per-shard budgets are internal.<br>Risk: Ignoring the limit risks pod OOM kills; while MDS_CACHE_OVERSIZED is reported the mon refuses Rook's unconfirmed `mds fail`/`fs fail`. |
| [F-STORE-020](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-020) | flush journal admin command | contract | D13 lists a flush-journal equivalent as an operator essential; operators, QA (e.g. test_volumes.py before `dump inode`) and `cache drop` use `ceph tell mds.<fs>:0 flush journal`. | Same command and {message, return_code} output; semantics: checkpoint every shard of the rank and truncate its WALs; EROFS when read-only. |
| [F-STORE-105](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-105) | Journal and OpenFileTable perf counters | subset | Default mgr flows read mds_log counters: the dashboard mds_log.ev, the Grafana mixin evadd/ev/seg through prometheus, `ceph fs status` mds_log.replayed for standby-replay. | Provide mds_log.ev, evadd and seg (aggregated over the rank's shard WALs, same meaning); replayed with standby-replay (P1); other mds_log and oft counters P2 or OUT. |
| [F-BG-059](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-059) | damage ls and damage rm commands | subset | With damage containment in P0 (D10), operators need the documented 'damage ls\|rm' to find and clear entries; otherwise MDS_DAMAGE (HEALTH_ERR) blocks Rook reconciles until a restart (matches F-ADM-050). | damage ls JSON array {damage_type, id, ino, path, plus frag/dname/snap_id per type, shard where known} and damage rm <id> on every rank for that rank's shards; the 'uninline' type is legacy (out). |
| [F-ADM-026](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-026) | Local admin socket transport | contract | ceph-exporter (deployed by cephadm per host and Rook per node) and `ceph daemon` use the asok: NUL/newline-terminated JSON, 4-byte big-endian length reply, default path $run_dir/$cluster-$name.asok. | Legacy binary request protocol (leading NUL, codes 0/1/2) is out.<br>Risk: Under Rook the socket must resolve inside /run/ceph (default /var/run/ceph/ceph-mds.<id>.asok) or the exporter never scrapes it. |
| [F-ADM-027](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-027) | Remote tell transport (MCommand / MCommandReply) | contract | `ceph tell mds.X`, mgr modules (volumes evict, dashboard, stats) and libcephfs mds_command use MCommand/MCommandReply (errno on the wire, tid echo, out-of-order async replies); rankless daemons answer daemon-level commands and -ENOSYS for rank commands. | Legacy MMonCommand ('mds tell' via the monitor) is out. |
| [F-ADM-028](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-028) | Tell authorization: allow * required | contract | mgr (allow *), client.admin and Rook's csi-cephfs-provisioner (client eviction) run tell commands; other callers must get -EACCES 'permission denied'. | Question: C++ allow_all() ignores the grant's fsname= and network; keep that or require the fsname to match the daemon's fs (stricter, no current peer affected)? |
| [F-ADM-029](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-029) | Command self-description (get_command_descriptions, help) | contract | The ceph CLI fetches get_command_descriptions before every tell or daemon command and splits arguments by descriptor type; without it no CLI command reaches the MDS. | Descriptors (Ceph descriptor grammar) for every implemented command, plus help. |
| [F-ADM-030](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-030) | Argument validation, output formats, output-file and return-code conventions | subset | Peers parse the JSON output (dashboard and stats expect `session ls` as a bare array, volumes checks rc) and send command maps with extra keys. | json and json-pretty (default) with the C++ JSON shapes (no top-level section names), cmdmap validation (-EINVAL), unknown keys ignored, rc/return_code conventions of the implemented commands; xml/table/html formats and output-file are P2. |
| [F-ADM-031](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-031) | Client-side target resolution for tell (libcephfs mds_command) | contract | The CLI, mgr modules and libcephfs resolve mds.<name>, gid, <fs>:<rank> and * against the FSMap; the MDS only needs correct FSMap entries (addrs, gid, name, rank, state, laggy_since). | No dedicated MDS code; covered by beacons and the FSMap (category CLU). |
| [F-ADM-032](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-032) | status command | subset | Operator essential (D13); qa waits on status.state and docs read recovery progress from it. | cluster_fsid, whoami, id, want_state, state, fs_name, rank_uptime, mdsmap_epoch, osdmap_epoch, osdmap_epoch_barrier, uptime, sysinfo; per-phase recovery status reports per-shard progress instead of C++ journal positions. |
| [F-ADM-035](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-035) | Op tracker dumps (ops, dump_ops_in_flight, blocked and historic ops) | subset | dump_ops_in_flight is an operator essential (D13, documented); blocked and historic dumps back slow-request diagnosis and qa TestValidTell. | dump_ops_in_flight, dump_blocked_ops(_count), dump_historic_ops(_by_duration) with ops[]{description, initiated_at, age, duration, type_data{flag_point, reqid, op_type, events[]}}; `ops --flags=locks` (C++ lock dump) dropped. |
| [F-ADM-037](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-037) | session ls / client ls | subset | The dashboard (session ls to <fscid>:0) and mgr/stats (client ls to rank 0) parse it, and operators use it to find clients to evict (D13). | Bare JSON array; per client session: id, auth_name, entity, inst, state, num_caps, num_leases, request_load_avg, uptime, requests_in_flight, num_completed_requests, num_completed_flushes, reconnecting, client_metadata{client_features{feature_bits}, metric_spec{metric_flags{feature_bits}}, every metadata key verbatim}; same filters as session evict; C++ recall/decay internals, prealloc and delegated inos optional.<br>Risk: mgr/stats indexes client_metadata.hostname, .root and metric_spec.metric_flags.feature_bits without defaults: one session lacking them aborts processing of the whole reply. |
| [F-ADM-038](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-038) | session evict / client evict / session kill and the session filter grammar | contract | Operator essential and peer-critical: mgr/volumes subvolume evict (auth_name= + client_metadata.root=), the dashboard (id=) and the ceph-csi provisioner; eviction blocklists the client, waits for the OSDMap and raises the epoch barrier before killing the session. | Filters id=, bare id, auth_name=, client_metadata.<key>=, state=, reconnecting=; -EAGAIN while replaying; session kill (blocking variant) optional.<br>Risk: A client can hold sessions on several ranks; each rank must evict its own. |
| [F-ADM-040](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-040) | flush journal | contract | Operator essential (D13: flush journal-equivalent), documented maintenance step and the most used qa command; reply {message, return_code} with rc = return_code. | Semantics: make every shard's journal durable and checkpointed (F-STORE-020); no C++ segment format. |
| [F-ADM-050](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-050) | damage ls and damage rm | subset | Damage containment is P0 (D10) and MDS_DAMAGE (HEALTH_ERR) blocks Rook reconciles and fires alerts; operators need damage ls to find damaged metadata and damage rm to clear entries after repair. | damage ls (array of id, damage_type, ino, path or shard where known) and damage rm <id>, per rank; entry format new. |
| [F-ADM-057](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-057) | Generic process commands: perf, counter, config, log, injectargs | subset | ceph-exporter needs counter dump/schema and config show (pid_file); operators need perf dump, config get/set/diff and injectargs (D13); qa uses perf dump heavily. | perf dump [logger [counter]], perf schema, counter dump, counter schema, config show\|get\|set\|unset\|diff\|help, injectargs; perf reset and log flush\|reopen P2; legacy perfcounters_dump/schema, 1, 2 out; assert, abort, leak_some_memory, raise, messenger dump, objecter_requests, perf histogram dropped. |
| [F-ADM-059](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-059) | Version and ping built-ins (0, version, git_version) | contract | ceph-exporter pings every socket with {"prefix":"0"} and expects the 1-byte reply "2" (else ceph_daemon_socket_up 0); version and git_version are trivial and used by qa. |  |
| [F-ADM-060](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-060) | MDS option catalogue and option metadata | subset | The unmodified monitor validates `ceph config set` against its compiled C++ option table, and operators, docs, cephadm and Rook use C++ option names, so options the Rust MDS honours must keep those names and types where the meaning carries over. | Honour mds_cache_memory_limit, mds_join_fs, mds_session_blocklist_on_evict/_on_timeout, session and recall limits, mds_max_snaps_per_dir, mds_op_complaint_time, mds_health_* thresholds, journal trim limits; ignore C++-internal options.<br>Question: New Rust-only options can be stored only with `config set --force` as untyped strings: reuse C++ names, accept --force, or change the mon? |
| [F-ADM-064](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-064) | MDS option names read or written by peers | contract | cephadm and Rook set mds_join_fs (Rook also mds_cache_memory_limit per pod) on every reconcile, cephadm memory autotune subtracts mds_cache_memory_limit, and snap_schedule reads mds_max_snaps_per_dir. | Risk: If mds_cache_memory_limit is not the real memory bound, Rook pods (limit x 0.5) are OOM-killed. |
| [F-ADM-066](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-066) | Unlabeled MDS perf counter loggers | subset | mgr/status, the dashboard, telemetry, ceph-exporter and the shipped Grafana dashboards read specific mds* counter names; the rest are C++ internals. | Loggers mds, mds_mem, mds_server, mds_cache, mds_log, mds_sessions with the peer-read names (summary list) at priority >= 5 with correct type and unit; C++-internal counters (traverse_*, dir_split/merge, replication, scrub_*, oft.*) dropped or left at 0.<br>Question: Map C++-specific names onto the new design (mds.subtrees = shards owned, mds_log.seg = journal segments) or leave them at 0? |
| [F-ADM-067](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-067) | Counter reporting to the mgr and the names mgr modules read | contract | mgr/status and the dashboard read counters from MMgrReport (priority >= mgr_stats_threshold 5, every stats_period); declarations carry path, nick, type, priority and unit. | mds.exported/imported (DEBUGONLY) and mds_log.replay (nonexistent) need not be sent. |
| [F-ADM-069](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-069) | Admin-socket counter dump and schema JSON format | contract | ceph-exporter parses counter dump/schema (type bitmask, metric_type, value_type, priority, units; LONGRUNAVG as {avgcount, sum, avgtime}); operators and qa parse perf dump. |  |
| [F-ADM-070](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-070) | Prometheus export through ceph-exporter | contract | ceph-exporter is deployed by default by cephadm and Rook: it pings, reads counter dump/schema (priority >= 5) and config show (pid_file), and labels series ceph_daemon=mds.<id>, which dashboards join with ceph_mds_metadata. |  |
| [F-ADM-071](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-071) | Memory gauges and gauge refresh cadence | subset | mds_mem.{ino,dn,dir,cap} feed fs status and the dashboard; mds_mem.rss and heap feed the Grafana memory panel. | rss (KiB) and heap (bytes) sampled periodically; ino/dn/dir/cap as counts of cached inodes, dentries, directories and caps refreshed at least every tick; `+`/`-` lifetime counters optional. |
| [F-ADM-072](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-072) | Grafana dashboards and Prometheus alerts shipped with Ceph | subset | cephadm deploys Prometheus and Grafana with these dashboards and alerts by default; they query fixed ceph_mds_* names and health codes. | Keep the names the shipped CephFS dashboard queries (summary list), including objecter op_r/op_w; alerts need only correct health codes; mds_rank_perf and subvolume panels follow F-ADM-068 (P1).<br>Risk: Missing names leave Grafana panels silently empty. |
| [F-ADM-073](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-073) | Health metric pipeline (MDSHealth in beacons to monitor checks) | contract | The unmodified monitor turns MDSHealth metrics carried in beacons into the health checks seen by operators, Rook's gate, cephadm and alerts. | Only ids 1..17, never 0; per-client metrics summarised above mds_health_summarize_threshold; recomputed periodically from current state.<br>Risk: A type-0 metric aborts the decoding monitor; each change of (type, sev, message) costs a mon proposal and an MDSMap epoch pushed to every client, so keep messages stable. |
| [F-ADM-074](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-074) | MDS_TRIM: behind on trimming | redesign | Operator signal that the journal grows faster than it is trimmed (replay time); the mon refuses mds fail/fs fail on a daemon reporting it. | Keep code MDS_TRIM, WARN, 'Behind on trimming (N/M)' and num_segments/max_segments; trigger on per-shard journal backlog. |
| [F-ADM-075](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-075) | Client-behaviour warnings: late cap release, cache-pressure recall, oldest tid | contract | Operators identify clients that do not release caps, ignore cache-pressure recall or do not advance their oldest tid, and evict them; essential when one tenant's client can block others. |  |
| [F-ADM-076](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-076) | MDS_SLOW_REQUEST and slow-request detection | contract | Main operator signal for stuck metadata operations, used together with dump_ops_in_flight; matching 'slow request' cluster-log lines. |  |
| [F-ADM-077](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-077) | MDS_SLOW_METADATA_IO and slow RADOS IO detection | contract | Tells operators the MDS is blocked on RADOS (OSD rather than MDS problem); threshold osd_op_complaint_time. |  |
| [F-ADM-078](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-078) | MDS_DAMAGE and MDS_READ_ONLY state warnings | contract | Damage containment (D10) and read-only mode on write errors must be visible; alerts key on MDS_DAMAGE and Rook stops reconciling while it is raised at HEALTH_ERR. | Question: Shard-contained damage at HEALTH_ERR stops Rook reconciles of every CR in the cluster namespace: keep ERR (C++, alerts) or report per-shard damage at WARN? |
| [F-ADM-079](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-079) | MDS_CACHE_OVERSIZED | contract | Signals cache memory above mds_cache_memory_limit x mds_health_cache_threshold (Rook derives the limit from pod memory); the mon refuses mds fail/fs fail on a daemon reporting it. |  |
| [F-ADM-082](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-082) | Monitor-derived file-system health checks | contract | Computed by the unchanged monitor from the FSMap (FS_DEGRADED, MDS_UP_LESS_THAN_MAX, MDS_ALL_DOWN, FS_WITH_FAILED_MDS, MDS_INSUFFICIENT_STANDBY, damaged-rank MDS_DAMAGE); alerts, Rook and cephadm key on them and they are right only if beacon states are right. | No dedicated MDS code; MULTIMDS_WITH_OLDSNAPS, FS_INLINE_DATA_DEPRECATED and the dead legacy summaries are out. |
| [F-ADM-083](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-083) | Health-gated monitor commands | contract | The mon refuses unconfirmed `fs set max_mds` while any MDS reports any metric, and `mds fail`/`fs fail` while a target reports TRIM or CACHE_OVERSIZED; Rook sends all three unconfirmed and a refusal aborts its MDS upgrade. | Report metrics only for real, current conditions; never a permanent informational metric.<br>Risk: A spurious or sticky metric blocks Rook upgrades and scaling. |
| [F-ADM-084](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-084) | Cluster log (clog) messages | subset | Operators read evictions, session denials, slow requests, write and journal errors and damage in the cluster log; qa ignorelists match the wording. | Evictions ('Evicting (and blocklisting) client session N (addr)'), session open denials, slow requests, late cap release and oldest-tid warnings, write/journal errors, damage, force read-only; flush the log before an abort; clog_to_monitors routing. |
| [F-ADM-087](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-087) | mgr libcephfs connection pool and mount identity | contract | Every mgr module (volumes, nfs, snap_schedule, mirroring, dashboard) mounts as mgr.<id> with uid/gid 0: many concurrent sessions per entity, sessions abandoned with abort_conn, old-mgr addresses blocklisted on failover. | Risk: Abandoned and blocklisted mgr sessions must be reaped (stale, autoclose) without blocking other tenants. |
| [F-ADM-088](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-088) | mgr/volumes: volume lifecycle (fs volume create, rm, rename, ls, info) | contract | `fs volume create/rm/rename/ls/info` is the default way to create file systems; the MDS sees fs new/fail/rm/rename through the FSMap and serves statx(/volumes) (dir size = rbytes) and readdir of /volumes/_deleting. |  |
| [F-ADM-100](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-100) | mgr/volumes: fs subvolume evict | contract | mgr/volumes evicts a tenant's clients from every up rank with filters auth_name= and client_metadata.root= (exact match), retrying on -ETIMEDOUT; part of the deauthorize flow. | Risk: client_metadata.root must be stored verbatim; each up rank must reach up:active or up:clientreplay within about 35 s. |
| [F-ADM-107](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-107) | mgr/status: fs status | contract | `ceph fs status` (always-on status module) reads mds_mem.{dn,ino,dir,cap}, mds_sessions.session_count, the rates of mds_server.handle_client_request and mds_log.replayed, and ceph_version from mds metadata. |  |
| [F-ADM-108](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-108) | mgr/dashboard: CephFS pages | subset | Dashboard CephFS pages read counters, list (session ls) and evict (client evict id=) clients on <fscid>:0, run fs authorize and browse files through libcephfs. | Counters mds_server.handle_client_request, mds_log.ev, mds_cache.num_strays, mds.{inodes,caps,subtrees,exported_inodes,imported_inodes}, mds_mem.ino; client list/evict; file browser and quotas; snapshot pages need P1. |
| [F-ADM-109](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-109) | mgr/prometheus: MDS and file-system metadata metrics | contract | Enabled with the default monitoring stack: ceph_mds_metadata (boot-beacon hostname, ceph_version, public_addr, rank, fs_id), ceph_fs_metadata and ceph_health_detail; perf counters are excluded by default. | No dedicated MDS code beyond boot metadata (CLU) and health codes. |
| [F-ADM-111](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-111) | mgr/mds_autoscaler | contract | Never talks to the MDS: sets the orchestrator daemon count to max_mds + standby_count_wanted from the FSMap, so it works if the FSMap is right and the MDS tolerates daemons added or removed on each max_mds change. | No dedicated MDS code. |
| [F-ADM-112](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-112) | mgr/nfs: CephFS exports | contract | Default NFS flow: validates the export path with statx, checks or sets the earmark, creates the per-export user (allow rw path=); Ganesha then mounts through libcephfs (delegations off, rados_cluster recovery). |  |
| [F-ADM-113](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-113) | cephadm MDS deployment | contract | cephadm runs /usr/bin/ceph-mds -n mds.<id> -f --setuser ceph --setgroup ceph --default-log-* with the keyring in /var/lib/ceph/mds/ceph-<id>, /etc/ceph/ceph.conf, the run dir for the asok, and caps mon 'profile mds', osd 'allow rw tag cephfs *=*', mds 'allow'. | Risk: All MDS RADOS objects must live in pools tagged cephfs (osd cap), and mon config-key access is limited to daemon-private/mds.<id>/. |
| [F-ADM-114](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-114) | cephadm MDS upgrade choreography | contract | Upgrades clear allow_standby_replay, set max_mds 1 (ranks > 0 stop and hand off their shards, D6) or fs fail, wait on mds ok-to-stop, redeploy, check `ceph versions`, then restore max_mds or joinable. | Risk: A ceph_version with fewer than three space-separated fields raises IndexError in the upgrade loop. |
| [F-ADM-115](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-115) | Rook operator: MDS pods, command line, keyring, config and probes | contract | Rook starts ceph-mds via PATH with --fsid, --keyring, --default-log/err/mon-cluster-log flags, --mon-host (bracketed addrvecs), --mon-initial-members, --id=<fs>-<letter>, --setuser/--setgroup, --foreground and --public-addr, an empty mds_data, SIGTERM stop and SIGHUP log reopen; liveness = name present in fs dump. | Risk: The FSMap name must equal --id; stopping must send a DNE beacon (Rook waits only on the FSMap); the image must ship bash, jq and the ceph CLI for the probe. |
| [F-ADM-116](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-116) | Rook operator: upgrade order, ok-to-stop gate and health gating | contract | Rook compares each MDS ceph_version ('ceph version X.Y.Z-B (sha) ...') with the image's `ceph --version`: an older or different-commit string reruns the max_mds 1 upgrade on every reconcile, an unparsable one fails every reconcile; HEALTH_ERR checks other than MDS_ALL_DOWN/AUTH_* stop reconciles. | Question: The Rust MDS must report the exact Ceph version string (same commit id) of the image it ships in: decide packaging and version reporting. |
| [F-ADM-121](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-121) | Client-side tools: cephfs-tool bench and cephfs-shell | contract | cephfs-tool bench and cephfs-shell use only libcephfs and work once the client protocol works; cephfs-shell du reads ceph.dir.rbytes, quotas work, snapshots fail with EPERM until P1. | No dedicated MDS code. |

### Operations: admin commands, counters, health, config, orchestrators: P1 (12)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-BG-027](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-027) | dump stray admin command | subset | Documented troubleshooting command for space that is not reclaimed (pinned strays); no mgr module, cephadm or Rook calls it, so diagnosis can follow the MVP (matches F-ADM-047). | List the rank's pending strays per shard with ino, prior path and cap holders; the C++ JSON quirks ('ino: ' keys) need not be kept. |
| [F-BG-028](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-028) | scrub start command | subset | Online scrub and repair of the new format are P1 (D10); keep the documented command so runbooks (disaster-recovery-experts.rst) and QA keep working. | 'scrub start <path> [recursive,repair,force] [tag]' accepted at least on rank 0 (ceph tell mds.<fs>:0) with reply {return_code, scrub_tag, mode}; scrub_path and scrub_start legacy aliases out; ~mdsdir and scrub_mdsdir see F-BG-035. |
| [F-BG-036](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-036) | Scrub pause, resume and abort | subset | Operator control of P1 scrubs (D10) through the documented commands. | scrub pause\|resume\|abort with the C++ JSON replies ({message, return_code} / {return_code}); cross-rank propagation is free MDS-to-MDS. |
| [F-BG-037](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-037) | Scrub status, summary and mgr task status | subset | scrub status is the operator view of P1 scrubs, and the ServiceMap task status 'scrub status' shows in ceph -s while scrubs run (F-ADM-085). | scrub status JSON {status, scrubs} with the C++ status strings (QA polls 'no active scrubs running'); task-status key 'scrub status' while not idle, empty map when idle; scrub_stats uninline counters out (legacy). |
| [F-ADM-042](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-042) | force_readonly | contract | Operator and qa command; the read-only mode it triggers (FORCE_RO to clients, MDS_READ_ONLY) is P0 through the write-error policy (F-STORE-100), so the command is cheap once that exists. |  |
| [F-ADM-044](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-044) | cache drop | contract | Documented operator action to shrink an MDS cache (recall caps from all clients, flush, trim); no peer default flow uses it. | Risk: C++ asserts when the journal flush fails (read-only rank); report the error instead. |
| [F-ADM-045](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-045) | cache status and dump cache | subset | `cache status` lets operators compare cache memory with mds_cache_memory_limit; `dump cache` dumps C++ objects and stalls the rank. | cache status {pool:{items, bytes}}; dump cache dropped. |
| [F-ADM-047](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-047) | dump stray | subset | Documented troubleshooting for space that is not reclaimed; strays and purge are P0 but listing them is diagnosis only. | Per-shard stray listing (ino, prior path, client caps) in a new format. |
| [F-ADM-048](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-048) | Scrub control commands (scrub start, abort, pause, resume, status, purge_status) | contract | Online scrub and repair are P1 (D10); docs and qa drive them with `ceph tell mds.<fs>:0 scrub start\|status\|abort\|pause\|resume`. | scrub_path and scrub_start remnants are out. |
| [F-ADM-081](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-081) | MDS_ESTIMATED_REPLAY_TIME | redesign | Progress of replays longer than 30 s; per-shard replay should be short, and qa parses the message text. | Keep code and 'replay: P% complete - elapsed time: Es, estimated time remaining: Rs'; compute over the shards being replayed. |
| [F-ADM-085](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-085) | Scrub task status in the mgr service map | contract | Comes with online scrub (D10: P1); `ceph -s` shows 'scrub status' from the mgr service map and qa polls it. |  |
| [F-ADM-110](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-110) | mgr/telemetry: MDS data collection | subset | Opt-in reporting; the basic channel reads counters that are P0 anyway plus rank-0 root rstat gauges, and the perf channel only logs failures of heap stats and dump_mempools. | mds.root_rfiles/root_rbytes/root_rsnaps on rank 0; heap and dump_mempools P2. |

### Operations: admin commands, counters, health, config, orchestrators: P2 (14)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-CAPS-096](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-096) | Lock state dumps and lock-related op events | redesign | Dumps of C++ lock states are internal (D13: internal dumps OUT or P2); ops in flight (P0, ADM) should still say what an op waits for (lock, revoke from which client). | C++ lock-state dumps and event strings are not reproduced; new event strings are free. |
| [F-STORE-107](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-107) | Op tracker journal events | contract | Op-tracker event names help operators but no tool parses them; the dumps themselves are P0 (D13). |  |
| [F-BG-035](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-035) | Scrubbing ~mdsdir / ~mdsN and scrub_mdsdir (stray loading) | subset | doc/cephfs/scrub.rst tells operators to run 'scrub start ~mdsdir recursive' to make the MDS evaluate strays; the new design has no mdsdir. | Accept ~mdsdir/~mdsN and scrub_mdsdir as 'verify and re-evaluate the strays of this rank's shards' so the documented step does not fail. |
| [F-BG-038](../mds-rust-map/features/09-background-services.md#f-bg-038) | Scrub counters and scrub purge_status | redesign | Scrub perf counters are diagnostics that no mgr module or orchestrator reads, and their C++ names are tied to C++ checks. | Per-tag uninline counters, scrub purge_status and mds_scrub_stats_review_period are legacy (out). |
| [F-ADM-033](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-033) | Process control commands: exit, respawn, lockup | subset | Used by qa and the libcephfs reclaim test, not by mgr, cephadm or Rook default flows; supervisors restart the process anyway. | exit and respawn (new process identity); lockup (sleeps holding mds_lock) dropped. |
| [F-ADM-034](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-034) | heap and cpu_profiler commands | tolerate | Only opt-in telemetry (perf_memory_metrics) sends `heap stats` and it only logs failures; tcmalloc and gperftools are C++ process specifics. | Return -EOPNOTSUPP ('not using tcmalloc'), as a C++ build without tcmalloc does. |
| [F-ADM-036](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-036) | op get and op kill | contract | Only qa uses op get/op kill (also as the release of the lock path and quiesce path test ops); no peer sends them. |  |
| [F-ADM-039](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-039) | session config / client config timeout | contract | Admin override of the per-session `timeout` metadata; Ganesha sets the key itself (F-PROTO-046), only qa and operators use the command. |  |
| [F-ADM-041](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-041) | flush_path | subset | qa only (test_scrub_checks); writing back one inode and its dirfrag is a C++ cache concept. | Flush the shard that owns the path; reply {message, return_code}. |
| [F-ADM-043](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-043) | osdmap barrier | contract | Only qa sets the OSD epoch barrier by hand; the barrier mechanism itself is P0 (F-STORE-098). |  |
| [F-ADM-046](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-046) | Cache and table dumps: dump tree, dump inode, dump dir, openfiles ls, dump snaps | subset | Debug and qa dumps of C++ cache, open-file and snap tables (D13: C++-internal dumps OUT or P2); only a by-inode lookup helps operators diagnose damage. | dump inode <ino> (path and attributes, new format); dump tree, dump dir, openfiles ls and dump snaps not implemented.<br>Risk: qa test_volumes reads old_inodes from `dump inode` (C++ snapshot format) and will not run unchanged. |
| [F-ADM-053](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-053) | lock path and quiesce path test hooks | subset | qa test hooks: lock path names C++ lock types (D16) and quiesce path belongs to quiesce (D12: P2). | quiesce path (released by op kill) only if quiesce is built; lock path out. |
| [F-ADM-058](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-058) | dump_mempools | tolerate | Only opt-in telemetry (perf_memory_metrics) reads dump_mempools and it only logs failures; mempools are a C++ allocator concept. | Return {mempool:{by_pool:{}, total:{items, bytes}}} or -EOPNOTSUPP. |
| [F-ADM-080](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-080) | MDS_CLIENTS_LAGGY: evictions deferred because of laggy OSDs | contract | Raised only with defer_client_eviction_on_laggy_osds (default false); follows the optional eviction deferral (F-PROTO-047). |  |

| Entry | Name | Why |
|---|---|---|
| [F-STORE-108](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-108) | Offline journal reset and EResetJournal | cephfs-journal-tool reset and EResetJournal are C++ tool compatibility (D2). |
| [F-STORE-109](../mds-rust-map/features/06-journal-and-storage.md#f-store-109) | cephfs-journal-tool inspection, export/import, header and event commands | C++ offline journal tool (D2). |

<a id="qos-metrics"></a>

## Metrics, QoS, quiesce and quarantine

### Metrics, QoS, quiesce and quarantine: P0 (4)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-096](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-096) | MClientMetrics encoding and client send rules | tolerate | D11: client metrics must be accepted and must never break a session; with MDS bit 14 the kernel sends MClientMetrics once per second and libcephfs every tick to rank 0. | Advertise bit 14 and metric spec 0-15 (16 with P1 subvolume metrics); decode types 0-16, skip unknown types by their ENCODE_START length, drop while not active; aggregation and forwarding to the mgr per F-QOS-011/012.<br>Risk: A metrics decode error must drop the message, never fault the session's connection. |
| [F-QOS-011](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-011) | Client metrics negotiation (feature bit 14 and metric_spec) | subset | Every OPEN to current clients carries supported_features and metric_spec (MClientSession v4+); bit 14 decides whether clients send metrics at all, and mgr/stats reads each client's own metric_spec from client ls without a default. | Advertise bit 14 and a metric_spec of types 0-15 in every OPEN (add 16 with P1 subvolume metrics); store the client's metric_spec and dump it as client_metadata.metric_spec.metric_flags.feature_bits in session ls/client ls. Non-MIMIC (v1) OPEN is legacy (out).<br>Risk: A session lacking metric_spec in client ls raises KeyError in mgr/stats and aborts the rest of that reply. |
| [F-QOS-013](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-013) | Tolerant decoding of unknown metric types | contract | D11: client metrics must never break a session; a decode exception faults the connection and resets the client's session socket (metrics 3.3). | Accept CEPH_MSG_CLIENT_METRICS (type 29) on any rank, in any state, from any session; skip unknown or not-yet-implemented items (type 16 before P1) by struct_len; unlike C++, do not fault on a known type with a too-new struct_compat; never reply.<br>Risk: libcephfs with client_collect_and_send_global_metrics=true sends every type, including 16, regardless of metric_spec once bit 14 is set. |
| [F-QOS-028](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-028) | MDS perf query configuration from ceph-mgr | subset | mgr/stats registers its queries and the mgr pushes them in MMgrConfigure to every MDS; the daemon holding rank 0 must evaluate them (D13 stats, F-ADM-106). | Decode and accept the perf-query part of MMgrConfigure on every rank (MgrClient obligation, F-PROTO-104); evaluating the queries and attaching results to MMgrReport is P1 (F-QOS-029/030).<br>Risk: A MgrClient that rejects MMgrConfigure carrying an MDSConfigPayload could stall perf-counter reporting (status, dashboard, prometheus) as soon as someone runs fs perf stats.<br>Reconciled: Only the decode is needed by default flows; answers serve the opt-in stats module. |

### Metrics, QoS, quiesce and quarantine: P1 (19)

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-QOS-012](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-012) | MClientMetrics ingestion of per-client metrics (types 0..9) | contract | fs perf stats and cephfs-top show the latest per-client values of types 0-9; D13 lists mgr/stats among the P0 mgr modules (matches F-ADM-106). | Types 0-9 in the v2 latency encoding; v1 latency decoding is legacy (out). Store the client's latest values, do not accumulate.<br>Question: D11 only demands tolerance of client metrics; classified P0 by D13. If the team defers fs perf stats data, F-QOS-012/014/018/020/021/028/029/030 drop to P1, while F-QOS-011's client ls fields and F-QOS-013 stay P0.<br>Reconciled: mgr `stats` is not an always-on module (src/mon/MgrMonitor.cc:80-106 at the map commit); `fs perf stats` and cephfs-top are opt-in observability. P0 keeps the tolerance (F-QOS-013, F-PROTO-096) and the client ls fields (F-QOS-011). |
| [F-QOS-014](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-014) | Metrics accepted on any active rank (client send rules) | redesign | Kernel metrics can arrive at any rank (last OPEN with bit 14) and libcephfs metrics only at rank 0; with multi-rank P0 (D6) the aggregator must merge them per client without the C++ zero-flapping from ranks that hold a session but get no metrics. | Risk: mgr/stats enriches only clients listed by rank 0's client ls; keep every client with a rank-0 session (e.g. root shard on rank 0) or they vanish from fs perf stats.<br>Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012: per-client metric aggregation serves only the opt-in stats module. |
| [F-QOS-015](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-015) | Subvolume id in InodeStat and cap flush acks | contract | subvolume_id in InodeStat v9 and cap flush acks only tags client I/O for subvolume metrics (P1, D11); with D3 it is the shard root ino. | P0 may send 0 (the kernel then creates no subvolume records) or the real id; filling it from day one is free.<br>Risk: A non-zero id must never change for an inode: the kernel WARN_ON_ONCEs on X->Y and ignores X->0.<br>Question: Reconcile with F-SNAP-054 (P0, real id) and F-PROTO-025 (fill it with P1). If admins may mark arbitrary directories as shard roots (D3), keep subvolume_id = outermost ceph.dir.subvolume root (nested subvolumes refused), not the innermost shard. |
| [F-QOS-016](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-016) | Subvolume I/O metrics (type 16) ingestion and path resolution | redesign | Per-subvolume I/O metrics are P1 (D11); the owning rank knows its shard root paths, but kernel samples can land on any rank and need ino lookup or forwarding to the owner (D6). | C++ silently drops samples whose subvolume root is not cached on the receiving rank. |
| [F-QOS-017](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-017) | Feature bit 22 enables kernel subvolume metrics | subset | Bit 22 in the MDS bitmap only switches the kernel's subvolume metrics on (libcephfs never tests the MDS's bit 22), so it belongs with P1 subvolume metrics (D15). | P0: do not advertise bit 22; the kernel then clears its subvolume tracker and still sends types 0-9. P1: advertise it once type-16 items are aggregated. The client's own bit 22 (CHARMAP for libcephfs, SUBVOLUME_METRICS for the kernel) is F-PROTO-025's concern.<br>Risk: All ranks must advertise identical bits: the last OPEN from any rank flips the kernel's tracker, and while enabled it skips sessions without bit 22 as metrics targets. |
| [F-QOS-018](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-018) | Per-rank metrics forwarding to rank 0 (MMDSMetrics) | redesign | With multi-rank P0 (D6), every rank must forward its clients' latest metrics to the rank that answers the mgr (rank 0); MMDSMetrics is MDS-to-MDS and free to redesign. | Subvolume aggregates and rank telemetry in the message come with P1 (F-QOS-016, F-QOS-022).<br>Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012. |
| [F-QOS-019](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-019) | Rank-0 ping sequence and delayed-rank detection | redesign | The mgr shows ranks with stale metrics as delayed_ranks; the ping and sequence protocol behind it is free. | P0 may report an empty rank_metrics_delayed set. |
| [F-QOS-020](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-020) | Client session lifecycle in metrics (REFRESH and REMOVE) | redesign | Per-client metric state must appear and disappear with sessions so closed clients leave fs perf stats (QA asserts counters vanish on disconnect); C++ can lose REMOVE updates. | Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012. |
| [F-QOS-021](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-021) | Metrics state culling on mdsmap changes | redesign | With multi-rank P0 (D6), the aggregator must drop state of ranks that left and survive a rank-0 change, without the C++ labeled-counter leak. | Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012. |
| [F-QOS-022](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-022) | Rank telemetry: CPU usage and open requests (mds_rank_perf) | contract | ceph_mds_rank_perf_cpu_usage/open_requests feed panels of the shipped Grafana CephFS dashboard through ceph-exporter (config-perf-counters 3.8); cheap per rank (matches F-ADM-068). | Question: Pull into P0 with the Grafana dashboard, as F-ADM-068 asks? |
| [F-QOS-023](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-023) | Subvolume quota_bytes and used_bytes reporting | redesign | quota_bytes/used_bytes per subvolume are part of P1 subvolume metrics (D11); the owning rank holds the shard root's exact quota and rbytes, removing C++'s last-broadcast-wins and stale-replica values. |  |
| [F-QOS-024](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-024) | Per-client labeled metric counters (mds_client_metrics) | contract | mds_client_metrics labeled counters are documented (metrics.rst) and exported by ceph-exporter; no mgr module needs them (matches F-ADM-068). | Keep set, label and counter names; fix the C++ value quirks (stuck latencies, opened_inodes = total_inodes). |
| [F-QOS-025](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-025) | Subvolume sliding-window metrics (mds_subvolume_metrics) | contract | mds_subvolume_metrics (8 counters, labels fs_name/subvolume_path) feeds the shipped Grafana subvolume dashboard and is QA-asserted; per-subvolume metrics are P1 (D11). | Window mechanics free; fix the n/(n-1) rate inflation. |
| [F-QOS-026](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-026) | Eviction of idle subvolume metrics | contract | Subvolume counter sets must disappear after a window without samples (documented, QA-asserted); comes with subvolume metrics (P1, D11). |  |
| [F-QOS-027](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-027) | Labeled metric counter naming and export | contract | ceph-exporter derives Prometheus series names from labeled set names and labels, so dashboards depend on them; comes with the labeled counters (P1). |  |
| [F-QOS-029](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-029) | MDS perf query key construction and counter pairs | subset | fs perf stats and cephfs-top need exact query echo, regex-capture keys and 16-byte counter pairs for the per-rank client and global client queries (D13, F-ADM-106). | MDS_RANK and CLIENT_ID sub-keys (ECMAScript regex capture groups over the rank and 'client.<gid> <addr>') with counters 0-15 and the C++ pair semantics; SUBVOLUME_PATH keys and counters 16-23 come with P1 subvolume metrics.<br>Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012. |
| [F-QOS-030](../mds-rust-map/features/10-qos-metrics-quiesce-quarantine.md#f-qos-030) | MDS perf report to ceph-mgr (MMgrReport) | contract | The rank-0 daemon attaches the query results to every MMgrReport (MDSMetricPayload) for fs perf stats (D13, F-ADM-106). | Risk: Never send a metric report type other than 0 or 1 (the mgr aborts on UnknownMetricPayload); report all current keys every period because the mgr drains on read.<br>Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012. Invariant kept for P1: never send a metric report type other than 0 or 1. |
| [F-ADM-068](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-068) | Labeled perf counter families (client, subvolume and rank metrics) | contract | Client and subvolume labeled families come with metrics aggregation (D11: subvolume metrics P1); mds_rank_perf (cpu_usage, open_requests) feeds the shipped Grafana CephFS dashboard. | Question: Pull mds_rank_perf into P0 for the Grafana CephFS dashboard (cheap, per rank)? |
| [F-ADM-106](../mds-rust-map/features/11-security-admin-and-observability.md#f-adm-106) | mgr/stats: fs perf stats (and cephfs-top) | subset | D13 lists stats: `fs perf stats` and cephfs-top need the client ls fields and rank 0 answering mgr perf queries for per-rank client keys and the global client counters 0-15. | client ls schema; rank-0 answers to the per-rank and global client queries; the subvolume query (counters 16-21) is P1 (D11).<br>Risk: Never send a metric report type other than 0/1 (the mgr aborts); clients without a session on rank 0 are missing from the output, so mounts should reach rank 0 (e.g. root shard on rank 0).<br>Question: D11 says client metrics 'tolerate at least' while D13 makes the stats flow P0: confirm rank-0 aggregation of global client metrics (F-QOS-028..030) is P0.<br>Reconciled: Same as F-QOS-012: the stats module must be enabled explicitly. |

| Entry | Name | Mode | Why | Scope, risk and open question |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [F-PROTO-097](../mds-rust-map/features/02-client-sessions-and-protocol.md#f-proto-097) | MQuarantineDisable encoding | contract | Quarantine is P2 (D12) and only libcephfs (bit 24) would receive this message; no kernel advertises the bit. | Risk: If built, do not count it in push_seq: libcephfs does not, and C++ counting stalls its REQUEST_CLOSE. |
| [F-CAPS-081](../mds-rust-map/features/03-caps-leases-and-locking.md#f-caps-081) | Implicit quiescelock wrlock | redesign | Lock side of quiesce (F-QOS-045), which is P2 (D12); the barrier would be built into the new engine differently. |  |
